## X A Policeman’s Business / 十 警察的本行

===Original===
There was living at Somers Town at that time a little man named Jakobs.
He was a man of some character, albeit an unfortunate person with “something behind him.” The something behind him, however, had come short of a lagging. “Carpets” (three months’ hard labour) almost innumerable had fallen to his share, but a lagging had never come his way.
A little wizened-faced man, with sharp black eyes, very alert in his manner, very neatly dressed, he conveyed the impression that he was enjoying a day off, but so far as honest work was concerned Jakobs’ day was an everlasting one.
Mr. Jakobs had been a pensioner of Colonel Black’s for some years. During that period of time Willie Jakobs had lived the life of a gentleman. That is to say, he lived in the manner which he thought conformed more readily to the ideal than that which was generally accepted by the wealthier classes.
There were moments when he lived like a lord﻿—again he had his own standard﻿—but these periods occurred at rare intervals, because Willie was naturally abstemious. But he certainly lived like a gentleman, as all Somers Town agreed, for he went to bed at whatsoever hour he chose, arose with such larks as were abroad at the moment, or stayed in bed reading his favourite journal.
A fortunate man was he, never short of a copper for a half-pint of ale, thought no more of spending a shilling on a race than would you or I, was even suspected of taking his breakfast in bed, a veritable hallmark of luxury and affluence by all standards.

===Chinese===
那时候，萨默斯镇（Somers Town）住着个矮个子，名叫雅各布斯（Jakobs）。
此人颇有些来历，虽说是个「有点案底」的不幸人物。不过他的案底，还够不上一次「长判」。「毯子」（黑话：三个月劳役）他挨过数不清多少回，长判却从来没轮到过他。
他一脸干瘪的小个子，一双锐利的黑眼睛，举止麻利，穿得干净利落，给人的印象仿佛正在歇班享清闲；可要论正经活计，雅各布斯的班是永远歇不完的。
雅各布斯先生（Mr. Jakobs）当布莱克上校（Colonel Black）的年金食客已有好些年。在那段岁月里，威利·雅各布斯（Willie Jakobs）过的是绅士日子。也就是说，他按自己心中更合理想的那一套过活，而不理会阔人阶层普遍认可的那一套。
他也有过着得像勋爵一般的时候——他的标准向来自成一套——只是这种时期难得一见，因为威利天生节制。但他确确实实过着绅士生活，这一点萨默斯镇全镇公认，因为他爱几点睡就几点睡，清早跟着当时在外头欢唱的云雀一同起身，或者干脆赖在床上读他心爱的报纸。
他是个有福气的人，喝半品脱啤酒的铜子儿从不短少，在赛马上押一先令同你我一样不当回事，甚至有人疑心他连早饭都在床上吃——无论按什么标准，这都是奢侈与富足的真凭实据。

===Original===
To him every Saturday morning came postal orders to the value of two pounds sterling from a benefactor who asked no more than that the recipient should be happy and forget that he ever saw a respected dealer in stocks and shares in the act of rifling a dead man’s pockets.
For this William Jakobs had seen.
Willie was a thief, born so, and not without pride in his skilful-fingered ancestry. He had joined the firm of Black and Company less with the object of qualifying for a pension twenty years hence than on the off chance of obtaining an immediate dividend.
He was guarded by the very principles which animated the head of his firm.

===Chinese===
每逢星期六早晨，都有一笔两英镑的邮政汇票寄到他手上，恩主别无所求，只求受惠人过得快活，忘掉自己曾亲眼看见一位受人尊敬的证券经纪商在掏一个死人的口袋。
因为这一幕，威廉·雅各布斯（William Jakobs）正是亲眼所见。
威利是个贼，天生的贼，而且对他那手指灵巧的祖上传下来的营生颇感自豪。他加入布莱克公司这一号，与其说是为了二十年后挣一份养老金，不如说是碰运气求一笔立竿见影的红利。
护着他这条命的，恰恰是激励着公司首脑的那同一套原则。

===Original===
There was an obnoxious member of the board﻿—obnoxious to the genial Colonel Black﻿—who had died suddenly. A subsequent inquisition came to the conclusion that he died from syncope: even Willie knew no better. He had stolen quietly into the managing director’s office one day in the ordinary course of business, for Master Jakobs stole quietly, but literally and figuratively. He was in search of unconsidered stamps and such loose coinage as might be found in the office of a man notoriously careless in the matter of small change. He had expected to find the room empty, and was momentarily paralysed to see the great Black himself bending over the recumbent figure of a man, busily searching the pockets of a dead man for a letter﻿—for the silent man on the floor had come with his resignation in his pocket and had indiscreetly embodied in this letter his reasons for taking the step. Greatest indiscretion of all, he had revealed the existence of this very compromising document to Colonel Black.
Willie Jakobs knew nothing about the letter﻿—had no subtle explanation for the disordered pocketbook. To his primitive mind Colonel Black was making a search for money: it was, in fact, a stamp-hunt on a large scale, and in his agitation he blurted this belief.
At the subsequent inquest Mr. Jakobs did not give evidence. Officially he knew nothing concerning the matter. Instead he retired to his home in Somers Town, a life pensioner subject to a continuation of his reticence. Two years later, one Christmas morning, Mr. Jakobs received a very beautiful box of chocolates by post, “with every good wish,” from somebody who did not trouble to send his or her name. Mr. Jakobs, being no lover of chocolate drops, wondered what it had cost and wished the kindly donor had sent beer.

===Chinese===
董事会里有个讨人嫌的成员——讨那位和蔼可亲的布莱克上校的嫌——突然死了。事后的调查得出结论：他死于昏厥。连威利也不晓得另有内情。某日，雅各布斯小师傅照例「办公」，悄没声地溜进了常务董事的办公室——雅各布斯小师傅偷东西向来悄没声，这话无论字面还是比喻都作数。他要找的是没人惦记的邮票，以及一位在零钱上出了名马虎的先生办公室里可能散放的硬币。他原以为屋里没人，一看之下顿时僵住：布莱克大人本人正俯身对着一个躺倒的人，忙着搜死者的口袋找一封信——因为地上那具一声不吭的躯体来时口袋里就揣着辞呈，还不知轻重地把自己走这一步的缘由写进了信里。而最不知轻重的，是他竟把这封极易惹祸的文件的存在，透露给了布莱克上校。
威利·雅各布斯对那封信一无所知——对那本翻乱了的皮夹子也讲不出什么微妙的解释。在他那颗朴素的头脑看来，布莱克上校是在找钱：说实在的，那不过是一场大规模的邮票搜寻，他一慌，就把这想法脱口说了出来。
后来的死因研讯里，雅各布斯先生没有出庭作证。官面上，他对此事一无所知。他退回萨默斯镇的家中，当上了终身年金客，条件是把沉默继续下去。两年后的一个圣诞早晨，雅各布斯先生收到邮局送来的一盒十分漂亮的巧克力，「谨致美好祝愿」，寄件人却懒得署名。雅各布斯先生本不爱吃巧克力，心下只琢磨这盒子花了多少钱，但盼这好心人寄的原是啤酒。

===Original===
“Hi, Spot, catch!” said Mr. Jakobs, and tossed a specimen of the confectioner’s art to his dog, who possessed a sweet tooth.
The dog ate it, wagging his tail, then he stopped wagging his tail and lay down with a shiver﻿—dead.
It was some time before Willie Jakobs realized the connection between the stiff little dog and this bland and ornate Christmas gift.
He tried a chocolate on his landlord’s dog, and it died. He experimented on a fellow-lodger’s canary, and it died too﻿—he might have destroyed the whole of Somers Town’s domestic menagerie but for the timely intervention of his landlord, who gave him in charge for his initial murder. Then the truth came out. The chocolates were poisoned. Willie Jakobs found his photograph in the public Press as the hero of a poisoning mystery: an embarrassment for Willie, who was promptly recognized by a Canning Town tradesman he had once victimized, and was arrested for the second time in a week.

===Chinese===
「嗨，斑点，接着！」雅各布斯先生说着，把一块糖果铺的艺术品抛给了他的狗——那狗是好吃甜食的。
狗接住吃了，摇着尾巴，随即尾巴不摇了，打个哆嗦躺倒——死了。
过了好一阵子，威利·雅各布斯才把那只僵直的小狗同这份温文体面、装潢华丽的圣诞礼物联系起来。
他拿一块巧克力试了房东的狗，狗死了。他又拿同屋房客的金丝雀做实验，金丝雀也死了——若非房东及时出面干预，以首起谋杀的罪名把他扭送了官，他险些要把萨默斯镇全城的家养动物园消灭个干净。真相这才大白：巧克力有毒。威利·雅各布斯发现自己的照片登上了各报，成了投毒疑案的主角——这对威利可是桩尴尬事：一个曾遭他坑害的坎宁镇（Canning Town）商人一眼认出了他，于是一星期之内他第二次被捕。

===Original===
Willie came out of gaol (it was a “carpet”) expecting to find an accumulation of one-pound postal orders awaiting him. Instead he found one five-pound note and a typewritten letter, on perfectly plain uncompromising paper, to the effect that the sender regretted that further supplies need not be expected.
Willie wrote to Colonel Black, and received in reply a letter in which “Colonel Black could not grasp the contents of yours of the 4th. He has never sent money, and fails to understand why the writer should have expected,” etc., etc.
Willie, furious and hurt at the base ingratitude and duplicity of his patron, carried the letter and a story to a solicitor, and the solicitor said one word﻿—“Blackmail!” Here, then, was a disgruntled Willie Jakobs forced to work: to depend upon chance bookings and precarious liftings. Fortunately his right hand had not lost its cunning, nor, for the matter of that, had his left. He “clicked” to good stuff, fenced it with the new man in Eveswell Road (he was lagged eventually because he was only an amateur and gave too much for the stuff), and did well﻿—so well, indeed, that he was inclined to take a mild view of Black’s offences.

===Chinese===
威利出狱（那是一回「毯子」）时，指望等着他的是积攒下来的一沓一镑邮政汇票。结果他只见到一张五镑纸币和一封打字机打出的信，用的纸素净得不留情面，大意是：寄件人很遗憾，今后不必再指望有什么供应了。
威利写信给布莱克上校，收到的回信里说：「布莱克上校不明白您四日来函的内容。他从未寄过钱，也不明白写信人凭什么指望」，如此等等。
威利对恩主如此卑劣的忘恩负义与两面三刀又怒又痛，揣着那封信和一肚子故事去找了一位事务律师，律师只说了一个词——「讹诈！」于是这位满怀怨愤的威利·雅各布斯只得重新做工：指望运气上门，靠些朝不保夕的进项过活。所幸他的右手不曾荒疏，而且就此而言，他的左手也不曾。他「顺」到了好货，转手销给伊夫斯韦尔路（Eveswell Road）的新销赃人（那人后来被判了长刑，因为他到底只是个外行，收货出价太高），日子混得不坏——好到这个地步，以至于他倒倾向于对布莱克的罪行从轻发落了。

===Original===
On the evening of Lord Verlond’s dinner party﻿—though, to do him justice, it must be confessed that Jakobs knew nothing of his lordship’s plans﻿—he sallied forth on business intent.
He made his way through the tiny court and narrow streets which separated him from Stibbington Street, there turning southwards to the Euston Road, and taking matters leisurely, he made his way to Tottenham Court Road, en route to Oxford Street.
Tottenham Court Road, on that particular night, was filled with interested people.
They were interested in shop windows, interested in one another, interested in boarding and alighting from buses. It was an ideal crowd from Jakobs’ point of view.
He liked people who concentrated, who fixed their minds on one thing and had no thought for any other. In a sense he was something of a psychologist, and he looked around to find some opulent person whose powers of concentration might be of service to himself.

===Chinese===
弗隆德勋爵（Lord Verlond）设宴那晚——不过说句公道话，得承认雅各布斯对勋爵大人的盘算一无所知——他雄赳赳地出门办事去了。
他穿过小院和窄巷，来到斯蒂宾顿街（Stibbington Street），在那儿折向南往尤斯顿路（Euston Road）去，一路不慌不忙，走上托特纳姆宫路（Tottenham Court Road），取道往牛津街（Oxford Street）去。
那天夜里，托特纳姆宫路上挤满了兴致勃勃的人。
他们对橱窗感兴趣，对彼此感兴趣，对上公共马车下车也感兴趣。在雅各布斯看来，这是一群理想的人众。
他喜欢专心的人，喜欢那些心思钉在一件事上、旁的一概不想的人。从某种意义上说，他也算得上半个心理学家，于是他四下张望，要找一位财力雄厚的人物，其专心致志的本领正好可以为他所用。

===Original===
Gathered round the steps of an omnibus, impatiently waiting for other passengers to disembark, was a little crowd of people, and Jakobs, with his quick, keen eye, spotted a likely client.
He was a stout man of middle age. His hat was placed at such an angle on his head that the Somers Towner diagnosed him as “canned.” He may or may not have been right in his surmise. It is sufficient that he appeared comfortably off, and that not only was his coat of good material, but he had various indications of an ostentatious character testifying to his present affluence.
Willie Jakobs had had no intention of taking a bus ride. I doubt very much whether he changed his plans even now, but certain it is that he began to elbow his way into the little throng which surrounded the bus, by this time surging forward to board it.
He elbowed his way with good effect, for suddenly ceasing his efforts, as though he had remembered some very important engagement, he began to back out. He reached the outskirts of the little knot, then turned to walk briskly away.
At that moment a firm hand dropped on his shoulder in quite a friendly way. He looked round quickly. A tall young man in civilian dress stood behind him.

===Chinese===
一小群人围在公共马车的踏梯旁，不耐烦地等着车上的乘客下来。雅各布斯眼疾手快，一眼相中了一位可能的客户。
那是个中年胖子。帽子在头上歪成那么个角度，叫萨默斯镇人一搭眼就诊断出他「喝高了」。这猜测对不对且不论。要紧的是此人看起来手头宽裕，不仅外套料子上好，身上还有种种炫富的迹象，证明他眼下境况阔绰。
威利·雅各布斯本没打算坐一趟公共马车。我很怀疑他此刻是不是改了主意，但可以肯定的是，他开始朝围着马车的那一小群人里挤——那群人这时正涌上前去上车。
他挤得卓有成效，因为忽然之间他收了劲，仿佛想起一桩顶要紧的约会，开始往外退。他退到那一小撮人的边上，随即转身快步走开。
就在此时，一只坚定的手以十分友好的姿态落在了他肩上。他飞快回头。一个穿便装的高个子青年站在他身后。

===Original===
“Hullo!” said the young man, kindly enough, “aren’t you going on?”
“No, Mr. Fellowe,” he said. “I was going down for a blow, but I remember I left the gas burning at home.”
“Let’s go back and put it out,” said Constable Fellowe, who was on a very special duty that night.
“On second thoughts,” said Jakobs reflectively, “I don’t think it’s worth while. After all, it’s one of those penny-in-the-slot machines and it can only burn itself out.”
“Then come along and see if my gas is burning,” said Frank humorously.

===Chinese===
「喂！」青年人说话相当和气，「不上去啦？」
「不去了，费洛先生（Mr. Fellowe），」他说，「我本想下去透口气，可想起来家里煤气还点着呢。」
「那咱们回去把它吹灭吧。」费洛警员说。他那晚负的是一项极特殊的差事。
「再一想，」雅各布斯若有所思地说，「我看犯不上。说到底，那是投币式的煤气表，烧来烧去只能把它自己烧光。」
「那就劳驾来看看我家的煤气点着没有。」弗兰克（Frank）打趣道。

===Original===
He held the other’s arm lightly, but when Jakobs attempted to disengage himself he found the pressure on his arm increased.
“What’s the game?” he asked innocently.
“The same old game,” said Frank, with a little smile. “Hullo. Willie, you’ve dropped something.”
He stooped quickly, without releasing his hold, and picked up a pocketbook.
The bus was on the point of moving off as Frank swung round and with a signal stopped the conductor.

===Chinese===
他轻轻挽住对方的手臂，可等雅各布斯想要抽身时，却发现臂上的力道加重了。
「这是演的哪一出？」他无辜地问。
「还是老一出。」弗兰克微微笑道。「喂，威利，你掉了东西。」
他不松手，迅速俯身，捡起一本皮夹。
马车正要开动，弗兰克猛一转身，一个手势叫住了售票员。

===Original===
“I think someone who has just boarded your bus has lost a pocketbook. I think it is that stoutish gentleman who has just gone inside.”
The stoutish gentleman hastily descended to make a public examination of his wardrobe. He discovered himself minus several articles which should, by all laws affecting the right of property, have been upon his person.
Thereafter the matter became a fairly commonplace incident.
“It’s a cop,” said Willie philosophically. “I didn’t see you around, Mr. Fellowe.”
“I don’t suppose you did, yet I’m big enough.”

===Chinese===
「我想刚上您车的人里有一位丢了皮夹。我想是那位刚进车厢的胖先生。」
那位微胖的先生急急下车，当众清点起自己的行头。他发现自己短了好几样东西，而按照一切有关财产权的法律，它们本该长在他身上。
此后这事就成了一桩相当平淡无奇的案子。
「栽了。」威利达观地说。「我没瞧见您在附近啊，费洛先生。」
「谅你也瞧不见，我块头不是够大么。」

===Original===
“And ugly enough,” added Willie impartially.
Frank smiled.
“You’re not much of an authority on beauty, Willie, are you?” he asked jocosely, as they threaded their way through the streets which separated them from the nearest police-station.
“Oh, I don’t know,” said Willie, “ ’andsome is as ’andsome does. Say, Mr. Fellowe, why don’t the police go after a man like Olloroff? What are they worrying about a little hook like me for﻿—getting my living at great inconvenience, in a manner of speaking. He is a fellow who makes his thousands, and has ruined his hundreds. Can you get him a lagging?”
“In time I hope we shall,” said Frank.

===Chinese===
「也够丑的。」威利不偏不倚地补上一句。
弗兰克笑了笑。
「论美丑你可不是什么权威，威利，对吧？」两人穿行在通往最近警局的街上，他这么打趣道。
「嗷，那倒未必，」威利说，「『心俊才算真的俊』嘛。哎，费洛先生，警察干嘛不去追奥洛罗夫（Olloroff）那样的人物？干嘛老跟我这么个小贼过不去——打个比方吧，我谋这份生计有多碍难。那家伙一赚几千镑，坑垮几百号人。你们能给他弄个长判不？」
「到了时候，我希望我们能。」弗兰克说。

===Original===
“There’s a feller!” said Willie. “He baits the poor little clerk﻿—gets him to put up a fiver to buy a million pounds’ worth of gold mines. Clerk puts it﻿—pinches the money from the till, not meanin’ to be dishonest, in a manner of speakin’, but expectin’ one day to walk into his boss, covered with fame and diamonds, and say, ‘Look at your long-lost Horace!’ See what I mean?”
Frank nodded.
“ ‘Look at your prodigal cashier,’ ” Jakobs continued, carried away by his imagination. “ ‘Put your lamps over my shiners, run your hooks over me Astrakhan collar. Master, it is I, thy servant!’ ”
It was not curious that they should speak of Black. There had been a case in court that day in which a too-credulous client of Black’s, who had suffered as a result of that credulity, had sued the colonel for the return of his money, and the case had not been defended.
“I used to work for him,” said Mr. Jakobs, reminiscently. “Messenger at twenty-nine shillings a week﻿—like bein’ messenger at a mortuary.”

===Chinese===
「那才叫人物呢！」威利说。「他钓那些可怜的小职员——哄他们掏五镑，合伙买价值一百万镑的金矿。职员掏了——从钱柜里摸钱，打个比方，倒不是存心不老实，只指望有朝一日满身名气满身钻石地走进老板跟前，说：『瞧瞧你们失散多年的霍勒斯（Horace）！』明白我的意思吗？」
弗兰克点头。
「『瞧瞧你们浪子回头的出纳员，』」雅各布斯越说越入戏，「『把灯凑过来照照我这亮家伙，伸爪子摸摸我这阿斯特拉罕羔皮领子。东家，是我呀，您的仆人！』」
两人说起布莱克并不奇怪。那天法庭上刚审过一桩案子：布莱克的一个客户过于轻信，为此吃了亏，起诉上校要求退钱，上校方面根本无人出庭应诉。
「我从前给他当过差，」雅各布斯先生怀旧地说。「信使，一礼拜二十九先令——跟在停尸房当信使一个滋味。」

===Original===
He looked up at Frank.
“Ever count up the number of Black’s friends who’ve died suddenly?” he asked. “Ever reckon that up? He’s a regular jujube tree, he is.”
“ ‘Upas’ is the word you want, Willie,” said Frank gently.
“You wait till the Four get him,” warned Mr. Jakobs cheerfully. “They won’t half put his light out.”
He said no more for a while, then he turned suddenly to Frank.

===Chinese===
他抬头看弗兰克。
「你数没数过布莱克的朋友里头有多少个暴死的？」他问。「算过这笔账没有？他简直是棵枣树，没跑。」
「你要找的词是『毒树』，威利。」弗兰克温和地说。
「你等着四义士来收拾他吧，」雅各布斯先生乐乐呵呵地提出警告。「到时候非把他的灯吹灭不可。」
他有一会儿没再言语，随后忽然转向弗兰克。

===Original===
“Come to think of it, Fellowe,” he said, with the gross familiarity of the habitué in dealing with his captor, “this is the third time you’ve pinched me.”
“Come to think of it,” admitted Frank cheerfully, “it is.”
“Harf a mo’.”
Mr. Jakobs halted and surveyed the other with a puzzled air.
“He took me in the Tottenham Court Road, he took me in the Charin’ Cross Road, an’ he apperryhended me in Cheapside.”

===Chinese===
「细细一想，费洛，」他说，带着老油子对付抓他那人的亲昵劲儿，「这是你第三回逮我了。」
「细细一想，」弗兰克痛痛快快地承认，「可不。」
「且慢。」
雅各布斯先生站住，一脸纳闷地打量对方。
「他在托特纳姆宫路逮的我，在查令十字路逮的我，又在奇普赛德（Cheapside）『缉拿』了我。」

===Original===
“You’ve a wonderful memory,” smiled the young man.
“Never on his beat,” said Mr. Jakobs to himself, “always in plain clothes, an’ generally watchin’ me﻿—now, why?”
Frank thought a moment.
“Come and have a cup of tea, Willie,” he said, “and I will tell you a fairy story.”
“I think we shall be gettin’ at facts very soon,” said Willie, in his best judicial manner.

===Chinese===
「你记性真好。」青年人笑道。
「从不在他巡的地段，」雅各布斯先生自言自语，「回回穿便衣，多半都在盯着我——这是为啥呢？」
弗兰克想了想。
「来喝杯茶吧，威利，」他说，「我给你讲个童话。」
「我看咱们很快就要摸着事实了。」威利拿出他最法官的腔调说。

===Original===
“I am going to be perfectly frank with you, my friend,” said Fellowe, when they were seated in a neighbouring coffee-shop.
“If you don’t mind,” begged Willie, “I’d rather call you by your surname﻿—I don’t want it to get about that I’m a pal of yours.”
Frank smiled again. Willie had ever been a source of amusement.
“You have been taken by me three times,” he said, “and this is the first time you have mentioned our friend Black. I think I can say that if you had mentioned him before it might have made a lot of difference to you, Willie.”

===Chinese===
「我要跟你彻底开诚布公，我的朋友。」两人在近旁一家咖啡店落座之后，费洛说道。
「您别见怪，」威利央求道，「我还是称您姓氏的好——我可不想传出去说我是您哥儿们。」
弗兰克又笑了。威利向来是个逗乐的宝库。
「你让我逮过三回，」他说，「这回你才头一次提起咱们那位朋友布莱克。我想我可以说，威利，你若是早些提起他，你的处境很可能就大不一样了。」

===Original===
Mr. Jakobs addressed the ceiling.
“Come to think of it,” he said, “he ’inted at this once before.”
“I ’int at it once again,” said Frank. “Will you tell me why Black pays you two pounds a week?”
“Because he don’t,” said Willie promptly. “Because he’s a sneakin’ hook an’ because he’s a twister, because he’s a liar﻿—”
“If there’s any reason you haven’t mentioned, give it a run,” said Constable Fellowe in the vernacular.

===Chinese===
雅各布斯先生对着天花板发言。
「细细一想，」他说，「他从前点过一回这个。」
「我这回再点一回。」弗兰克说。「你愿不愿意告诉我，布莱克干吗每礼拜给你两镑？」
「因为他根本没给。」威利应声答道。「因为他是个鬼鬼祟祟的贼，因为他是个黑心骗子，因为他是个撒谎精——」
「你要还有没说到的理由，也一并道来。」费洛警员用行话说道。

===Original===
Willie hesitated.
“What’s the good of my tellin’ you?” he asked. “Sure as death you’ll tell me I’m only lyin’.”
“Try me,” said Frank, and for an hour they sat talking, policeman and thief.
At the end of that time they went different ways﻿—Frank to the police-station, where he found an irate owner of property awaiting him, and Mr. Jakobs, thankfully, yet apprehensively, to his Somers Town home.

===Chinese===
威利犹豫了。
「我说了顶什么用？」他问。「你准会说我净瞎扯。」
「试试看。」弗兰克说。于是警察与贼对坐，一谈就是一个钟头。
一个钟头后两人分道扬镳——弗兰克回警局，一位怒气冲冲的失主正等着他；雅各布斯先生则怀着感激而又忐忑的心情，回萨默斯镇的家去。

===Original===
His business completed at the station, and a station sergeant alternately annoyed and mystified by the erratic behaviour of a plainclothes constable, who gave orders with the assurance of an Assistant-Commissioner, Frank found a taxi and drove first to the house of Black, and later (with instructions to the driver to break all the rules laid down for the regulation of traffic) to Hampstead.
May Sandford was expecting the colonel. She stood by the drawing-room fire, buttoning her glove and endeavouring to disguise her pleasure that her sometime friend had called.
“Where are you going?” was his first blunt greeting, and the girl stiffened.
“You have no right to ask in that tone,” she said quietly, “but I will tell you. I am going to dinner.”

===Chinese===
他在局里办完公事，撇下一位又恼又惑的巡佐——那便衣警员发号施令，口气笃定得像位助理总监——随后弗兰克寻了辆出租车，先开到布莱克的宅子，后又（嘱咐司机把交通规章统统破它一遍）直奔汉普斯特德（Hampstead）。
梅·桑德福德（May Sandford）正等着上校。她站在客厅壁炉边扣手套，竭力掩饰旧日友人来访的欢喜。
「你要去哪儿？」这是他劈头一句生硬的招呼，姑娘顿时绷起了脸。
「你没权利用这种口气问，」她平静地说，「不过我愿意告诉你。我要去赴宴。」

===Original===
“With whom?”
The colour came to her face, for she was really annoyed.
“With Colonel Black,” she said an effort to restrain her rising anger.
He nodded.
“I’m afraid I cannot allow you to go,” he said coolly.
The girl stared.

===Chinese===
「跟谁？」
她脸上泛起红晕，因为她当真恼了。
「跟布莱克上校。」她强压着往上冒的火气说。
他点头。
「恐怕我不能容你去。」他冷冷地说。
姑娘瞪着他。

===Original===
“Once and for all, Mr. Fellowe,” she said with quiet dignity, “you will understand that I am my own mistress. I shall do as I please. You have no right to dictate to me﻿—you have no right whatever”﻿—she stamped her foot angrily﻿—“to say what I may do and what I may not do. I shall go where and with whom I choose.”
“You will not go out tonight, at any rate,” said Frank grimly.
An angry flush came to her cheeks.
“If I chose to go tonight, I should go tonight,” she said.

===Chinese===
「最后一次说清楚，费洛先生，」她带着静静的尊严说，「你要明白，我是我自己的主人。我乐意怎么做就怎么做。你无权对我发号施令——你压根没有权利」——她气得直跺脚——「来规定我什么能做什么不能做。我乐意去哪儿、乐意跟谁去，就去哪儿、就跟谁去。」
「反正今夜你别想出门。」弗兰克狠狠地说。
一阵怒红涌上她的脸颊。
「我今夜要是想走，今夜就走。」她说。

===Original===
“Indeed, you will do nothing of the sort.” He was quite cool now﻿—master of himself﻿—completely under control.
“I shall be outside this house,” he said, “for the rest of the night. If you go out with this man I shall arrest you.”
She started and took a step back.
“I shall arrest you,” he went on determinedly. “I don’t care what happens to me afterwards. I will trump up any charge against you. I will take you to the station, through the streets, and put you in the iron dock as though you were a common thief. I’ll do it because I love you,” he said passionately, “because you are the biggest thing in the world to me﻿—because I love you better than life, better than you can love yourself, better than any man could love you. And do you know why I will take you to the police-station?” he went on earnestly. “Because you will be safe there, and the women who look after you will allow no dog like this fellow to have communication with you﻿—because he dare not follow you there, whatever else he dare. As for him﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你确实休想。」此刻他已十分冷静——自己是自己的主人——完全收放自如。
「今夜剩下的时间，」他说，「我就守在这宅子外面。你要是跟这个人出去，我就逮捕你。」
她一颤，退了一步。
「我就逮捕你，」他斩钉截铁地接着说。「我不在乎自己事后怎么样。我会随便给你编个罪名。我会把你带走，一路穿过大街，送进警局，把你关进铁栏被告席，就像关一个寻常的小贼。我这么做是因为我爱你，」他激动地说，「因为对我来说你是天底下顶大的事——因为我爱你胜过生命，胜过你爱自己，胜过任何一个男人所能爱你的。你知道我为什么要把你带去警局吗？」他诚恳地接着说。「因为在那儿你是安全的，照看你的那些女人不会让这么一条狗再跟你有任何往来——因为别处他敢跟，那儿他不敢。至于他——」

===Original===
He turned savagely about as a resplendent Black entered the room. Black stopped at the sight of the other’s face and dropped his hand to his pocket.
“You look out for me,” said Frank, and Black’s face blanched.
The girl had recovered her speech.
“How dare you﻿—how dare you!” she whispered. “You tell me that you will arrest me. How dare you! And you say you love me!” she said scornfully.

===Chinese===
他猛地转身，恰逢一身华服、光彩照人的布莱克走进屋来。布莱克一见对方的脸色便止了步，手落向口袋。
「你给我提防着点儿。」弗兰克说。布莱克的脸刷地白了。
姑娘这时找回了说话的能力。
「你好大的胆——你好大的胆！」她低声道。「你说你要逮捕我。你怎敢！你还说你爱我！」她轻蔑地说。

===Original===
He nodded slowly. “Yes,” he said, quietly enough. “I love you. I love you enough to make you hate me. Can I love you any more than that?”
His voice was bitter, and there was something of helplessness in it too, but the determination that underlay his words could not be mistaken.
He did not leave her until Black had taken his leave, and in his pardonable perturbation he forgot that he intended searching the colonel for a certain green bottle with a glass stopper.
Colonel Black returned to his flat that night to find unmistakable evidence that the apartment had been most systematically searched. There existed, however, no evidence as to how his visitors had gained admission. The doors had been opened, despite the fact that they were fastened by a key which had no duplicate, and with locks that were apparently unpickable. The windows were intact, and no attempt had been made to remove money and valuables from the desk which had been ransacked. The only proof of identity they had left behind was the seal which he found attached to the blotting-pad on his desk.

===Chinese===
他缓缓点头。「是的，」他说，语气已足够平静，「我爱你。我爱到足以让你恨我。我还能爱得比这更多吗？」
他的话音苦涩，其中也夹着几分无奈，但话底下那份决心，谁也错认不了。
直到布莱克告辞，他才离开她。而在那阵情有可原的心绪纷乱之中，他忘了自己原打算搜一搜上校身上那只带玻璃塞子的绿瓶子。
布莱克上校当夜回到公寓，发现确凿无疑的迹象：这套屋子被人极有章法地搜过一遍。然而来访者是如何进门的，却半点痕迹也无。门分明被打开过，尽管用的是一把没有第二把的钥匙上的锁，那锁又显然无法撬拨。窗户完好无损，被翻得乱七八糟的书桌上，钱钞贵重物品一样未动。来客留下的唯一身份凭证，是他在桌上吸墨纸垫上发现的那枚封印。

===Original===
They had gone methodically to work, dropped a neat round splash of sealing-wax, and had as neatly pressed the seal of the organization upon it. There was no other communication, but in its very simplicity this plain “IV” was a little terrifying. It seemed that the members of the Four defied all his efforts at security, laughed to scorn his patent locks, knew more about his movements than his most intimate friends, and chose their own time for their visitations.
This would have been disconcerting to a man of less character than Black; but Black was one who had lived through a score of years﻿—each year punctuated, at regular intervals, with threats of the most terrible character. He had ever lived in the shadow of reprisal, yet he had never suffered punishment.
It was his most fervent boast that he never lost his temper, that he never did anything in a flurry. Now, perhaps for the first time in his life, he was going to work actuated by a greater consideration than self-interest﻿—a consideration of vengeance.
It made him less careful than he was wont to be. He did not look for shadowers that evening, yet shadowers there had been﻿—not one but many.

===Chinese===
他们从容不迫地干活，滴了一小滴齐整的圆形封蜡，又同样齐整地把组织的印章摁了上去。再没有别的通信，可正是这份简单，让这个光秃秃的「IV」透着几分骇人。四义士的成员们似乎全然不把他的一切防范放在眼里，把他那些专利锁视同儿戏，对他的行踪比他最亲密的朋友知道得还多，连登门造访的时间也由他们自己挑。
若换了定力稍逊于布莱克的人，这足以令人心神不宁；可布莱克是熬过了二十年的人——年年都按着固定的节拍，插进几段最狠毒的威胁。他始终活在报复的阴影之下，却从未受过惩罚。
他最爱自诩的，便是自己从不发脾气，从不手忙脚乱地行事。而现在，或许是他生平头一遭，他要为一种比利己更重大的考量行事了——复仇的考量。
这使他不及素日那么谨慎。那天傍晚他没有提防盯梢的人，然而盯梢的人确曾有过——不是一个，而是许多。

## III An Adventure in Pimlico / 三 皮姆利科奇遇

===Original===
In the meantime our constable had reached a small tavern in the vicinity of Regent Street.
He entered the bar and, ordering a drink, took a seat in the corner of the spacious saloon. There were two or three people about; there were two or three men drinking at the bar and talking﻿—men in loud suits, who cast furtive glances at every newcomer. He knew them to be commonplace criminals of the first type. They did not engage his attention: he flew higher.
He sat in the corner, apparently absorbed in an evening paper, with his whisky and soda before him scarcely touched, waiting.
It was not the first time he had been here, nor would it be the first time he had waited without any result. But he was patient and dogged in the pursuit of his object.
The clock pointed to a quarter after ten, when the swing-doors were pushed open and two men entered.

===Chinese===
这且不提，咱们的警员已来到摄政街（Regent Street）附近一家小酒馆。
他走进酒吧，要了杯酒，在宽敞店堂的角落里落座。店里只有两三个客人；吧台边有两三个男人边喝边聊——都穿着花哨扎眼的衣裳，眼睛贼溜溜地扫视每个新来的人。他知道这是些最平常不过的下等罪犯。这类人引不起他的注意：他要钓的是更大的鱼。
他坐在角落里，看晚报看得煞有介事，面前那杯威士忌苏打几乎没动，等着。
这不是他头一回来，也不会是头一回白等一场。但为达目的，他有的是耐心和犟劲。
时钟指向十点一刻，弹簧门被推开，进来两个人。

===Original===
For the greater part of half an hour the two were engaged in a low-voiced consultation. Over his paper Frank could see the face of Sparks. He was the jackal of the Black gang, the man-of-all-trades. To him were deputed the meanest of Black’s commissions, and worthily did he serve his master. The other was known to Frank as Jakobs, a common thief and a pensioner of the benevolent colonel.
The conversation was punctuated either by glances at the clock above the bar or at Sparks’ watch, and at a quarter to eleven the two men rose and went out.
Frank followed, leaving his drink almost untouched.
The men turned into Regent Street, walked a little way up, and then hailed a taxi.
Another cab was passing. Frank beckoned it.

===Chinese===
有大半个钟头，那两人一直低声商议。弗兰克从报纸边缘望得见斯帕克斯（Sparks）的脸。此人是布莱克团伙里的豺狼，打杂的全把式。布莱克手下最见不得人的差事都派给他，而他也当之无愧地效忠主子。另一个弗兰克认得，是雅各布斯，一个寻常小贼，那位仁厚上校的年金食客。
谈话不时被抬眼望吧台上方的挂钟或斯帕克斯的怀表打断，到十一点差一刻，两人起身出去了。
弗兰克跟了出去，那杯酒几乎没动。
两人拐进摄政街，往前走了一小段，招手叫了辆出租车。
另一辆车正巧驶过，弗兰克招手叫住。

===Original===
“Follow that yellow cab,” he said to the driver, “and keep a reasonable distance behind, and when it sets down, pass it and drop me farther along the street.”
The man touched his cap. The two cabs moved on.
They went in the direction of Victoria, passed the great station on the left, turned down Grosvenor Road on the right, and were soon in the labyrinth of streets that constitute Pimlico.
The first cab pulled up at a big gaunt house in a street which had once been fashionable, but which now hovered indescribably between slums and shabby gentility.
Frank saw the two men get out, and descended himself a few hundred yards farther along on the opposite side of the street. He had marked the house. There was no difficulty in distinguishing it; a brass plate was attached to the door announcing it to be an employment agency﻿—as, indeed, it was.

===Chinese===
「跟上那辆黄车，」他对司机说，「保持个合适的距离，等它靠边下客，你就开过去，把我放在街那头远一点的地方。」
司机碰了碰帽檐。两辆车一前一后开动了。
车子朝维多利亚（Victoria）方向开去，从大车站的左边经过，右拐进格罗夫纳路（Grosvenor Road），很快钻进了构成皮姆利科（Pimlico）的那片迷宫似的街巷。
头一辆车在一条街上的一所空荡荡的大宅前停住。那街当年曾经时髦，如今却说不清道不明地悬在贫民窟与破落体面之间。
弗兰克看着那两人下车，自己在街对面再往前几百码处下了车。他记下了那所房子。辨认它毫不费事：门上钉着一块铜牌，标明这是一家职业介绍所——事实上，它也确实是。

===Original===
His quarry had entered before he strode across towards the house. He crossed the road and took a position from whence he could watch the door.
The half-hour after twelve had chimed from a neighbouring church before anything happened.
A policeman on his beat had passed Frank with a resentful sidelong glance, and the few pedestrians who were abroad at that hour viewed him with no less suspicion.
The chime of the neighbouring church had hardly died away when a private car came swiftly along the road and pulled up with a jerk in front of the house.

===Chinese===
等他大步朝那宅子走去时，他盯的猎物已经进了门。他横过马路，拣了个能盯住大门的位置。
邻近教堂的钟敲过十二点半，才有动静。
一名巡警巡到这里，经过弗兰克身边时怨气冲冲地斜了他一眼；那个钟点还在外头走动的寥寥几个行人，看他的眼神也同样的疑神疑鬼。
邻近教堂的钟声余音未散，一辆私家车已顺着街飞驰而来，猛地一顿，停在那宅子门前。

===Original===
A man descended. From where he stood Frank had no difficulty in recognizing Black. That he was expected was evident from the fact that the door was immediately opened to him.
Three minutes later another car came down the street and stopped a few doors short of the house, as though the driver was not quite certain as to which was his destination.
The newcomer was a stranger to Frank. In the uncertain light cast by a street lamp he seemed to be fashionably dressed.
As he turned to give instructions to his chauffeur, Fellowe caught a glimpse of a spotless white shirtfront beneath the long dark overcoat.
He hesitated at the foot of the steps which led to the door, and ascended slowly and fumbled for a moment at the bell. Before he could touch it the door opened. There was a short parley as the new man entered.

===Chinese===
一个男人下了车。从站的地方，弗兰克一眼就认出是布莱克。人家在等他，这一点显而易见，因为门立刻为他开了。
三分钟后，又一辆车顺街而来，在隔几户人家外停住，仿佛司机拿不准目的地是哪一所。
新来的这位弗兰克不认识。在路灯投下的朦胧光影里，他似乎衣着入时。
他转身吩咐司机时，费洛瞥见他深色长大衣底下露出雪白笔挺的衬衫前胸。
他在通向大门的台阶下踌躇了一下，慢慢走上去，在门铃前摸索了片刻。还没等他碰铃，门就开了。新客进屋时，门口短短交涉了几句。

===Original===
Frank, waiting patiently on the other side of the road, saw a light appear suddenly on the first floor.
Did he but know, this gathering was in the nature of a board meeting, a board meeting of a company more heavily financed than some of the most respected houses in the City, having its branches in various parts of the world, its agents, its business system﻿—its very books, if they could be found and the ciphered entries unravelled.
Black sat at one end of the long table and the last arrival at the other. He was a florid young man of twenty-six, with a weak chin and a slight yellow moustache. His face would be familiar to all racing men, for this was the sporting baronet, Sir Isaac Tramber.
There was something about Sir Isaac which kept him on the outside fringe of good society, in spite of the fact that he came of a stock which was indelibly associated with England’s story: the baronetcy had been created as far back as the seventeenth century.
It was a proud name, and many of his ancestors had borne it proudly. None the less, his name was taboo, his invitations politely refused, and never reciprocated.

===Chinese===
弗兰克在马路对面耐心守着，看见二楼忽然亮起一盏灯。
他要是知道就好了：这场聚会其实就是董事会，一家公司的董事会，其资金之雄厚超过伦敦城（the City）里某些最受敬重的商号，在世界各地设有分号，有代理，有营业体系——甚至有自己的账本，只要找得到、又解得开那些密码条目的话。
布莱克坐在长桌一端，最后到的那位坐在另一端。这是个面色红润的年轻人，二十六岁，下巴绵软，蓄一撇浅黄的唇髭。凡是赛马圈里的人没有不认得他这张脸的，因为这位正是赛马场上的从男爵，艾萨克·特兰伯爵士（Sir Isaac Tramber）。
艾萨克爵士身上有种东西，使他永远徘徊在上流社会的边缘，尽管他出身一个同英国历史密不可分的门第：这从男爵爵位早在十七世纪便已册封。
那是个骄傲的姓氏，他的许多祖辈都当之无愧地戴着它。尽管如此，他的名字却成了忌讳，他发的请帖被人客气地辞掉，也从没人回请过他。

===Original===
There had been some unfathomable scandal associated with his name. Society is very lenient to its children. There are crimes and sins which it readily, or if not readily, at any rate eventually, forgives and condones, but there are some which are unpardonable, unforgivable. Once let a man commit those crimes, or sin those sins, and the doors of Mayfair are closed forever against him.
Around his head was a cloud of minor scandal, but that which brought down the bar of good society was the fact that he had ridden his own horse at one of the Midland meetings. It had started a hot favourite﻿—five to two on.
The circumstances of that race are inscribed in the annals of the Jockey Club. How an infuriated mob broke down the barriers and attempted to reach this amateur jockey was ably visualized by the sporting journalists who witnessed the extraordinary affair. Sir Isaac was brought before the local stewards and the case submitted to the stewards of the Jockey Club. The next issue of the *Racing Calendar* contained the ominous announcement that Sir Isaac Tramber had been “warned off” Newmarket Heath.
Under this ban he sat for four years, till the withdrawal of the notice.
He might again attend race-meetings and own horses, and he did both, but the ban of society, that unwritten “warning off” notice, had not been withdrawn.

===Chinese===
他的名字曾牵涉进一桩无从究诘的丑闻。上流社会对待自家儿女素来宽厚。有些罪过它爽快地——即便不爽快，至少终归——宽恕和赦免；但也有些罪过不可赦、不可恕。一个人一旦犯了那种罪、造了那种孽，梅费尔（Mayfair）的大门就对他永远关上了。
他头顶本就笼罩着种种小小的丑闻，可真正断送他上流社会资格的，是他骑着自己的马参加了一次米德兰赛马会。那匹马出场时是大热门——赔率开到五对二。
那场赛马的前后情由，都载入了赛马会的史册。愤怒的人群如何冲垮围栏、如何试图揪住这位业余骑师，亲睹这场奇事的赛马记者们写得活灵活现。艾萨克爵士被带到当地赛事监察官面前，案子又呈交了赛马会的监察官。下一期《赛马年鉴》登出一则不祥的通告：艾萨克·特兰伯爵士已被「逐出」纽马基特荒原（Newmarket Heath）。
这道禁令他领了四年，直到通告撤销。
他可以再度出席赛马会、再度养马了，两样他都干了，可是上流社会的那道禁令——那张没写出来的「逐客」通告——却始终没有撤销。

===Original===
The doors of every decent house were closed to him.
Only one friend he had in the fashionable world, and there were people who said that the Earl of Verlond, that old and crabbed and envenomed man, merely championed his unpromising protégé out of sheer perversity, and there was ample justification for this contention of a man who was known to have the bitterest tongue in Europe.
The descent to hell is proverbially easy, and Sir Isaac Tramber’s descent was facilitated by that streak of decadence which had made itself apparent even in his early youth.
As he sat at one end of the board-table, both hands stuffed into his trousers pockets, his head on one side like a perky bird, he proved no mean man of business, as Black had discovered earlier in their acquaintanceship.
“We are all here now, I think,” said Black, looking humorously at his companion. They had left Sparks and his friend in a room below.

===Chinese===
每一户体面人家的门都对他关着。
他在时髦场中只有一个朋友。有人说，弗隆德勋爵（Lord Verlond）——那个又老又乖僻又刻毒的人——之所以回护这个不成器的被保护人，纯粹是出于一股子别扭劲儿，而这话对这位素以全欧洲最毒之舌著称的人物来说，倒也事出有因。
堕入地狱向来是容易的，而艾萨克·特兰伯爵士的下坠，又被那股颓靡的根性大大加速——那根性在他少年时代便已显露无遗。
此刻他坐在会议桌的一端，双手插在裤袋里，脑袋歪向一边，活像只神气的小鸟。事实证明他做生意的本事并不含糊——这一点，布莱克在他们相识之初便已领教。
「我看人齐了。」布莱克望着同伴，眼里带着笑意。他们把斯帕克斯和他那位朋友留在了楼下一个房间里。

===Original===
“I have asked you to come tonight,” he said, “to hear a report of this business. I am happy to tell you that we have made a bigger profit this year than we have ever made in the course of our existence.”
He went on to give details of the work for which he had been responsible, and he did so with the air and in the manner of one who was addressing a crowded boardroom.
“People would say,” said the colonel oracularly, “that the business of outside broker is inconsistent with my acknowledged position in the world of finance; therefore I deem it expedient to dissociate myself from our little firm. But the outside broker is a useful person﻿—especially the outside broker who has a hundred thousand clients. There are stocks of mine which he can recommend with every evidence of disinterestedness, and just now I am particularly desirous that these stocks should be recommended.”
“Do we lose anything by Fanks’ death?” asked the baronet carelessly. “Hard luck on him, wasn’t it? But he was awfully fat.”
The colonel regarded the questioner with a calm stare.

===Chinese===
「今晚请你过来，」他说，「是听取这桩生意的报告。我很高兴地告诉你，今年的进项，比我们开业以来的任何一年都要丰厚。」
接着他细数自己负责的那摊事，那做派、那口吻，俨然正对着一间坐满董事的会议室致辞。
「人家会说，」上校一副预言家的口气，「场外经纪这门营生，同我在金融界公认的地位不相称；所以我以为，还是同咱们的小商号脱离关系为宜。可场外经纪是个有用的人——尤其是手下有十万客户的场外经纪。我有几种股票，他可以摆出一副全无利害关系的模样大力推荐，而眼下我特别巴望这些股票被人推荐。」
「法恩克斯（Fanks）一死，咱们损失什么了吗？」从男爵漫不经心地问。「他死得可惜，是不是？不过他实在是太胖了。」
上校目光平静地端详着发问的人。

===Original===
“Do not let us refer to Fanks,” he said evenly. “The death of Fanks has very much upset me﻿—I do not wish to speak about it.”
The baronet nodded. “I never trusted him, poor chap,” he said, “any more than I trusted the other chap who made such an awful scene here a year ago﻿—February, wasn’t it?”
“Yes,” said the colonel briefly.
“It’s lucky for us he died too,” said the tactless aristocrat, “because﻿—”
“We’ll get on with the business.”

===Chinese===
「咱们不要提法恩克斯了。」他不动声色地说。「法恩克斯的死叫我心里很不好受——我不愿谈这件事。」
从男爵点头。「我从来不信任他，可怜的家伙，」他说，「就像我不信任一年前在这儿大闹一场的那家伙一样——二月里，是不是？」
「是的。」上校简短地说。
「也多亏他死了，」这位不知轻重的贵族说，「因为——」
「咱们接着谈正事。」

===Original===
Colonel Black almost snarled the words.
But the baronet had something to say. He was troubled about his own security. It was when Black showed some sign of ending the business that Sir Isaac leant forward impatiently.
“There is one thing we haven’t discussed, Black,” he said.
Black knew what the thing was, and had carefully avoided mention of the subject.
“What is it?” he asked innocently.

===Chinese===
布莱克上校几乎是咬着牙说出这句话的。
可从男爵还有话要说。他为自己的安全忧心。等布莱克露出收场之意时，艾萨克爵士不耐烦地探身向前。
「有一件事咱们还没商量，布莱克。」他说。
布莱克知道那是什么事，也一直小心地避开不提。
「什么事？」他一脸无辜地问。

===Original===
“These fellows who are threatening us, or rather threatening you; they haven’t any idea who it is who is running the show, have they?” he asked, with some apprehension.
Black shook his head smilingly. “I think not,” he said.
“You are speaking, of course, of the ‘Four Just Men.’ ”
Sir Isaac gave a short nod.

===Chinese===
「那帮威胁咱们——或者说威胁你——的家伙，他们压根不知道这台戏是谁在唱，对吧？」他问，语气里带着几分惶恐。
布莱克微笑着摇头。「我想不知道。」他说。
「你说的当然是指『四义士』（the Four Just Men）。」
艾萨克爵士短短点了下头。

===Original===
“Yes,” Black went on, with an assumption of indifference, “I have had an anonymous letter from these gentlemen. As a matter of fact, my dear Sir Isaac, I haven’t the slightest doubt that the whole thing is a bluff.”
“What do you mean by ‘a bluff’?” demanded the other.
Black shrugged his shoulders.
“I mean that there is no such organization as the ‘Four Just Men.’ They are a myth. They have no existence. It is too melodramatic for words. Imagine four people gathered together to correct the laws of England. It savours more of the sensational novel than of real life.”
He laughed with apparent ease.

===Chinese===
「是啊，」布莱克接着说，装出一副满不在乎的样子，「这几位先生给我来过一封匿名信。说实在的，亲爱的艾萨克爵士，我毫不怀疑，这整个儿就是唬人。」
「你说『唬人』是什么意思？」对方诘问。
布莱克耸耸肩。
「我的意思是，根本没有什么『四义士』这个组织。那是瞎编的。他们不存在。这戏码夸张得没边儿了。想想看，四个人凑到一块儿来纠正英格兰的法律——这更像惊险小说里的桥段，不像真事。」
他笑得相当轻松。

===Original===
“These things,” he said, wagging his finger jocosely at the perturbed baronet, “do not happen in Pimlico. No, I suspect that our constable, the man I spoke to you about, is at the bottom of it. He is probably the whole Four of these desperate conspirators.” He laughed again.
Sir Isaac fingered his moustache nervously.
“It’s all rot to say they don’t exist; we know what they did six years ago, and I don’t like this other man a bit,” he grumbled.
“Don’t like which other man?”
“This interfering policeman,” he replied irritably. “Can’t he be squared?”

===Chinese===
「这种事，」他朝忧心忡忡的从男爵开玩笑地晃着手指说，「在皮姆利科是闹不起来的。不，我疑心根子就在咱们那位警员身上，就是我同你提过的那个人。这帮亡命阴谋家，多半是他一个人全包了。」他又笑了。
艾萨克爵士神经质地捻着唇髭。
「说他们不存在纯属胡扯；六年前他们干了什么咱们又不是不知道，再说另外那个人我一点儿也不喜欢。」他嘟囔道。
「不喜欢另外哪个？」
「这个多管闲事的警察。」他烦躁地答道。「就不能把他摆平吗？」

===Original===
“The constable?”
“Yes; you can square constables, I suppose, if you can square sergeants.” Sir Isaac Tramber had the gift of heavy sarcasm.
Black stroked his chin thoughtfully.
“Curiously enough,” he said, “I have never thought of that. I think we can try.” He glanced at his watch. “Now I’ll ask you just to clear out,” he said. “I have an appointment at half-past one.”
Sir Isaac smiled slowly.

===Chinese===
「那个警员？」
「对；我想警员能摆平，巡佐自然也能摆平。」艾萨克·特兰伯爵士颇有几分拙劣讥诮的天才。
布莱克若有所思地摸着下巴。
「说来也怪，」他说，「这事我从没想过。我看可以试试。」他瞥了一眼怀表。「现在请你先行回避，」他说，「一点半我有个约会。」
艾萨克爵士缓缓地笑了。

===Original===
“Rather a curious hour for an appointment,” he said.
“Ours is a curious business,” replied Colonel Black.
They rose, and Sir Isaac turned to Black.
“What is the appointment?” he asked.
Black smiled mysteriously. “It is rather a peculiar case,” he began.

===Chinese===
「这个约会可真挑了个古怪钟点。」他说。
「咱们干的本来就是古怪的营生。」布莱克上校答道。
两人起身，艾萨克爵士转向布莱克。
「什么约会？」他问。
布莱克神秘地一笑。「是桩相当特别的案子。」他开口道。

===Original===
He stopped suddenly.
There were hurried footsteps on the stairs without. A second later the door was flung open and Sparks burst into the room.
“Guv’nor,” he gasped, “they’re watching the house.”
“Who is watching?”
“There’s a busy3 on the other side of the road,” said the man, speaking graphically. “I spotted him, and the moment he saw I noticed him he moved off. He’s back again now. Me and Willie have been watching him.”

===Chinese===
他突然刹住了话头。
楼梯上传来一阵急促的脚步声。片刻之后门被推开，斯帕克斯闯了进来。
「老板，」他喘着气说，「有人盯着咱这房子。」
「谁在盯？」
「马路对过蹲着个『暗探』，」那人说得活灵活现。「我一眼就瞅出来了，他一见我瞧破他，挪了窝。这会儿又回来了。俺跟威利一直盯着他呢。」

===Original===
The two followed the agitated Sparks downstairs, where from a lower window they might watch, unobserved, the man who dared spy on their actions.
“If this is the police,” fumed Black, “that dog Gurden has failed me. He told me Scotland Yard were taking no action whatever.”
Frank, from his place of observation, was well aware that he had caused some consternation. He had seen Sparks turn back hurriedly with Jakobs and reenter the house. He observed the light go out suddenly on the first floor, and now he had a pretty shrewd idea that they were watching him through the glass panel of the doorway.
There was no more he could learn. So far his business had been a failure. It was no secret to him that Sir Isaac Tramber was an associate of Black’s, or that Jakobs and estimable Sparks were also partners in this concern.
He did not know what he hoped to find, or what he had hoped to accomplish.

===Chinese===
两人跟着慌慌张张的斯帕克斯下了楼，从楼下一扇窗户里，他们可以不被人察觉地观察那个胆敢窥探他们行动的人。
「要是这是警察的人，」布莱克怒道，「格登（Gurden）那条狗可把我坑了。他跟我说过，苏格兰场根本不会有什么动作。」
弗兰克从自己的观察点上，很清楚自己引起了不小的骚动。他看见斯帕克斯同雅各布斯匆匆折回，重新进了那宅子。他注意到二楼的灯忽然灭了，而现在他心里透亮：他们正从门上的玻璃格里瞧着他。
他再打听不出什么了。到此刻为止，他的差事算是砸了。艾萨克·特兰伯爵士是布莱克的同伙，雅各布斯和那位「可敬的」斯帕克斯也是这买卖的合伙人，这些他早有数。
他不知道自己指望找到什么，也不知道原本想办成什么。

===Original===
He was turning away in the direction of Victoria when his attention was riveted on the figure of a young man which was coming slowly along on the opposite sidewalk, glancing from time to time at the numbers which were inscribed on the fanlights of the doors.
He watched him curiously, then in a flash he realized his objective as he stopped in front of No. 63.
In half a dozen steps he had crossed the road towards him. The boy﻿—he was little more﻿—turned round, a little frightened at the sudden appearance.
Frank Fellowe walked up to him and recognized him. “You need not be scared,” he said, “I am a police officer. Are you going into that house?”
The young man looked at him for a moment and made no reply. Then, in a voice that shook, he said “Yes.”

===Chinese===
他正要转身往维多利亚方向走，忽然目光被对面人行道上一个年轻人的身影钉住了。那人慢慢走来，不时抬头看各家门楣气窗上标着的门牌号。
他好奇地打量着，等那人在第 63 号门前站定，他一下子明白了此行的目标。
他几步跨过马路迎上去。那是个孩子——也大不了多少——转过身来，被这突然出现的人吓了一跳。
弗兰克·费洛走上前，认出了他。「你不用害怕，」他说，「我是警察。你要进那所房子吗？」
年轻人看了他片刻，没有作声。然后，他用发抖的声音说了句「是」。

===Original===
“Are you going there to give Colonel Black certain information about your employer’s business?”
The young man seemed hypnotized by fear. He nodded. “Is your employer aware of the fact?”
Slowly he shook his head.
“Did *he* send you?” he asked suddenly, and Frank observed a note of terror in his voice.
“No,” he smiled, wondering internally who the “he” was. “I am here quite on my own, and my object is to warn you against trusting Colonel Black.”

===Chinese===
「你是要去把东家生意的某些情况报告给布莱克上校？」
年轻人像是被恐惧慑住了。他点了点头。「你东家知道有这回事吗？」
他缓缓摇头。
「是*他*派你来的吗？」他突然问。弗兰克听出他话音里有一丝惊惶。
「不是，」他微笑着说，心里纳闷这个「他」究竟是谁。「我完全是自己的主意来的，目的是提醒你，别信布莱克上校。」

===Original===
He jerked up his head, and Frank saw the flush that came to his face.
“You are Constable Fellowe,” he said suddenly.
To say that Frank was a little staggered is to express the position mildly.
“Yes,” he repeated, “I am Constable Fellowe.”
Whilst he was talking the door of the house had opened. From the position in which he stood Frank could not see this. Black merged stealthily and came down the steps towards him.

===Chinese===
他猛地抬起头，弗兰克看见一阵红晕涌上他的脸。
「你是费洛警员。」他突然说。
说弗兰克有点吃惊，还是往轻里说的。
「不错，」他重复道，「我是费洛警员。」
说话间，那宅子的大门已经开了。从所站的位置，弗兰克看不见这一幕。布莱克悄没声地闪出来，拾级而下，朝他走来。

===Original===
The agent had no other desire than to discover the identity of the man who was shadowing him. He was near enough to hear what the young man said.
“Fellowe,” he boomed, and came down the rest of the steps at a run. “So it’s you, is it?” he snarled. “It’s you interfering with my business again.”
“Something like that,” said Frank coolly.
He turned to the young man again.
“I tell you,” he said in a tone of authority, “that if you go into this house, or have anything whatever to do with this man, you will regret it to the last day of your life.”

===Chinese===
此公此刻别无所图，只想查明盯他梢的人是谁。这时他离得近，年轻人说的话都听在了耳里。
「费洛！」他瓮声喝道，一路小跑着下完余下的台阶。「原来是你，嗯？」他狞声道。「又是你来搅我的买卖。」
「差不多吧。」弗兰克冷冷地说。
他又转向那年轻人。
「我告诉你，」他用权威的口吻说，「你要是走进这所房子，或者同这个人发生半点瓜葛，你会后悔到咽气的那一天。」

===Original===
“You shall pay for this!” fumed Black. “I’ll have your coat from your back, constable. I’ll give you in charge. I’ll﻿—I’ll﻿—”
“You have an excellent opportunity,” said Frank. His quick eye had detected the figure of a constable on the other side of the road, walking slowly towards them. “There’s a policeman over there; call him now and give me in charge. There is no reason why you shouldn’t﻿—no reason why you should want to avoid publicity of the act.”
“Oh, no, no!”
It was the youth who spoke.
“Colonel Black, I must come another time.”

===Chinese===
「这个你要付出代价！」布莱克勃然大怒。「我要扒了你的警皮，警员。我要送官究办。我要——我要——」
「机会就在眼前。」弗兰克说。他眼尖，已瞧见马路对面一个警察的身影正朝他们缓缓走来。「那儿就有个警察；现在就喊他，把我送官好了。你没什么不能喊的——也没道理不想把这事闹得人尽皆知。」
「哦，不，不！」
说话的是那年轻人。
「布莱克上校，我得改天再来了。」

===Original===
He turned furiously on Frank. “As to you﻿—” he began, gaining courage from Black’s presence.
“As to you,” retorted Frank, “avoid bad company!”
He hesitated, then turned and walked quickly away, leaving the two men alone on the pavement.
The three watchers in the hall eyed the scene curiously, and two of them at least anticipated instructions from Black which would not be followed by pleasant results for Frank.

===Chinese===
他狠狠转向弗兰克。「至于你——」他开了腔，仗着布莱克在场壮起了胆子。
「至于你嘛，」弗兰克回敬道，「少跟坏人来往！」
他迟疑了一下，随即转身快步走开，把那两个人撇在了人行道上。
门厅里三个守望的人好奇地注视着这一幕，其中至少两位正等着布莱克发话，而那话一旦出口，弗兰克多半没有好果子吃。

===Original===
With an effort, however, Black controlled his temper. He, too, had seen the shadow on the other side of the road.
“Look here, Constable Fellowe,” he said, with forced geniality, “I know you’re wrong, and you think you’re right. Just come inside and let’s argue this matter out.”
He waited, his nimble mind evolving a plan for dealing with this dangerous enemy. He did not imagine that Frank would accept the invitation, and he was genuinely astounded when, without another word, the constable turned and slowly ascended the steps to the door.

===Chinese===
然而布莱克强压怒火，按住了性子。马路对面那条人影，他也瞧见了。
「听我说，费洛警员，」他强作和蔼地说，「我知道你错了，而你自己以为对。请进来吧，咱们把这件事理论个明白。」
他等着，机敏的头脑里正盘算着收拾这个危险对头的计策。他没料到弗兰克会接受邀请，所以当那警员一言不发、转身缓步登上台阶走向大门时，他当真吃了一惊。

## II Colonel Black, Financier / 二 布莱克上校，金融家

===Original===
The firm of Black and Gram had something of a reputation in City circles. Gram might have been a man beyond reproach﻿—a veritable Bayard of finance, a churchgoer, and a generous subscriber to charities. Indeed, Black complained with good-humoured irritation﻿—if the combination can be visualized﻿—that Gram would ruin him one of these fine days by his quixotic munificence.
Gram allowed his heart to dictate to his head; he was too soft for business, too retiring. The City was very sceptical about Gram. It compared him with a certain Mrs. Harris, but Black did not fly into a temper; he smiled mysteriously at all the suspicion which the City entertained or expressed, and went on deploring the criminal rustiness of a man who apparently sought, by Black’s account, to made the firm reputable in spite of the rumours which centred about Colonel J. Black.
In this way did Black describe himself, though the Army list was innocent of his name, and even a search through the voluminous rolls of the American honorary ranks failed to reveal any association.
Black and Gram floated companies and dealt largely in stocks and shares. They recommended to their clients certain shares, and the clients bought or sold according to the advice given, and at the end of a certain period of time, Black and Gram wrote politely regretting that the cover deposited had been exhausted, and urgently requesting, with as little delay as possible, the discharge of those liabilities which in some extraordinary fashion the client had incurred. This, at any rate, was the humble beginnings of a firm which was destined to grow to important proportions. Gram went out of the business﻿—was never in it, if the truth be told. One doubts if he ever breathed the breath of life﻿—and Black grew in prosperity. His was a name to conjure with in certain circles. In others it was never mentioned. The financial lords of the City﻿—the Farings, the Wertheiners, the Scott-Teasons﻿—had no official knowledge of his existence. They went about their business calmly, loaning their millions at a ridiculously small percentage, issuing Government loans, discounting bills, buying bullion, and suchlike operations which filled the hours between eleven o’clock, when their electric broughams set them down in Threadneedle Street, and four o’clock, when their electric broughams picked them up again.
They read of Colonel Black in their grave way, because there were days when he dominated the financial columns. They read of his mighty stock deals, of his Argentine electric deal, his rubber notations and his Canadian copper mines. They read about him, neither approving nor disapproving. They regarded him with that dispassionate interest which a railway engine has for a motorcar.
When, on one never-to-be-forgotten occasion, he approached the financial lords with a promising proposition, they “regretted they were unable to entertain Colonel Black’s interesting suggestion.” A little baffled, a little annoyed, he approached the big American group, for it was necessary for the success of his scheme that there should be names on his prospectus. Shrewd fellows, these Americans, thought Colonel Black, and he set forth his proposals in terms which were at once immodest and alluring. In reply﻿—

===Chinese===
布莱克—格拉姆商行（Black and Gram）在伦敦城（the City）的圈子里小有名声。格拉姆（Gram）也许本是个无可指摘的人——金融界一位货真价实的贝亚尔骑士，上教堂做礼拜，给慈善事业慷慨捐款。的确，布莱克还带着一种好脾气的恼火——倘若这二者可以并存的话——抱怨说，格拉姆那种堂吉诃德式的豪爽，早晚有一天会把他毁掉。
格拉姆让心指挥脑袋；做生意他心肠太软，人又太不喜出头露面。伦敦城对格拉姆深表怀疑，把他比作那位子虚乌有的「哈里斯太太」，可布莱克从不发火；面对伦敦城肚里揣着或嘴上挂着的种种疑心，他只神秘地微笑，继而痛惜格拉姆那「罪不可赦」的迂腐——照布莱克的说法，此公分明是想让商行挣个好名声，全然不顾满城风雨都围着 J·布莱克上校本人打转。
布莱克便是这样给自己安头衔的，尽管《陆军名录》里压根没有他的名字，甚至连卷帙浩繁的美国名誉军衔名册翻个底朝天，也寻不出半点瓜葛。
布莱克—格拉姆商行创设公司，大做股票生意。他们向客户推荐某些股票，客户依着指点买进卖出，过了一段时日，布莱克—格拉姆便客客气气来函表示遗憾：您存缴的保证金已经用罄，并敦请阁下从速清偿您以某种奇特方式欠下的债务。这至少就是这家日后声势浩大的商行寒微的开端。格拉姆退出了这盘生意——说实话，他压根就没进过这盘生意，人们简直怀疑他是否呼吸过人间的空气——而布莱克日渐兴旺。在某些圈子里，他的名字一言可以动天。在另一些圈子里，这名字从来无人提起。伦敦城的金融巨头们——法林家、韦特海默家、斯科特-蒂森家——对他是否在世毫无官方的认知。他们安安稳稳做自己的事：以低得可笑的利息放出数以百万计的贷款，发行国债，贴现票据，买进金银条——诸如此类的营生，填满了从十一点到四点这几个钟头：十一点，他们的电动布鲁厄姆轿车把他们送到针线街（Threadneedle Street）；四点，车又把他们接回去。
他们以凝重的姿态读到布莱克上校的消息，因为有些日子，财经版简直由他独占。他们读到他手笔阔大的股票交易，读到他那桩阿根廷电气买卖、他的橡胶牌价和他的加拿大铜矿。他们读他的消息，既不赞许，也不反对。他们打量他，带着火车头看待汽车的那种不动声色的兴味。
有一个永难忘怀的日子，他带着一个前景可观的方案去亲近那些金融巨头，人家「深表遗憾，无法考虑布莱克上校有趣的建议」。他有点受挫，有点恼火，转而去叩美国大财团的大门，因为他的计划要成功，招股书上就不能没有响亮的名字。这些美国人是精明角色，布莱克上校想。于是他把自己的方案说得既狂妄又诱人。对方的回信是——

===Original===
> “**Dear friend**,” (it was one of those American businesses that turn down a million dollars with five cents’ worth of friendship), “we have carefully considered your proposition, and whilst we are satisfied that you will make money by its fruition, we are not so certain that we shall.”
Black came to the City of London one afternoon to attend a board of directors’ meeting. He had been out of town for a few days, recruiting in advance, as he informed the board with a touch of facetiousness, for the struggle that awaited him.
He was a man of middle height, broad of shoulder. His face was thin and lank, his complexion sallow, with a curious uniform yellowness. If you saw Colonel Black once you would never forget him﻿—not only because of that yellow face of his, that straight black bar of eyebrow and the thin-lipped mouth, but the very personality of the man impressed itself indelibly on the mind of the observer.
His manner was quick, almost abrupt; his replies brusque. A sense of finality marked his decisions. If the financial lords knew him not, there were thousands that did. His name was a household word in England. There was hardly a middle-class family that did not hold his stock. The little “street punters” hung on his word, his issues were subscribed for twice over. And he had established himself in five years; almost unknown before, he had risen to the dizziest heights in that short space of time.
Punctual to the minute, he entered the boardroom of the suite of offices he occupied in Moorgate Street.

===Chinese===
> 「**亲爱的朋友**，」（这是那种肯用五美分交情回绝一百万美元的美国商行）「我们已仔细考虑了您的提议。我们相信此议开花结果时您必能赚钱，至于我们是否也能，就不那么有把握了。」
一天下午，布莱克来到伦敦城出席董事会。他离城数日，用他半带打趣地告诉众董事的话说，是提前养精蓄锐，以备等待着他的那场搏斗。
他中等身材，肩膀宽阔。脸瘦长，面色蜡黄，黄得奇特而均匀。布莱克上校这个人，只要你见过一面就永远不会忘记——不仅因为那张黄脸、那道又黑又直的粗眉和那两片薄嘴唇，更因为这人本身的气性，会不可磨灭地烙进旁观者的脑子。
他举止急促，近乎突兀；答话生硬。他拍板定案，总带着一锤定音的气派。金融巨头们不认识他，成千上万的人却认识。在英国，他的名字家喻户晓。中产人家几乎户户持有他的股票。街面的小「投机客」对他一言一动奉若神明，他发行的股票每每获得两倍的超额认购。他发迹只用了五年；此前几乎默默无闻，却在这么短的工夫里攀上了令人眩晕的高峰。
他分秒不差地走进自己在穆尔盖特街（Moorgate Street）那套写字间里的会议室。

===Original===
The meeting had threatened to be a stormy one. Again an amalgamation was in the air, and again the head of one group of ironmasters﻿—it was an iron combine he was forming﻿—had stood against the threats and blandishments of Black and his emissaries.
“The others are weakening,” said Fanks, that big, hairless man; “you promised us that you would put him straight.”
“I will keep my promise,” said Black shortly.
“Widdison stood out, but he died,” continued Fanks. “We can’t expect Providence to help us all the time.”
Black’s eyebrows lowered.

===Chinese===
这场会眼看要闹得不欢而散。又要谈合并了，而一家铁业巨头的当家人——布莱克拼凑的正是一个钢铁联合企业——再一次顶住了布莱克及其说客的威胁与哄诱。
「别人都在松口了，」法恩克斯（Fanks）说。那是个高大无毛的男人。「你答应过我们，说能把他捋顺。」
「我会兑现承诺。」布莱克简短地说。
「威迪森（Widdison）顶住不肯，可他死了，」法恩克斯接着说。「咱们总不能指望老天爷回回帮忙。」
布莱克的眉毛压了下来。

===Original===
“I do not like jests of that kind,” he said. “Sandford is an obstinate man, a proud man; he needs delicate handling. Leave him to me.”
The meeting adjourned lamely enough, and Black was leaving the room when Fanks beckoned to him.
“I met a man yesterday who knew your friend, Dr. Essley, in Australia,” he said.
“Indeed.”
Colonel Black’s face was expressionless.

===Chinese===
「这种玩笑我不爱听。」他说。「桑德福德（Sandford）是个倔人，也是个傲人；得用细功夫对付。把他交给我。」
会议无精打采地散了场。布莱克正要离席，法恩克斯朝他招了招手。
「我昨天碰到一个人，早年在澳大利亚就认识你的朋友埃斯利博士（Dr. Essley）。」他说。
「是嘛。」
布莱克上校的脸上毫无表情。

===Original===
“Yes﻿—he knew him in his very early days﻿—he was asking me where he could find him.”
The other shrugged his shoulders.
“Essley is abroad, I think﻿—you don’t like him?”
Augustus Fanks shook his head. “I don’t like doctors who come to see me in the middle of the night, who are never to be found when they are wanted, and are always jaunting off to the Continent.”
“He is a busy man,” excused Black. “By the way, where is your friend staying?”

===Chinese===
「是啊——他认识的是他早年的样子——他还向我打听，上哪儿能找着他。」
对方耸耸肩。
「我想埃斯利在国外——你不喜欢他？」
奥古斯塔斯·法恩克斯（Augustus Fanks）摇头。「我不喜欢那种半夜三更来看病、要用的时候遍寻不着、动不动就跑到大陆上逍遥的大夫。」
「他是个忙人。」布莱克替他开脱。「对了，你那位朋友住在哪儿？」

===Original===
“He isn’t a friend, he’s a sort of prospector, name of Weld, who has come to London with a mining proposition. He is staying at Varlet’s Temperance Hotel in Bloomsbury.”
“I will tell Essley when he returns,” said Black, nodding his head.
He returned to his private office in a thoughtful mood. All was not well with Colonel Black. Reputedly a millionaire, he was in the position of many a financier who counted his wealth in paper. He had got so far climbing on the shadows. The substance was still beyond his reach. He had organized successful combinations, but the cost had been heavy. Millions had flowed through his hands, but precious little had stuck. He was that curious contradiction﻿—a dishonest man with honest methods. His schemes were financially sound, yet it had needed almost superhuman efforts to get them through.
He was in the midst of an unpleasant reverie when a tap on the door aroused him. It opened to admit Fanks.
He frowned at the intruder, but the other pulled up a chair and sat down. “Look here, Black,” he said, “I want to say something to you.”

===Chinese===
「算不上朋友，是个探矿人之类，姓韦尔德（Weld），带着一项矿业方案来伦敦。眼下住在布卢姆斯伯里（Bloomsbury）的瓦莱特禁酒旅馆。」
「等埃斯利回来，我告诉他。」布莱克点头说。
他回到自己的私人办公室，心事重重。布莱克上校的日子并不舒坦。名义上是百万富翁，实际处境却同许多按纸面计算财富的金融家一样：一路踩着影子往上爬，爬到现在这个地步，实物仍然够不着。他组织过几桩成功的联合企业，代价却极沉重。几百万几百万的钱从他手里流过，粘在手上的微乎其微。他是那种奇特的矛盾体——用正当手段办事的骗子。他的方案在财务上都站得住，可要把它们办成，几乎需要超人的气力。
他正陷在不愉快的遐想里，一阵敲门声惊醒了他。门开了，进来的是法恩克斯。
他对这不速之客皱起眉头，可对方拖过一把椅子坐下了。「听我说，布莱克，」他说，「我有话要对你讲。」

===Original===
“Say it quickly.”
Fanks took a cigar from his pocket and lit it.
“You’ve had a marvellous career,” he said. “I remember when you started with a little bucket-shop﻿—well, we won’t call it a bucket-shop,” he said hastily as he saw the anger rising in the other’s face, “outside broker’s. You had a mug﻿—I mean an inexperienced partner who found the money.”
“Yes.”
“Not the mysterious Gram, I think?”

===Chinese===
「那就快说。」
法恩克斯从兜里掏出一支雪茄，点上。
「你这番经历不可谓不辉煌。」他说。「我记得你起家时开的是一家小小的『桶店』——好吧，咱们别叫它桶店，」他一见对方脸上腾起怒气，赶忙改口，「叫场外经纪行。你当时搭着一个冤大头——我是说，一个负责出钱、毫无经验的合伙人。」
「是的。」
「我猜不是那位神秘的格拉姆吧？」

===Original===
“His successor﻿—there was nothing mysterious about Gram.”
“A successor named Flint?”
“Yes.”
“He died unexpectedly, didn’t he?”
“I believe he did,” said Black abruptly.

===Chinese===
「是他的继任——格拉姆这人毫无神秘可言。」
「继任的叫弗林特（Flint）？」
「是的。」
「他死得突然，是不是？」
「大概吧。」布莱克唐突地说。

===Original===
“Providence again,” said Fanks slowly; “then you got the whole of the business. You took over the notation and a rubber company, and it panned out. Well, after that you floated a tin mine or something﻿—there was a death there, wasn’t there?”
“I believe there was﻿—one of the directors; I forget his name.”
Fanks nodded.
“He could have stopped the flotation﻿—he was threatening to resign and expose some methods of yours.”
“He was a very headstrong man.”

===Chinese===
「又是老天爷帮忙。」法恩克斯慢悠悠地说。「于是整盘生意归了你。你接手了那宗牌价生意和一家橡胶公司，还干出了名堂。再往后，你又创设了一座锡矿还是什么——那儿也死过一个人，对不对？」
「好像是有——是个董事；名字我忘了。」
法恩克斯点头。
「他本可以搅黄那次招股——他当时正闹着要辞职，把你的某些手段抖出去。」
「那人固执得很。」

===Original===
“And he died.”
“Yes,”﻿—a pause﻿—“he died.”
Fanks looked at the man who sat opposite to him.
“Dr. Essley attended him.”
“I believe he did.”

===Chinese===
「然后他死了。」
「是的。」——顿了一顿——「他死了。」
法恩克斯看着坐在对面的人。
「给他看病的是埃斯利博士。」
「好像是。」

===Original===
“And he died.”
Black leant over the desk. “What do you mean?” he asked. “What are you suggesting about my friend, Dr. Essley?”
“Nothing, except that Providence has been of some assistance to you,” said Fanks. “The record of your success is a record of death﻿—you sent Essley to see me once.”
“You were ill.”
“I was,” said Fanks grimly, “and I was also troubling you a little.” He flicked the ash from his cigar to the carpet. “Black, I’m going to resign all my directorships on your companies.”

===Chinese===
「然后他死了。」
布莱克俯身向着写字台。「你什么意思？」他问。「你对我的朋友埃斯利博士含沙射影什么？」
「没什么，只不过老天爷确实帮过你一些忙。」法恩克斯说。「你的成功史就是一部死亡记录——你还打发埃斯利来看过我一回。」
「你当时病了。」
「我是病了，」法恩克斯冷冷地说，「而且我也有点碍你的事。」他把雪茄灰弹到地毯上。「布莱克，你在各公司给我安的董事位置，我打算一并辞掉。」

===Original===
The other man laughed unpleasantly.
“You can laugh, but it isn’t healthy, Black. I’ve no use for money that is bought at too heavy a price.”
“My dear man, you can resign,” said Colonel Black, “but might I ask if your extraordinary suspicions are shared by anybody else?”
Fanks shook his head.
“Not at present,” he said.

===Chinese===
对方笑了一声，笑得叫人不舒服。
「你尽管笑，可这不是闹着玩的，布莱克。价钱太高的钱，我要来无用。」
「亲爱的伙计，你辞好了，」布莱克上校说，「不过我能不能问一句，你这套稀奇的疑心，还有别人与你分享吗？」
法恩克斯摇头。
「眼下没有。」他说。

===Original===
They looked at one another for the space of half a minute, which was a very long time.
“I want to clear right out,” Fanks continued. “I reckon my holdings are worth £150,000﻿—you can buy them.”
“You amaze me,” said Black harshly.
He opened a drawer of his desk and took out a little green bottle and a feather. “Poor Essley,” he smiled, “wandering about Spain seeking the secrets of Moorish perfumery﻿—he would go off his head if he knew what you thought of him.”
“I’d sooner he went off his head than that I should go off the earth,” said Fanks stolidly. “What have you got there?”

===Chinese===
两人对视了半分钟之久——这可是很长很长的时间。
「我要干干净净地抽身，」法恩克斯接着说。「我估摸我手里的股份值 150,000 英镑——你可以买下。」
「你真让我吃惊。」布莱克声色俱厉地说。
他拉开写字台的抽屉，取出一只小绿瓶和一根羽毛。「可怜的埃斯利，」他微笑道，「正在西班牙四处游荡，寻摸摩尔人香水的秘方——他要是知道你这么看他，非发疯不可。」
「我宁可他发疯，也不愿我自己从这地球上消失。」法恩克斯木然道。「你手里拿的是什么？」

===Original===
Black unstoppered the bottle and dipped in the feather. He withdrew it and held it close to his nose.
“What is it?” asked Fanks curiously.
For answer, Black held up the feather for the man to smell.
“I can smell nothing,” said Fanks. Tilting the end quickly downwards. Black drew it across the lips of the other.
“Here﻿ ﻿…” cried Fanks, and went limply to the ground.
---

===Chinese===
布莱克拔开瓶塞，把羽毛探进去蘸了蘸，抽出来凑到鼻子底下。
「什么东西？」法恩克斯好奇地问。
作为回答，布莱克举起羽毛让那人闻。
「我什么也闻不见。」法恩克斯说。布莱克倏地把羽毛尖往下一倾，抹过对方的嘴唇。
「这儿……」法恩克斯叫了一声，瘫软在地。
---

===Original===
“Constable Fellowe!”
Frank Fellowe was leaving the charge-room when he heard the snappy tones of the desk-sergeant calling him.
“Yes, sergeant?” he said, with a note of inquiry in his voice. He knew that there was something unpleasant coming.
Sergeant Gurden seldom took any opportunity of speaking to him, except in admonishment. The sergeant was a wizen-faced man, with an ugly trick of showing his teeth when he was annoyed, and no greater contrast could be imagined than that which was afforded by the tall, straight-backed young man in the constable’s uniform, standing before the desk, and the shrunken figure that sat on the stool behind.
Sergeant Gurden had a dead-white face, which a scrubby black moustache went to emphasize. In spite of the fact that he was a man of good physical development, his clothing hung upon him awkwardly, and indeed the station-sergeant was awkward in more ways than one.

===Chinese===
「费洛警员！」
弗兰克·费洛正要走出值班室，听见值台巡佐利落的嗓音在叫他。
「什么事，巡佐？」他说，话音里带着询问。他知道没好事。
格登巡佐（Sergeant Gurden）难得有话同他说，除非是训话。这位巡佐生了一张干瘪脸，有个讨人嫌的习惯：一恼就龇牙。站在这张案桌前、穿着警员制服的挺拔高个青年，同桌后凳子上缩着的那个干瘪身影，其反差之大无以复加。
格登巡佐的脸白得没有血色，一撮稀疏的黑髭更衬得它扎眼。他体格其实不差，衣裳穿在身上却总不利落，而这位警局巡佐的别扭，也远不止在衣裳一桩上。

===Original===
Now he looked at Fellowe, showing his teeth.
“I have had another complaint about you,” he said, “and if this is repeated it will be a matter for the Commissioner.”
The constable nodded his head respectfully. “I am very sorry, sergeant,” he said, “but what is the complaint?”
“You know as well as I do,” snarled the other; “you have been annoying Colonel Black again.”
A faint smile passed across Fellowe’s lips. He knew something of the solicitude in which the sergeant held the colonel.

===Chinese===
此刻他龇着牙，看着费洛。
「又有人告你了，」他说，「再犯，就得报到总监那儿去了。」
警员恭恭敬敬地点头。「很抱歉，巡佐，」他说，「不过告的是什么呢？」
「你我心里都清楚。」对方咆哮道。「你又去搅扰布莱克上校了。」
一丝浅笑掠过费洛的唇边。巡佐对上校关怀备至，这一层他多少有所耳闻。

===Original===
“What the devil are you smiling at?” snapped the sergeant. “I warn you,” he went on, “that you are getting very impertinent, and this may be a matter for the Commissioner.”
“I had no intention of being disrespectful, sergeant,” said the young man. “I am as tired of these complaints as you are, but I have told you, as I will tell the Commissioner, that Colonel Black lives in a house in Serrington Gardens and is a source of some interest to me﻿—that is my excuse.”
“He complains that you are always watching the house,” said the sergeant, and Constable Fellowe smiled.
“That is his conscience working,” he said. “Seriously, sergeant, I happen to know that the colonel is not too friendly disposed﻿—”
He stopped himself.

===Chinese===
「你笑个什么鬼？」巡佐厉声道。「我警告你，」他接着说，「你这放肆劲儿越来越足了，这事说不定要闹到总监那儿。」
「我没有不敬的意思，巡佐，」青年人说。「这些投诉，我和您一样腻烦。可我同您说过，日后也要同总监这么说：布莱克上校住在塞灵顿花园（Serrington Gardens）的一所宅子里，而此人对我不无引人寻味之处——这就是我的理由。」
「他抱怨你老盯着那宅子看。」巡佐说。费洛警员笑了。
「那是他良心在发作。」他说。「说正经的，巡佐，我恰好知道，这位上校的心思不大干净——」
他把话收住了。
===Original===
“Well?” demanded the sergeant.
“Well,” repeated Constable Fellowe, “it might be as well perhaps if I kept my thoughts to myself.”
The sergeant nodded grimly.
“If you get into trouble you will only have yourself to blame,” he warned. “Colonel Black is an influential man. He is a ratepayer. Don’t forget that, constable. The ratepayers pay your salary, find the coat for your back, feed you﻿—you owe everything to the ratepayers.”
“On the other hand,” said the young man, “Colonel Black is a ratepayer who owes me something.”

===Chinese===
「不干净什么？」巡佐追问。
「不干净，」费洛警员重复道，「我看还是把我的想法搁在自己肚子里为好。」
巡佐狠狠点头。
「你要惹了麻烦，只能怪你自己。」他告诫道。「布莱克上校是个有影响的人物。他是纳税人。别忘了这个，警员。纳税人给你发饷，给你置办身上的制服，养活你——你的一切都是纳税人给的。」
「反过来讲，」青年人说，「布莱克上校这个纳税人，倒欠着我点什么。」

===Original===
Hitching his cape over his arm, he passed from the charge-room down the stone steps into the street without.
The man on duty at the door bade him a cheery farewell.
---
Fellowe was an annoying young man, more annoying by reason of the important fact that his antecedents were quite unknown to his most intimate friends. He was a man of more than ordinary education, quiet, restrained, his voice gently modulated; he had all the manners and attributes of a gentleman.
He had a tiny little house in Somers Town where he lived alone, but no friend of his, calling casually, had ever the good fortune to find him at home when he was off duty. It was believed he had other interests.

===Chinese===
他把披肩往臂上一搭，走出值班室，下了石阶，来到街上。
门口当值的警员同他快快活活地道了别。
---
费洛是个招人烦的青年，更招人烦的是这样一件要紧事：他最知己的朋友，也全然不知他的来历。他受过高于常人的教育，安静，克制，嗓音温润有节；言谈举止，一身的绅士做派。
他在萨默斯镇有一所小小的房子，独自居住。可无论哪位朋友顺路来访，都不曾有好运气在他歇班时撞见他在家。据说他另有营生。

===Original===
What those interests were could be guessed when, with exasperating unexpectedness, he appeared in the amateur boxing championship and carried off the police prize, for Fellowe was a magnificent boxer﻿—hard-hitting, quick, reliable, scientific.
The bad men of Somers Town were the first to discover this, and one, Grueler, who on one never-to-be-forgotten occasion had shown fight on the way to the station, testified before breathless audiences as to the skill and science of the young man.
His breezy independence had won for him many friends, but it had made him enemies too, and as he walked thoughtfully along the street leading from the station, he realized that in the sergeant he had an enemy of more than average malignity.
Why should this be? It puzzled him. After all, he was only doing his duty. That he was also exceeding his duty did not strike him as being sufficient justification for the resentment of his superior, for he had reached the enthusiastic age of life where only inaction was unpardonable. As to Black, Frank shrugged his shoulders. He could not understand it. He was not of a nature to suspect that the sergeant had any other motive than the perfectly natural desire which all blasé superiors have, to check their too impulsive subordinates.
Frank admitted to himself that he was indeed a most annoying person, and in many ways he understood the sergeant’s antagonism to himself. Dismissing the matter from his mind, he made his way to his tiny house in Croome Street and let himself into his small dining-room.

===Chinese===
那营生是什么，等他惹人恼火地冷不丁出现在业余拳击锦标赛上、捧走警察组的奖杯时，人们才猜出几分——费洛是个出色的拳手：出手重，反应快，靠得住，有章法。
萨默斯镇的亡命徒们头一个领教了这一点。其中有个叫格鲁勒（Grueler）的，在一个永难忘怀的场合，在押去警局的路上奋起反抗过一回，此后便向屏息凝神的听众们连说带比划，作证这位年轻人的拳技何等精妙、何等科学。
他那股子爽利的独立劲儿为他赢得许多朋友，也结下不少仇家。此刻他一路沉思着从警局走回来，意识到在巡佐身上，他结下了一个怨毒超出常格的仇敌。
何至于此呢？他纳闷。说到底，他不过是尽职而已。要说他还越了职，那在他看来也远不足以成为上司记恨的理由，因为他正到了人生里那个血热的年纪——在那年纪看来，唯有袖手旁观才是不可饶恕的。至于布莱克，弗兰克耸耸肩，他实在想不通。他天生不是那种心思，会疑心巡佐除了上峰管束毛躁部属的那点天经地义的念头之外，还别有用心。
弗兰克自己也承认，他确实是个顶顶招人烦的人，在许多方面，他理解巡佐为何同他作对。他把这事从脑子里撵出去，走向自己在克鲁姆街（Croome Street）的小屋，开门进了那间小小的餐室。

===Original===
The walls were distempered, and the few articles of furniture that were within were such as are not usually met with in houses of this quality. The old print above the mantelpiece must have been worth a workingman’s annual income. The small gate-legged table in the centre of the felt-covered floor was indubitably Jacobean, and the chairs were Sheraton, as also was the sideboard.
Though the periods may not have harmonized, there is harmony enough in great age. A bright fire was burning in the grate, for the night was bitterly cold. Fellowe stopped before the mantelpiece to examine two letters which stood awaiting him, replaced them from where he had taken them, and passed through the folding doors of the room into a tiny bedroom.
He had an accommodating landlord. Property owners in Somers Town, and especially the owners of small cottages standing on fairly valuable ground, do not as a rule make such renovations as Fellowe required. The average landlord, for instance, would not have built the spacious bathroom which the cottage boasted, but then Fellowe’s landlord was no ordinary man.
The young man bathed, changed himself into civilian clothing, made himself a cup of tea, and, slipping into a long overcoat which reached to his heels, left the house half an hour after he had entered.
Frank Fellowe made his way West. He found a taxicab at King’s Cross and gave an address in Piccadilly. Before he had reached that historic thoroughfare he tapped at the window-glass and ordered the cabman to drop him.

===Chinese===
墙壁刷着粉浆，屋里寥寥几件家具，却不是这种档次的房子里通常见得到的。壁炉架上方那幅旧版画，抵得上一个工人全年的收入。毡毯铺地的房间正中那张小折叠桌，确凿无疑是詹姆斯一世时期的古董，椅子是谢拉顿式，餐边柜也是。
年代虽或不甚相配，古物自有古物的和谐。炉膛里火烧得正旺，因为夜里冷得砭骨。费洛在壁炉架前站住，看了看立在那儿等他的两封信，又原样放回，然后穿过房间的折门，走进一间小小的卧室。
他的房东通情达理。萨默斯镇的房产主，尤其是占着挺值钱的地皮的小屋业主，照例不肯做费洛要求的那些改造。比方说，寻常房东断然不会造出这小屋引以为豪的那间宽敞浴室，不过话又说回来，费洛的房东本就不是寻常人。
青年人洗了澡，换上便装，给自己沏了杯茶，然后披上一件垂到脚跟的长大衣，进屋半小时后又出了门。
弗兰克·费洛朝城西去了。他在国王十字（King's Cross）寻到一辆出租车，报了个皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）的地址。车还没到那条历史有名的大街，他就敲敲车窗，叫司机放他下来。

===Original===
At eleven o’clock that night Sergeant Gurden, relieved from his duty, left the station-house. Though outwardly taciturn and calm, he was boiling internally with wrath.
His antipathy to Fellowe was a natural one, but it had become intensified during the past few weeks by the attitude which the young man had taken up towards the sergeant’s protégé.
Gurden was as much of a mystery to the men in his division as Fellowe, and even more so, because the secrecy which surrounded Gurden’s life had a more sinister import than the reservation of the younger man.
Gurden was cursed with an ambition. He had hoped at the outset of his career to have secured distinction in the force, but a lack of education, coupled with an address which was apt to be uncouth and brusque, had militated against his enthusiasm.
He had recognized the limitations placed upon his powers by the authorities over him. He had long since come to realize that hope of promotion, first to an inspectorship, and eventually to that bright star which lures every policeman onward, and which is equivalent to the baton popularly supposed to be in every soldier’s knapsack, a superintendentship, was not for him.

===Chinese===
当夜十一点，格登巡佐交了班，离开警局。他外表沉默平静，肚子里却怒火翻滚。
他对费洛的反感本是天生的，可最近几个星期，因为这青年对巡佐那位被保护人所持的态度，这反感又陡然加了几分。
在分区同僚眼里，格登同费洛一样是个谜，甚至更谜——因为裹在格登生活外面的那层隐秘，比年轻人的深藏不露，来意更要凶险。
格登被野心诅咒着。入行之初，他本指望在警界挣得功名，可惜学问底子薄，谈吐又常常粗鲁生硬，这份雄心便处处受挫。
他认了命，承认上头给他划定了界线。他早就明白，晋升之路——先升督察，再升那颗引领每个警察奋然向前的亮星，那地位相当于传说中每个士兵背囊里都装着的元帅权棒，也就是警司——与他无缘。

===Original===
Thwarted ambition had to find a new outlet, and he concentrated his attention upon acquiring money. It became a passion for him, an obsession. His parsimony, his meanness, and his insatiable greed were bywords throughout the Metropolitan police force.
It had become a mania with him, this collecting of money, and his bitterest enmity was reserved for those who placed the slightest obstacle between the officer and the gratification of his ambitions.
It must be said of Colonel Black that he had been most kind. Cupidity takes a lenient view of its benefactor’s morals, and though Sergeant Gurden was not the kind of man willingly to help the lawless, no person could say that an outside broker, undetected of fraud, was anything but a desirable member of society.
Black had made an appointment with him. He was on his way now to keep it. The colonel lived in one of those onetime fashionable squares in Camden Town. He was obviously well off, ran a car of his own, and had furnished No. 60 Serrington Gardens, with something like lavish comfort.
The sergeant had no time to change. There was no necessity, he told himself, for his relations with Black were of such a character that there was no need to stand on ceremony.

===Chinese===
受挫的野心总要找新的出路，于是他把全副心思聚敛到搞钱上。这成了他的热望，他的魔怔。他的吝啬、小气与填不满的贪心，在整个伦敦警察厅里人人当作口头禅。
攒钱这件事成了癖，谁在这名警官同他野心的满足之间设下最细小的障碍，谁就领受他最深的仇怨。
说到布莱克上校，必须承认他待人格外周到。贪财的人看恩主的品行，向来宽厚。虽说格登巡佐并非甘愿为非作歹之徒，可谁也不能说，一个欺诈罪行尚未败露的场外经纪人，不是社会中叫人满意的一员。
布莱克同他约好了时间，此刻他正赴约去。上校住在卡姆登镇（Camden Town）一处旧日曾经时髦的广场宅子里。他显然家道殷实，自备汽车，把塞灵顿花园 60 号那所宅子布置得近乎奢华舒适。
巡佐来不及换衣裳。也犯不着换，他对自己说，他同布莱克的关系，本就到了不必拘礼的地步。

===Original===
The square was deserted at this time of night, and the sergeant made his way to the kitchen entrance in the basement and rang the bell. The door was opened almost instantly by a manservant.
“Is that you, sergeant?” said a voice from the darkness, as Gurden made his way upstairs to the unlighted hall above.
Colonel Black turned on the light.
He held out a long muscular hand in welcome to the police officer. “I am so glad you have come,” he said.
The sergeant took the hand and shook it warmly.

===Chinese===
这个钟点的广场上空无一人。巡佐走到地下层的厨房入口，按了铃。门几乎立刻开了，开门的是个男仆。
「是您吗，巡佐？」黑暗里传来一个声音。格登上楼，走进楼上那间没点灯的门厅。
布莱克上校拧亮了灯。
他伸出一只瘦长有力的手欢迎这位警官。「您能来，我太高兴了。」他说。
巡佐握住那只手，热情地摇了摇。

===Original===
“I have come to apologize to you. Colonel Black,” he said. “I have severely reprimanded Police-Constable Fellowe.”
Black waved his hand deprecatingly.
“I do not wish to get any member of your admirable force into trouble,” he said, “but really this man’s prying into my business is inexcusable and humiliating.”
The sergeant nodded. “I can well understand your annoyance, sir,” he said, “but you will understand that these young constables are always a little overzealous, and when a man is that way he is inclined to overdo it a little.”
He spoke almost pleadingly in his desire to remove any bad impression that might exist in Black’s mind as to his own part in Police-Constable Fellowe’s investigations.

===Chinese===
「我是来向您道歉的，布莱克上校。」他说。「我已经狠狠训斥了费洛警员一顿。」
布莱克摆摆手，示意不敢当。
「我并不想让贵方任何一位可敬的警员惹上麻烦，」他说，「可这个人如此刺探我的事务，实在不可原谅，也叫人难堪。」
巡佐点头。「您的恼火我完全理解，先生，」他说，「不过您也知道，这些年轻警员总是过分热心，人一到了那个劲儿上，难免就做过一点头。」
他几乎是在恳求着说话，一心要抹掉布莱克心里可能存着的疑影：在费洛警员的调查里，他自己是不是也有一份。

===Original===
Black favoured him with a gracious bow.
“Please do not think of it, I beg of you,” he said. “I am perfectly sure that the young constable did not intend willingly to hurt my amour-propre.”
He led the way to a spacious dining-room situated at the back of the house. Whisky and cigars were on the table. “Help yourself, sergeant,” said Colonel Black. He pushed a big comfortable chair forward.
With a murmured word of thanks, the sergeant sank into its luxurious depths.
“I am due back at the station in half an hour,” he said, “if you will excuse me then.”

===Chinese===
布莱克赏了他一个优雅的躬。
「请千万不要放在心上，我求您了。」他说。「我完全相信，那位年轻警员并非有意伤害我的自尊。」
他在前头引路，来到房屋后部一间宽敞的餐室。桌上摆着威士忌和雪茄。「请自便，巡佐。」布莱克上校说。他把一把宽大的安乐椅推上前来。
巡佐低声道了谢，一屁股陷进那把椅子的软深处。
「半小时后我得回警局，」他说，「到时候请您放我走。」

===Original===
Black nodded.
“We shall be able to do our business in that time,” he said, “but before we go any further, let me thank you for what you have already done.”
From the inside pocket of his coat he took a flat pocketbook, opened it and extracted two banknotes. He laid them on the table at the sergeant’s elbow. The sergeant protested feebly, but his eyes twinkled at the sight of the crinkling paper.
“I don’t think I have done anything to deserve this,” he muttered.
Colonel Black smiled, and his big cigar tilted happily.

===Chinese===
布莱克点头。
「咱们的买卖，这点工夫尽够了。」他说，「不过在往下谈之前，请先让我为您已经做的事道谢。」
他从大衣内袋里掏出一本扁平的皮夹，打开，抽出两张钞票，搁在巡佐手边的桌上。巡佐虚让了几句，可一见那窸窣作响的纸票子，眼睛就亮了。
「我不觉得我做了什么配得这个的。」他嘟囔道。
布莱克上校微微一笑，嘴里的粗雪茄快活地翘了起来。

===Original===
“I pay well for little services, sergeant,” he said. “I have many enemies﻿—men who will misrepresent my motives﻿—and it is essential that I should be forewarned.”
He strode up and down the apartment thoughtfully, his hands thrust into his trousers pockets.
“It is a hard country, England,” he said, “for men who have had the misfortune to dabble in finance.”
Sergeant Gurden murmured sympathetically.
“In our business, sergeant,” the aggrieved colonel went on, “it frequently happens that disappointed people﻿—people who have not made the profits which they anticipated﻿—bring extraordinary accusations against those responsible for the conduct of those concerns in which their money is invested. I had a letter today”﻿—he shrugged his shoulders﻿—“accusing me﻿—me!﻿—of running a bucket-shop.”

===Chinese===
「小劳小酬，我向来出手大方，巡佐。」他说。「我的仇家多——尽是些会歪曲我用心的人——凡事预先得个信儿，对我是最要紧的。」
他双手插在裤袋里，若有所思地在屋里踱来踱去。
「英格兰这地方，」他说，「对不幸沾了金融一行的人来说，可真够严酷。」
格登巡佐同情地咕哝了两句。
「干咱们这行的，巡佐，」这位满腹委屈的上校接着说，「常有这样的事：失望的人——没赚到预期利润的人——对那些替他们打理投资事务的人提出离奇的指控。今天我就收到一封信」——他耸耸肩——「指控我——我！——开的是黑经纪行。」

===Original===
The sergeant nodded; he could well understand that aspect of speculation.
“And one has friends,” Black went on, striding up and down the apartment, “one has people one wants to protect against similar annoyances﻿—take my friend Dr. Essley﻿—Essley, *E* double *s* *l* *e* *y*,” he spelt the name carefully; “you have heard of him?”
The sergeant had not heard of any such body, but was willing to admit that he had.
“There is a man,” said the colonel, “a man absolutely at the head of his profession﻿—I shouldn’t be surprised to learn that even he is no safer from the voice of slander.”
The sergeant thought it very likely, and murmured to the effect.

===Chinese===
巡佐点头；投机的这一面他太能理解了。
「人总有朋友，」布莱克接着说，一面在屋里踱来踱去，「总有想护着免受同类烦扰的人——就拿我朋友埃斯利博士来说——埃斯利，E，两个 s，l，e，y。」他把这名字一个字母一个字母念得仔仔细细。「您听说过吧？」
巡佐从没听说过这么一号人，却乐于承认听说过。
「有这么一个人，」上校说，「一个在本行里绝对站上头一把交椅的人——若说连他也免不了遭人谗谤，我一点不吃惊。」
巡佐觉得极有可能，便咕哝着应承了这个意思。

===Original===
“There is always a possibility that malignity will attach itself to the famous,” the colonel continued, “and because I know that you would be one of the first to hear such slander, and that you would moreover afford me an opportunity﻿—a private opportunity﻿—of combating such slander, that I feel such security. God bless you, sergeant!” He patted the other’s shoulder, and Gurden was genuinely affected.
“I can quite understand your position, sir,” he said, “and you may be sure that when it is possible to render you any assistance I shall be most happy and proud to render it.”
Again Colonel Black favoured his visitor with a little pat.
“Or to Dr. Essley,” he said; “remember the name. Now, sergeant,” he went on, “I sent for you tonight,”﻿—he shrugged his shoulders﻿—“when I say sent for you, that, of course, is an exaggeration. How can a humble citizen like myself command the services of an officer of the police?”
