Sergeant Gurden fingered his moustache self-consciously.

===Chinese===
「名人是永远躲不开恶毒的，」上校接着说，「正因为我晓得，这样的谗言您必然是头一批听见的人，而且您还会给我一个机会——一个私下里辩白的机会——我才这样安心。上帝保佑您，巡佐！」他拍拍对方的肩，格登当真心头一热。
「您的处境我完全明白，先生，」他说，「请您放心，但凡有机会效劳，我必尽心尽力，且引以为荣。」
布莱克上校又拍了拍他的客人。
「也给埃斯利博士留个心，」他说，「记住这个名字。好了，巡佐，」他接着说，「今晚是我派人请您来的」——他耸耸肩——「说是『派人请』，当然是夸张了。像我这样一介草民，怎么使得动一位警官大驾呢？」
格登巡佐不自然地捻着髭须。

===Original===
“It is rather,” the colonel went on, “that I take advantage of your inestimable friendship to seek your advice.”
He stopped in his walk, drew a chair opposite to where the sergeant was sitting, and seated himself.
“Constable Fellowe, the man of whom I have complained, had the good fortune to render a service to the daughter of Mr. Theodore Sandford﻿—I see you know the gentleman.”
The sergeant nodded; he had heard of Mr. Theodore Sandford, as who had not? For Theodore Sandford was a millionaire ironmaster who had built a veritable palace at Hampstead, had purchased the Dennington “Velasquez,” and had presented it to the nation.
“Your constable,” continued Colonel Black, “sprang upon a motorcar Miss Sandford was driving down a steep hill, the brakes of which had gone wrong, and at some risk to himself guided the car through the traffic when, not to put too fine a point on it, Miss Sandford had lost her head.”

===Chinese===
「不如说，」上校接着说，「是借重您这份千金难买的友谊，来讨您的高见。」
他停下踱步，在巡佐对面拉过一把椅子坐下。
「费洛警员，就是我告状的那位，有幸为西奥多·桑德福德先生（Theodore Sandford）的千金效过一次劳——我看您认识这位先生。」
巡佐点头；西奥多·桑德福德先生他当然听说过——谁没听说过？西奥多·桑德福德是位铁业巨子，在汉普斯特德盖了一座货真价实的宫殿，买下了丹宁顿家藏的那幅「委拉斯开兹」，还献给了国家。
「贵局那位警员，」布莱克上校接着说，「当时扑向桑德福德小姐（Miss Sandford）正开下一道陡坡的汽车——车闸出了毛病——冒着相当的风险，把车从车流里引了出来；说得直白些，桑德福德小姐当时已经慌了神。」

===Original===
“Oh, it was him, was it?” said the sergeant disparagingly.
“It was him,” agreed the colonel out of sheer politeness. “Now these young people have met unknown to the father of Miss Sandford, and﻿—well, you understand.”
The sergeant did not understand, but said nothing.
“I do not suggest,” said the colonel, “that there is anything wrong﻿—but a policeman, sergeant, not even an officer like yourself﻿—a policeman!”
Deplorable! said the sergeant’s head, eyes and hands.

===Chinese===
「哦，原来是他干的，是吗？」巡佐不以为然地说。
「就是他。」上校纯出于礼貌地附和。「如今这两个年轻人背着桑德福德小姐的父亲会面，这个——嗯，您明白的。」
巡佐并不明白，可什么也没说。
「我并不是说，」上校说，「这里头有什么不妥——可一个警察，巡佐，哪怕不是您这样的长官——毕竟是个警察！」
「糟透了！」巡佐的脑袋、眼睛和手都这么说。

===Original===
“For some extraordinary reason which I cannot fathom,” the colonel proceeded, “Mr. Sandford tolerates the visits of this young man; that, I fear, is a matter which we cannot go into, but I should like you﻿—well, I should like you to use your influence with Fellowe.”
Sergeant Gurden rose to depart. He had no influence, but some power. He understood a little of what the other man was driving at, the more so when﻿—
“If this young man gets into trouble, I should like to know,” said Colonel Black, holding out his firm hand; “I should like to know very much indeed.”
“He is a rare pushful fellow, that Fellowe,” said the sergeant severely. “He gets to know the upper classes in some way that I can’t understand, and I dare say he has wormed himself into their confidence. I always say that the kitchen is the place for the policeman, and when I see a constable in the drawing-room I begin to suspect things. There is a great deal of corruption﻿—” He stopped, suddenly realizing that he himself was in a drawing-room, and that corruption was an ugly and an incongruous word.
Colonel Black accompanied him to the door.

===Chinese===
「出于某种我参不透的奇特缘由，」上校继续说，「桑德福德先生竟容得这年轻人登门；那个嘛，恕我们不便深究。不过我希望您——嗯，希望您对费洛施展一下您的影响。」
格登巡佐起身告辞。影响他谈不上，权力倒有一些。对方打的什么算盘，他约略懂了些，尤其当——
「这年轻人若惹上麻烦，我愿意知道，」布莱克上校说着，伸出发硬的手掌，「我实在非常非常愿意知道。」
「那个费洛，钻营的劲儿可真够大。」巡佐严厉地说。「他怎么结识上上等人的，我闹不明白，我敢说他早就变着法儿讨了人家的信任。我一贯讲，警察的立足之地在厨房；一看见警员待在客厅里，我就要犯疑心。这里头的腐化可不少——」他猛地收住话头，忽然想到自己此刻正坐在一间客厅里，而「腐化」这个词，又难听又不合时宜。
布莱克上校送他到门口。

===Original===
“You understand, sergeant,” he said, “that this man﻿—Fellowe, did you call him?﻿—may make a report over your head or behind your back. I want you to take great care that such a report, if it is made, shall come to me. I do not want to be taken by surprise. If there is any charge to answer I want to know all about it in advance. It will make the answering ever so much easier, as I am a busy man.”
He shook hands with the sergeant and saw him out of the house.
Sergeant Gurden went back to the station with a brisk step and a comforting knowledge that the evening had been well spent.

===Chinese===
「您明白的，巡佐，」他说，「这个人——您管他叫费洛来着？——没准会越过您或在您背后递报告。我要请您格外留神，这样的报告若真写了出来，务必到我手里。我不想被人打个冷不防。若有什么罪名要应答，我要预先知道全部情形。我是个忙人，这样应答起来就省事多了。」
他同巡佐握手，送他出了宅门。
格登巡佐迈着轻快的步子回警局去，心里暖洋洋的，确信这个晚上过得值。

## VIII Colonel Black Has Shock / 八 布莱克上校受惊

===Original===
Mr. Sandford had an appointment with Colonel Black. It was the final interview before the break.
The City was busy with rumours. A whisper had circulated; all was not well with the financier﻿—the amalgamation on which so much depended had not gone through.
Black sat at his desk that afternoon, idly twiddling a paper-knife. He was more sallow than usual; the hand that held the knife twitched nervously.
He looked at his watch. It was time Sandford came. He pushed a bell by the side of his desk and a clerk appeared.
“Has Mr. Sandford arrived?” he asked.

===Chinese===
桑德福德先生（Mr. Sandford）同布莱克上校有个约会。这是决裂前的最后一次会谈。
伦敦城里流言纷起。一句耳语四下传开：那位金融家境况不妙——多少大事系于其上的合并，黄了。
那天下午，布莱克坐在写字台前，闲闲地捻着一把裁纸刀。他脸色比平日更黄；握刀的手神经质地抽动。
他看看表。桑德福德该到了。他按了按桌边的铃，一个职员进来。
「桑德福德先生到了吗？」他问。

===Original===
“He has just come, sir,” said the man.
“Show him in.”
The two men exchanged formal greetings, and Black pointed to a chair.
“Sit down, Sandford,” he said curtly. “Now, exactly how do we stand?”
“Where we did,” said the other uncompromisingly.

===Chinese===
「刚到，先生。」那人说。
「带他进来。」
两人客套地寒暄了一番，布莱克指了指椅子。
「坐，桑德福德。」他生硬地说。「现在，咱们到底谈到了什么地步？」
「原地未动。」对方寸步不让。

===Original===
“You will not come into my scheme?”
“I will not,” said the other.
Colonel Black tapped the desk with his knife, and Sandford looked at him. He seemed older than when he had last seen him. His yellow face was seamed and lined.
“It means ruin for me,” he said suddenly. “I have more creditors than I can count. If the amalgamation went through I should be established. There are lots of people in with me too﻿—Ikey Tramber﻿—you know Sir Isaac? He’s a friend of﻿—er﻿—the Earl of Verlond.”
But the elder man was not impressed.

===Chinese===
「你不加入我的方案？」
「不加。」对方说。
布莱克上校用刀尖敲着桌面，桑德福德望着他。他比上次见面见老了许多。那张黄脸布满沟壑皱纹。
「这就把我毁了。」他突然说。「我的债主多得数不清。合并要是成了，我也就站稳了。跟着我入股的人也有一大批——艾基·特兰伯（Ikey Tramber）——你认识艾萨克爵士吧？他是——呃——弗隆德勋爵的朋友。」
可这位长者不为所动。

===Original===
“It is your fault if you’re in a hole,” said he. “You have taken on too big a job﻿—more than that, you have taken too much for granted.”
The man at the desk looked up from under his straight brows.
“It is all very well for you to sit there and tell me what I should do,” he said, and the shakiness of his voice told the other something of the passion he concealed. “I do not want advice or homily﻿—I want money. Come into my scheme and amalgamate, or﻿—”
“Or﻿—” repeated the ironmaster quietly.
“I do not threaten you,” said Black sullenly; “I warn you. You are risking more than you know.”

===Chinese===
「你陷了坑，那是你自作自受。」他说。「你揽下的摊子太大——还不止这个，你把太多事想当然了。」
桌后那人从平直的眉毛底下抬起眼来。
「你坐在那儿教训我该怎么办，说得倒轻巧。」他说。话音里的颤抖，让对方窥见了他强压着的激愤。「我不要忠告，也不要说教——我要的是钱。加入我的方案，合并，不然——」
「不然——」铁业巨头平静地重复道。
「我不是威胁你，」布莱克阴沉地说，「我是提醒你。你冒的险，比你知道的大。」

===Original===
“I’ll take the risk,” said Sandford. He got up on to his feet. “Have you anything more to say?”
“Nothing.”
“Then I’ll bid you goodbye.”
The door closed with a slam behind him, and Black did not move. He sat there until it was dark, doing no more than scribble aimlessly upon his blotting-pad.

===Chinese===
「这险我冒了。」桑德福德说。他站起身。「你还有话要说吗？」
「没有了。」
「那就别过了。」
门在他身后砰地关上，布莱克没有动。他一直坐到天黑，做的无非是在吸墨纸垫上胡乱涂画。

===Original===
It was nearly dark when he drove back to the flat he occupied in Victoria Street and let himself in.
“There is a gentleman waiting to see you, sir,” said the man who came hurrying to help him out of his coat.
“What sort of a man?”
“I don’t know exactly, sir, but I have got a feeling that he is a detective.”
“A detective?” He found his hands trembling, and cursed his folly. He stood uncertainly in the centre of the hall. In a minute he had mastered his fears and turned the handle of the door.

===Chinese===
天快黑透时，他乘车回到自己在维多利亚街的公寓，开门进去。
「有位先生等着见您，先生。」迎上来替他脱大衣的男仆说。
「什么样的人？」
「我说不准，先生，可我总觉得，他是个侦探。」
「侦探？」他发觉自己的手在抖，暗骂自己糊涂。他站在门厅中央，一时拿不定主意。不过一分钟，他便压住了恐惧，拧动了门的把手。

===Original===
A man rose to meet him.
He had a feeling that he had met him before. It was one of those impressions that it is so difficult to explain.
“You wanted to see me?” he asked.
“Yes, sir,” said the man, a note of deference in his voice. “I have called to make a few inquiries.”
It was on the tip of Black’s tongue to ask him whether he was a police officer, but somehow he had not the courage to frame the words.

===Chinese===
一个男人起身迎他。
他觉得从前见过这人。那种印象，最难说清。
「你要见我？」他问。
「是的，先生。」那人话音里带着恭敬。「我来是为几件事查问查问。」
布莱克话到嘴边，想问他是不是警察，可不知怎么的，竟没胆量把话凑成句。

===Original===
The effort was unnecessary, as it proved, for the next words of the man explained his errand.
“I have been engaged,” he said, “by a firm of solicitors to discover the whereabouts of Dr. Essley.”
Black looked hard at him. “There ought to be no difficulty,” he said, “in that. The doctor’s name is in the Directory.”
“That is so,” said the man, “and yet I have had the greatest difficulty in running him to earth. As a matter of fact,” explained the man, “I was wrong when I said I wanted to discover his whereabouts. It is his identity I wish to establish.”

===Chinese===
事实证明，这份力气省了——那人的下一句话，已说明了他的来意。
「我受一家律师行委托，」他说，「寻访埃斯利博士的下落。」
布莱克死死盯着他。「那不该有什么难处。」他说。「博士的名字，名人地址录里就写着。」
「话是这么说，」那人道，「可我把他就地寻获，费了老大的劲。说实在的，」那人解释道，「我说想查明他的下落，这话不对。我要查明的，是他的身份。」

===Original===
“I do not follow you,” said the financier.
“Well,” said the man, “I don’t know exactly how to put it. If you know Dr. Essley, you will recall the fact that he was for some years in Australia.”
“That is true,” said Black. “He and I came back together.”
“And you were there some years, sir?”
“Yes, we were there for a number of years, though we were not together all the time.”

===Chinese===
「我没听懂。」金融家说。
「是这样，」那人说，「我不大知道该怎么讲。您要是认识埃斯利博士，就还记得他在澳大利亚待过几年。」
「那是真的。」布莱克说。「他同我是一起回来的。」
「您在那儿也待了好些年，先生？」
「是的，我们待了不少年，不过并不是一直在一起。」

===Original===
“I see,” said the man. “You went out together, I believe?”
“No,” replied the other sharply, “we went at different periods.”
“Have you seen him recently?”
“No, I have not seen him, although I have frequently written to him on various matters.” Black was trying hard not to lose his patience. It would not do for this man to see how much the questions were irritating him.
The man jotted down something in his notebook, closed it and put it in his pocket.

===Chinese===
「明白了。」那人说。「你们是一起出去的，我记得没错吧？」
「不，」对方尖锐地答道，「我们去的不是同一个时期。」
「近来见过他吗？」
「没有，没见过，虽说为各种事我常给他写信。」布莱克竭力按着性子。这些问话叫他有多恼，可千万不能让这人看出来。
那人在小本子上记了点什么，合上，揣进兜里。

===Original===
“Would you be surprised to learn,” he asked quietly, “that the real Dr. Essley who went out to Australia died there?”
Black’s fingers caught the edge of the table and he steadied himself.
“I did not know that,” he said. “Is that all you have to ask?” he said, as the man finished.
“I think that will do, sir,” said the detective.
“Can I ask you on whose behalf you are inquiring?” demanded the colonel.

===Chinese===
「如果我告诉您，」他不动声色地问，「当年去澳大利亚的那位真埃斯利博士，死在了那儿，您会吃惊吗？」
布莱克的手指抠住桌沿，稳住了自己。
「这我不知道。」他说。「要问的就这些？」那人问完，他又问道。
「我看问妥了，先生。」侦探说。
「能不能请问，您是替谁查问的？」上校诘问。

===Original===
“That I am not at liberty to tell.”
After he had gone, Black paced the apartment, deep in thought.
He took down from the shelf a continental Baedeker and worked out with a pencil and paper a line of retirement. The refusal of Sandford to negotiate with him was the crowning calamity.
He crossed the room to the safe which stood in the corner, and opened it. In the inside drawer were three flat packets of notes. He picked them out and laid them on the table. They were notes on the Bank of France, each for a thousand francs.
It would be well to take no risks. He put them in the inside pocket of his coat. If all things failed, they were the way to freedom.

===Chinese===
「这个，我不便奉告。」
人走后，布莱克在屋里踱来踱去，陷入沉思。
他从架上取下一本欧洲大陆的贝德克尔旅行指南，用铅笔和纸盘算出一条退路。桑德福德拒不再谈，是压顶的祸事。
他走到屋角的保险柜前，打开。里面的抽屉里躺着三叠扁扁的钞票。他取出来，摊在桌上。那是法兰西银行的钞票，一张一千法郎。
还是小心不为过。他把它们塞进大衣内袋。万般皆休的时候，它们就是通往自由的路。

===Original===
As for Essley﻿—he smiled. He must go anyway.
He left his flat and drove eastwards to the City.
Two men followed him, though this he did not know.
---

===Chinese===
至于埃斯利——他微微一笑。横竖都得去。
他离开公寓，驱车向东，往伦敦城去。
有两个人跟着他，只是他不知道。
---

===Original===
Black boasted that his corporation kept no books, maintained no record, and this fact was emphasized the night that the Four had visited him unbidden. Their systematic search for evidence, which they had intended to use against him at a recognized tribunal, had failed to disclose the slightest vestige of documentary evidence which might be employed.
Yet, if the truth be told, Black kept a very complete set of books, only they were in a code of his own devising, the key of which he had never put on paper, and which he only could understand.
He was engaged on the evening of the detective’s visit in placing even these ledgers beyond the reach of the Four. He had good reason for his uneasiness.
The Four had been very active of late, and they had thought fit to issue another challenge to Colonel Black.
He was busy from nine o’clock to eleven, tearing up apparently innocent letters and burning them. When that hour struck, he looked at his watch and confirmed the time. He had very important business that night.
He wrote a note to Sir Isaac Tramber, asking him to meet him that night. He had need of every friend, every pull, and every bit of help that could come to him.

===Chinese===
布莱克常吹嘘，他的公司不记账、不留档，四义士那晚不请自来之后，这一点更是坐实了。他们有章法地搜找证据，本想拿到一个正式的法庭上用来对付他，结果连一星半点用得上的书面证据也没能翻出来。
然而说句实话，布莱克记着一套极齐全的账，只不过用的是他自编的密码，钥匙从不落在纸上，只有他一个人看得懂。
侦探来访的那个晚上，他忙着把这些账册也挪到四义士够不着的地方。他的不安，事出有因。
四义士近来活动频繁，他们认为有必要再向布莱克上校下一次战书。
从九点到十一点，他忙着把那些看似无害的信撕掉烧净。钟点一到，他看看表，核准了时间。那晚他有一桩顶要紧的买卖。
他给艾萨克·特兰伯爵士写了张短笺，约他当晚会面。每一个朋友、每一份人情、每一点能落到他头上的助力，他都用得着。

## XVI Colonel Black Meets a Just Man / 十六 布莱克上校会见一位义士

===Original===
Dr. Essley’s house at Forest Hill stood untenanted. The red lamp before the door was unlit, and though the meagre furnishings had not been removed, the house, with its drawn blinds and grimy steps, had the desolate appearance of emptiness.
The whisper of a rumour had agitated the domestic circles of that respectable suburb﻿—a startling rumour which, if it were true, might well cause Forest Hill to gasp in righteous indignation.
“Dr.” Essley was an unauthorized practitioner, a fraud of the worst description, for he had taken the name and the style of a dead man.
“All I know,” explained Colonel Black, whom a reporter discovered at his office, “is that I met Dr. Essley in Australia, and that I was impressed by his skill. I might say,” he added in a burst of frankness, “that I am in a sense responsible for his position in England, for I not only advanced him money to buy his practice, but I recommended him to all my friends, and naturally I am upset by the revelation.”
No, he had no idea as to the “doctor’s” present location. He had last seen him a month before, when the “doctor” spoke of going to the Continent.

===Chinese===
埃斯利博士在福里斯特山（Forest Hill）的宅子空了。门前的红灯没有点亮，寒酸的家具虽尚未搬走，屋子放下窗帘、台阶蒙尘，一派空落落的荒凉景象。
一则流言的耳语，搅动了这个体面郊区的家庭圈子——流言骇人，倘若属实，足以叫福里斯特山倒抽一口冷气，义愤填膺。
这位「博士」埃斯利是个无照行医的骗子，还是最恶劣的那种——他盗用了一个死人的姓名和头衔。
「我只知道，」一位记者在办公室里寻到的布莱克上校解释说，「我是在澳大利亚认识埃斯利博士的，他的医术令我钦佩。可以说，」他一时坦诚起来，又补了一句，「从某种意义上讲，他在英国的位子是我造就的，因为我不仅垫钱给他盘下诊所，还把他推荐给了我所有的朋友。真相曝出来，我自然很痛心。」
不，「博士」眼下在哪儿，他毫无所知。他最后一次见到他，是一个月前，那时「博士」说起要去大陆。

===Original===
Colonel Black had as much to tell﻿—and no more﻿—to the detectives who came from Scotland Yard. They came with annoying persistence and never seemed tired of coming. They waited for him on the doorstep and in his office. They waited for him in the vestibules of the theatres, at the entrance doors of banks. They came as frequently as emissaries of houses to whom Colonel Black was under monetary obligation.
A week after the events chronicled in the last chapter. Colonel Black sat alone in his flat with a light heart. He had collected together a very considerable amount of money. That it was money to which he had no legal right did not disturb the smooth current of his thoughts. It was sufficient that it *was* money, and that a motorcar which might carry him swiftly to Folkestone was within telephone-call day and night. Moreover, he was alive.
The vengeance of an organization vowed against Dr. Essley had passed over the head of Colonel Black﻿—he might be excused if he thought that the matter of a grey wig and a pair of shaggy eyebrows, added to some knowledge of medicine, had deceived the astute men who had come to England to track him down.
This infernal man Fellowe, who appeared and disappeared as if by magic, puzzled him﻿—almost alarmed him.
Fellowe was not one of the “Four Just Men”﻿—instinct told him that much. Fellowe was an official.

===Chinese===
苏格兰场派来的侦探，布莱克上校对他们说的也就这么多——仅此而已。这些侦探来得烦人地勤，仿佛永远不知疲倦。他们在门阶上等他，在办公室里等他，在剧院的门厅里、在银行的门口等他。他们登门的频次，抵得上那些布莱克上校欠着钱的商号派来的讨债使者。
上一章记叙的事件过后一个星期，布莱克上校独自坐在公寓里，心情轻快。他已凑拢了一笔相当可观的款项。这钱在法律上并不归他所有，却搅不乱他思绪的平流。要紧的是它*是*钱，而一辆能载他飞奔福克斯通（Folkestone）的汽车，日夜一个电话就能叫到。何况，他还活着。
一个组织立誓向「埃斯利博士」复仇，那复仇的浪头从布莱克上校头顶掠了过去——他若以为，一顶灰色假发、两道蓬乱的浓眉，再加几分医学知识，就骗过了那些远道来英国追查他的精明人物，那也情有可原。
只有这个邪门的费洛，来得神奇去得神奇，叫他捉摸不透——几乎叫他心惊。
费洛不是「四义士」的人——本能告诉他这一点。费洛是官家的人。

===Original===
A Sergeant Gurden who had been extremely useful to Black had been suddenly transferred to a remote division, and nobody knew why. With him had disappeared from his familiar beats a young police-constable who had been seen dining with Cabinet Ministers.
It was very evident that there was cause for perturbation﻿—yet, singularity enough, Colonel Black was cheerful; but there was a malignant quality to his cheerfulness. He busied himself with the destruction of such of his papers﻿—and they were few﻿—which he had kept by him.
He turned out an old pocketbook and frowned when he saw its contents. It was a wagon-lit coupon for the journey from Paris to Madrid, and was made out in the name of Dr. Essley﻿—a mad slip which might have led to serious consequences, he told himself. He burnt the incriminating sheet and crumbled the ashes before he threw them into the fireplace.
It was dark before he had finished his preparations, but he made no attempt to light the room. His dress-suit was laid out in an adjoining room, his trunks stood packed.

===Chinese===
一位对布莱克极有用的格登巡佐，突然被调到边远的分区去了，谁也不知道为什么。同他一起从熟识的巡段上消失的，还有一个有人见过同内阁大臣共进晚餐的年轻警员。
很明显，这是叫人心神不宁的因由——然而奇怪的是，布莱克上校反倒兴冲冲的；只是他的兴冲冲里带着一股狠劲。他忙着销毁手边留存的文件——为数倒也不多。
他翻出一本旧皮夹，看清里面的东西，皱起了眉。那是一张巴黎到马德里的卧铺车票，票面上的名字是埃斯利博士——一个疯狂的疏失，他说不定会惹出大祸。他把这张惹祸的纸烧掉，把纸灰捻碎，才扔进壁炉。
一切准备停当时天已黑透，他却不打算点灯。他的晚礼服放在隔壁房间里，衣箱都已收拾停当。

===Original===
He looked at his watch. In half an hour he would be on his way to the Sandfords. Here was another risk which none but a madman would take﻿—so he told himself, but he contemplated the outcome of his visit with equanimity.
He went into his bedroom and began his preparations, then remembered that he had left a bundle of notes on his writing-table, and went back. He found the notes and was returning when there was a click, and the room was flooded with light.
He whipped round with an oath, dropping his hand to his hip-pocket.
“Don’t move, please,” said the visitor quietly.

===Chinese===
他看看表。再过半小时，他就该动身去桑德福德家了。这是一场只有疯子才肯冒的险——他对自己这么说，可他盘算此行的结局时，心里平静如水。
他走进卧室开始更衣，忽想起一沓钞票还落在写字台上，便折回去。钞票找到了，正要返回，只听咔哒一声，满屋灯火通明。
他咒骂着猛地转身，手落向裤后袋。
「请别动。」来客平静地说。

===Original===
“You!” gasped Black.
The tall man with the little pointed beard nodded.
“Keep your hand away from your pocket, colonel,” he said; “there is no immediate danger.”
He was unarmed. The thin cigar between his white teeth testified his serenity.
“De la Monte!” stammered Black.

===Chinese===
「是你！」布莱克倒抽一口气。
那个蓄着尖尖短须的高个子男人点了点头。
「手别往口袋里伸，上校。」他说。「眼下没有危险。」
他没有带武器。白牙齿间叼着的细雪茄，证明着他心绪的安宁。
「德拉蒙特！」布莱克结结巴巴地说。

===Original===
Again the bearded man nodded.
“The last time we met was in Cordova,” he said, “but you have changed since then.”
Black forced a smile.
“You are confusing me with Dr. Essley,” he said.
“I am confusing you with Dr. Essley,” agreed the other. “Yet I think I am justified in my confusion.”

===Chinese===
蓄须的男人又点了点头。
「上一回咱们见面，是在科尔多瓦。」他说。「不过您从那以后变样了。」
布莱克挤出一丝笑。
「您把我同埃斯利博士弄混了。」他说。
「我就是把您同埃斯利博士弄混了。」对方表示同意。「不过我想，我这混淆是有道理的。」

===Original===
He did not remove his cigar, seemed perfectly at ease, even going so far as to cast an eye upon a chair, inviting invitation.
“Essley or Black,” he said steadily, “your day is already dusk, and the night is very near.”
A cold wave of terror swept over the colonel. He tried to speak, but his throat and his mouth were dry, and he could only make inarticulate noises.
“Tonight﻿—now?” he croaked﻿—his shaking hands went up to his mouth. Yet he was armed and the man before him bore no weapon. A quick movement of his hand and he would lay the spectre which had at one time terrorized Europe. He did not doubt that he was face to face with one of the dreaded Four, and he found himself endeavouring to memorize the face of the man before him for future use. Yet he did not touch the pistol which lay snug in his hip-pocket. He was hypnotized, paralysed by the cool confidence of the other. All that he knew was that he wanted the relief which could only come if this calm man were to go. He felt horribly trapped, saw no way of escape in the presence of this force.
The other divined what was going on in Black’s mind.

===Chinese===
他不取下雪茄，神态安闲自若，甚至朝一把椅子瞥了一眼，那神情仿佛在示意对方请自己坐。
「埃斯利也罢，布莱克也罢，」他不紧不慢地说，「您的白昼已经向晚，黑夜近在眼前了。」
一阵寒流似的恐惧扫过上校全身。他想说话，喉咙和嘴里却干得冒烟，只能发出含混的声响。
「今夜——就是现在？」他嘶哑地问——颤抖的双手抬向嘴边。他身上可是有枪的，眼前这人却手无寸铁。手一动，他就能撂倒那个曾令全欧洲胆寒的鬼影。他毫不怀疑，自己面对面站着的是四义士之一，而且他发觉自己正竭力默记眼前这张脸，以备日后之需。然而他没有去碰安安稳稳躺在裤后袋里的手枪。他被对方冷静的自信催眠了，镇瘫了。他只知道一件事：唯有这位心平气和的人离开，他才能得到解脱。他感到自己陷在可怕的罗网里，在这股力量面前无路可逃。
对方看穿了布莱克心里转的念头。

===Original===
“I have only one piece of advice to offer you,” he said, “and that is this﻿—keep away from the Sandfords’ dinner.”
“Why﻿—why?” stammered Black.
The other walked to the fireplace and flicked the ash of his cigar into the grate.
“Because,” he said, without turning round, “at the Sandford dinner you come within the jurisdiction of the ‘Four Just Men’﻿—who, as you may know, are a protecting force. Elsewhere﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我只有一句忠告奉送，」他说，「就是这个——离桑德福德家的晚宴远一点。」
「为什么——为什么？」布莱克结巴起来。
对方踱到壁炉边，把雪茄灰弹进炉膛。
「因为，」他背对着他说，「在桑德福德的晚宴上，您就落进了『四义士』的管区——您大概知道，他们是一支护卫的力量。在别处——」

===Original===
“Yes﻿—elsewhere?”
“You come within the jurisdiction of the law, Colonel Black, for at this present moment an energetic young Assistant-Commissioner of Police is applying for a warrant for your arrest on the charge of murder.”
With a little nod, Manfred turned his back and walked leisurely towards the door.
“Stop!”

===Chinese===
「是的——在别处？」
「在别处，您就落进了法律的管区，布莱克上校，因为此时此刻，一位精明干练的年轻警察助理总监，正在申请逮捕您的拘票，罪名是谋杀。」
曼弗雷德（Manfred）微微颔首，转身朝门口从容走去。
「站住！」

===Original===
The words were hissed. Black, revolver in hand, was livid with rage and fear.
Manfred laughed quietly. He did not check his walk, but looked backward over his shoulder.
“Let the cobbler stick to his last,” he quoted. “Poison, my dear colonel, is your last﻿—or the knife in the case of Jakobs. An explosion, even of a Webley revolver, would shatter your nerves.”
He opened the door and walked out, closing it carefully behind him.

===Chinese===
这两个字是从齿缝里挤出来的。布莱克手握左轮枪，脸气得发青，又怕得发青。
曼弗雷德轻轻笑了。他脚下不停，只回头望了一眼。
「鞋匠还是守着他的楦头好。」他引了一句老话。「毒药，亲爱的上校，才是您的看家本领——再不然，对付雅各布斯那样的就用刀子。一声爆响，哪怕是韦伯利左轮的，也够震碎您的神经。」
他拉开门走出去，随手把门轻轻带上。

===Original===
Black sank into the nearest chair, his mouth working, the perspiration streaming down his face.
This was the end. He was a spent force. He crossed the room to the telephone and gave a number. After a little while he got an answer.
Yes, the car was in readiness; there had been no inquiries. He hung up the telephone and called up six depots where cars could be hired. To each he gave the same instructions. Two cars were to be waiting﻿—he changed the locality with each order. Two fast cars, each able to cover the eighty miles to Dover without fear of a breakdown.
“I shall take one,” he said, “the other must follow immediately behind﻿—yes, empty. I am going to Dover to meet a party of people.”
He would take no risk of a breakdown. The second car must be close at hand in case he had an accident with the first.

===Chinese===
布莱克跌进最近的一把椅子里，嘴抽动着，汗珠顺着脸淌。
完了。他已是强弩之末。他横过屋子走到电话机前，拨了个号码。过了一会儿，有了回音。
是的，车已备好；没有任何人来打听过。他挂上电话，又给六处可雇汽车的车行挂了电话，给每处下的吩咐一模一样。要备两部车等着——每次吩咐换一个地点。两部快车，每一部都得能一口气跑完到多佛的八十英里，不怕半路抛锚。
「我坐一部，」他说，「另一部紧随在后——对，空车。我要去多佛接一伙人。」
抛锚的险他一丝也不冒。第二部车必须紧跟在近旁，以防第一部出岔子。

===Original===
He was something of an organizer. In the short space of time he was at the telephone, he arranged the cars so that whatever avenue of escape he was forced to take he would find the vehicles waiting.
This done, he completed his dressing. The reaction from the fear had come. He was filled with black hate for the men who had put a period to his career of villainy. Most of all he hated Sandford, the man who could have saved him.
He would take the risk of the Four﻿—take his chance with the police. Curiously, he feared the police least of all.
One final blow he would strike and break the man whose obstinacy had broken him.
He was mad with anger﻿—he saw nothing but the fulfilment of his plan of revenge. He went into his room, unlocked a cupboard and took out the green bottle. There was no need for the feather, he would do the job thoroughly.

===Chinese===
他倒也算得个组织人才。打电话这短短一阵工夫，他把几辆车安排得无论被迫取哪条逃路，都会有车候着。
诸事办妥，他更衣完毕。恐惧过后的反作用涌了上来。他对那几个给他的恶行画上句号的人，恨意滔天。他最恨的是桑德福德——那个本可以救他的人。
四义士这险他冒了——跟警察碰运气他也认了。奇怪的是，他最不怕的恰恰是警察。
他要最后再下一记毒手，击碎那个用固执击碎了他的人。
他怒得发了狂——眼里只剩他那复仇计划的完成。他走进房里，开了柜子的锁，取出那只绿瓶。羽毛这回用不着了，这趟活他要干得干净彻底。

===Original===
He finished his dressing, pocketed his banknotes, and slipped the little green bottle into his waistcoat pocket. One last look round he gave, then, with a sense of the old exhilaration which had been his before the arrival of Manfred, he put on his hat, threw an overcoat over his arm and went out.
---
It was a gay little party that assembled at the Great South Central Hotel. May Sandford had invited a girl friend, and Mr. Sandford had brought back the junior partner of one of the City houses he did business with.
Black was late and did not arrive till a quarter of an hour after the time settled for dinner. Sandford had given orders for the meal to be served when the colonel came in.

===Chinese===
他穿扮停当，钞票揣进衣袋，小绿瓶滑进马甲口袋。最后环视一圈，随后，带着曼弗雷德到来之前他曾有过的那股旧日的亢奋，他戴上帽子，臂上搭一件大衣，出了门。
---
大南中央旅馆里聚起了一小席欢宴。梅·桑德福德邀了一位女朋友，桑德福德先生则带回来一位和他有生意往来的伦敦城商号的年轻合伙人。
布莱克迟到了，比定好的开席时刻晚了一刻钟才到。上校进门时，桑德福德刚吩咐上菜。

===Original===
“Sit down, Black,” said Sandford. There was a chair between the ironmaster and his daughter, and into this he dropped.
His hand shook as he took up the spoon to his soup.
He put the spoon down again and unfolded his serviette. A letter dropped out. He knew those grey envelopes now, and crushed the letter into his pocket without attempting to read it.
“Busy man, Black, eh?” smiled Sandford. He was a florid, hearty man with a wisp of white whisker on either side of his rubicund face, and in his pleasant moments he was a very lovable man. “You ought to be grateful I did not agree to the amalgamations﻿—you would have been worked to death.”
“Yes,” said the colonel shortly. He stuck out his jaw﻿—a trick he had when he was perturbed.

===Chinese===
「坐，布莱克。」桑德福德说。铁业巨子和女儿之间空着一把椅子，他一屁股坐了进去。
他拿起汤匙舀汤，手在抖。
他又放下汤匙，展开餐巾。一封信掉了出来。这种灰色信封他如今认得了，看也没看，一把攥进衣袋。
「大忙人哪，布莱克，嗯？」桑德福德笑道。他是个红光满面、精气神十足的人，红润的脸庞两侧各留一撮白颊须，高兴起来是个非常讨人喜欢的老头。「你该谢我才对，亏得我没答应那桩合并——不然非把你活活累死不可。」
「是啊。」上校简短地说。他下巴往前一凸——他心绪不宁时就有这个习惯。

===Original===
“In a way,” bantered the elder man, “you’re an admirable chap. If you were a little more reasonable you would be more successful.”
“Wouldn’t you call me successful?”
Sandford pouted thoughtfully.
“Yes and no,” he said. “You are not altogether successful. You see, you have achieved what you would call success too easily.”
Colonel Black did not pursue the subject, nor did he encourage the other to go any further. He needed opportunity. For a time he had to sit patiently, joining in, with such scraps of speech as he could muster, the conversation that rippled about him.

===Chinese===
「从某个角度讲，」长者打趣道，「你是个了不起的家伙。你要是肯通融一点，还会更成功。」
「难道你还不算我成功？」
桑德福德若有所思地噘起嘴。
「算也不算。」他说。「你算不得全然成功。你瞧，你把你自己所谓的那种成功，来得太容易了。」
布莱克上校没有接这个话头，也不想让对方再往下说。他需要的是时机。一时间，他只好耐着性子坐着，捡起只言片语，应付着身边流过的谈笑。

===Original===
At his left hand were the girl’s wineglasses. She refused the lighter wines and drew forth a laughing protest from her father.
“Dearie, on your birthday﻿—you must sip some champagne!”
“Champagne, then!” she said gaily. She was happy for many reasons, but principally because﻿—well, just because.
That was the opportunity.
Absentmindedly he drew her glass nearer, then he found the bottle in his pocket. With one hand he removed the cork and spilt half the contents of the phial on to his serviette. He re-corked the bottle and slipped it into his pocket. He took the glass on to his lap. Twice he wiped the edge of it with the damp napkin. He replaced the glass unnoticed.

===Chinese===
他的左手边是姑娘的酒杯。她谢绝了低度的酒，招来父亲一声笑呵呵的抗议。
「乖丫头，过生日呢——香槟总得抿一口！」
「那就香槟！」她快活地说。她高兴，缘由有一大把，但主要还是因为——嗯，就是因为高兴。
机会来了。
他心不在焉地把她的酒杯拉近，随即摸到了口袋里的瓶子。他一只手拔开瓶塞，把小瓶里一半的药液泼在餐巾上，重新塞好瓶塞，把瓶子滑回口袋。他把酒杯挪到膝上，用浸湿的餐巾把杯沿擦了两遍，再把酒杯不动声色地放回原处。

===Original===
Now it was done he felt better. He leant back in his chair, his hands thrust deep into his trousers pockets. It was an inelegant attitude, but he derived a sense of comfort.
“Black, wake up, my dear fellow!” Sandford was talking to him, and he roused himself with a start. “My friend here was rude enough to comment on your hair.”
“Eh?” Black put up his hand to his head.
“Oh, it’s all right and it isn’t disarranged﻿—but how long has it been white?”

===Chinese===
事已办成，他心里松快了些。他往椅背上一靠，双手深深插进裤袋。这坐相不雅，他却觉得舒坦。
「布莱克，醒醒，我亲爱的伙计！」桑德福德在跟他说话，他一个激灵回过神。「我这位朋友冒失得很，居然评论起你的头发来了。」
「嗯？」布莱克抬手摸头。
「哦，没事，一点没乱——可它白了有多久啦？」

===Original===
“White?”
He had heard of such things and was mildly interested.
“White? Oh﻿—er﻿—quite a time.”
He did not further the discussion. The waiters were filling the glasses. He looked across to Sandford. How happy, how self-sufficient he was. He intercepted the tender little looks that passed between father and daughter. There was perfect sympathy between the two. It was a pity that in a minute or so one should be dead and the other broken. She so full of life, so splendid of shape, so fresh and lovely. He turned his head and looked at her. Curious, very curious, how frail a thing is life, that a milligram of a colourless fluid should be sufficient to snap the cord that binds soul to body.
The waiter filled the glasses﻿—first the girl’s, then his.

===Chinese===
「白了？」
这种事他听说过，颇有几分兴致。
「白了？哦——呃——有一阵子了。」
他没有深谈。侍者正在斟酒。他望向桌子对面的桑德福德。这人多么幸福，多么自足。他拦下了父女之间传递的脉脉小眼色。父女俩之间一片融洽。可惜啊，再过一分钟，一个死，一个碎。她这般生机充盈，体态这般曼妙，这般鲜妍可爱。他转过头端详她。奇怪，真是奇怪，生命这东西何其脆弱——一毫克的透明液体，就足以挣断那根系魂于身的细绳。
侍者斟满了酒杯——先斟姑娘的，再斟他的。

===Original===
He raised his own with unconcern and drank it off.
The girl did not touch hers. She was talking to the man on her left. Black could see only the rounded cheek and one white shoulder.
He waited impatiently.
Sandford tried to bring him into the conversation, but he refused to be drawn. He was content to listen, he said. To listen, to watch and to wait. He saw the slim white fingers close round the stem of the glass, saw her half raise it, still looking towards her partner.
Black pushed his chair a little to one side as the glass reached her lips. She drank, not much, but enough.

===Chinese===
他若无其事地举杯，一饮而尽。
姑娘没有碰她的杯子。她正同左边的人说话。布莱克看得见的，只有她圆润的脸颊和一段雪白的肩膀。
他不耐烦地等着。
桑德福德想把他拉进谈话，他却不肯上钩。他说他乐意听着。听着，看着，等着。他看见那纤纤的白手指合拢在杯脚上，看见她把杯子半举起来，眼睛仍望着她的同伴。
杯子挨到唇边时，布莱克把椅子往旁边挪了挪。她喝了，不多，但够了。

===Original===
The colonel held his breath. She replaced the glass, still talking with the man on her left.
Black counted the slow seconds. He counted sixty﻿—a hundred, oblivious to the fact that Sandford was talking to him.
The drug had failed!
“Are you ill, colonel?”
Everybody was staring at him.

===Chinese===
上校屏住了呼吸。她放下杯子，还在同左边的男人说话。
布莱克数着缓慢的秒。数到六十——数到一百，连桑德福德在跟他说话都没听见。
药失灵了！
「您不舒服吗，上校？」
人人都盯着他瞧。

===Original===
“Ill?” he repeated hoarsely. “No, I am not ill﻿—why should I be ill?”
“Open one of those windows, waiter.”
A blast of cold air struck him and he shivered.
He left the table hurriedly and went blundering blindly from the room. There was an end to it all.

===Chinese===
「不舒服？」他嗓音沙哑地反问。「没有啊，我好好的——我怎么会不舒服？」
「侍者，开一扇窗。」
一股冷气扑到他身上，他打了个寒噤。
他匆匆离席，跌跌撞撞、昏头昏脑地冲出房间。一切都完了。

===Original===
In the corridor of the hotel he came in his haste into collision with a man. It was the man who had called upon him some time before.
“Excuse me,” said the man, catching his arm. “Colonel Black, I believe.”
“Stand out of my way.”
Black spat out the words savagely.

===Chinese===
在旅馆走廊里，他慌不择路，同一个男人撞了个满怀。正是先前上门拜访过他的那个人。
「对不住，」那人一把抓住他的胳膊。「我想是布莱克上校吧。」
「让开。」
布莱克凶狠地吐出这两个字。

===Original===
“I am Detective-Sergeant Kay from Scotland Yard, and shall take you into custody.”
At the first hint of danger the colonel drew back. Suddenly his fist shot out and caught the officer under the jaw. It was a terrific blow and the detective was unprepared. He went down like a log.
The corridor was empty. Leaving the man upon the floor, the fugitive sped into the lobby. He was hatless, but he shaded his face and passed through the throng in the vestibule into the open air. He signalled a taxi.
“Waterloo, and I will give you a pound if you catch my train.”

===Chinese===
「我是苏格兰场的刑事巡佐凯（Kay），要拘捕你归案。」
危险初露苗头，上校便往后一缩。突然，他一拳飞出，正中那警官的下巴。这一拳势大力沉，侦探毫无防备，像根木头似的栽倒了。
走廊空无一人。逃犯把那人撂在地上，一头扎进大堂。他没戴帽子，遮着脸，穿过门厅里的人群来到露天，招手叫了辆出租车。
「滑铁卢车站，赶上我的火车，我给你一英镑。」

===Original===
He was speeding down the Strand in less than a minute. He changed his instructions before the station was reached.
“I have lost the train﻿—drop me at the corner of Eaton Square.”
At Eaton Square he paid the cabman and dismissed him. With little difficulty he found two closed cars that waited.
“I am Colonel Black,” he said, and the first chauffeur touched his cap. “Take the straightest road to Southampton and let the second man follow behind.”

===Chinese===
不到一分钟，他已顺着斯特兰德街飞驰。快到车站时，他又改了口令。
「火车赶不上了——送我到伊顿广场街角。」
到了伊顿广场，他付了车钱，打发司机走。他没费什么事，就找到了候着的两辆篷车。
「我是布莱克上校。」他说，头一个司机碰了碰帽檐。「抄最近的直路去南安普敦，叫第二辆车跟在后面。」

===Original===
The car had not gone far before he changed his mind.
“Go first to the Junior Turf Club in Pall Mall,” he said.
Arrived at the club, he beckoned the porter.
“Tell Sir Isaac Tramber that he is wanted at once,” he directed.
Ikey was in the club﻿—it was a chance shot of the colonel’s, but it bagged his man.

===Chinese===
车没开出多远，他又变了主意。
「先去帕尔摩街的青年赛马俱乐部。」他说。
到了俱乐部，他招手叫门房。
「告诉艾萨克·特兰伯爵士，立刻有人找。」他吩咐道。
艾基正在俱乐部里——这是上校碰运气的一箭，却正中靶心。

===Original===
“Get your coat and hat,” said Black hurriedly to the flustered baronet.
“But﻿—”
“No buts,” snarled the other savagely. “Get your coat and hat, unless you want to be hauled out of your club to the nearest police-station.”
Reluctantly Ikey went back to the club and returned in a few seconds struggling into his greatcoat.
“Now what the devil is this all about?” he demanded peevishly; then, as the light of a street lamp caught the colonel’s uncovered head, he gasped:
“Good Lord! Your hair has gone white! You look just like that fellow Essley!”

===Chinese===
「拿上你的外衣帽子。」布莱克急匆匆对手忙脚乱的从男爵说。
「可是——」
「没什么可是，」对方凶狠地咆哮。「拿上外衣帽子，除非你想从你的俱乐部里被拖到最近的警察局去。」
艾基老大不情愿地折回俱乐部，几秒钟后便一边挣扎着套大衣一边出来了。
「这到底演的哪一出？」他不耐烦地喝问；随即，一盏街灯的光照亮上校没戴帽子的头，他倒抽一口气：
「老天爷！你的头发全白了！你活脱脱就是那个埃斯利！」

## VII Dr. Essley Meets Man / 七 埃斯利博士遇上对手

===Original===
Dr. Essley was in his study, making a very careful microscopic examination. The room was in darkness save for the light which came from a powerful electric lamp directed to the reflector of the instrument. What he found on the slide was evidently satisfactory, for by and by he removed the strip of glass, threw it into the fire and turned on the lights.
He took up a newspaper cutting from the table and read it.
It interested him, for it was an account of the sudden death of Mr. Augustus Fanks.
“The deceased gentleman,” ran the account, “was engaged with Colonel Black, the famous financier, discussing the details of the new iron amalgamation, when he suddenly collapsed and, before medical assistance could be procured, expired, it is believed, of heart failure.”
There had been no inquest, for Fanks had in truth a weak heart and had been under the care of a specialist, who, since his speciality was heart trouble, discovered symptoms of the disease on the slightest pretext.

===Chinese===
埃斯利博士正在书房里，做一项极仔细的显微检查。屋里一片漆黑，只有一盏强力电灯射向仪器反光镜的光。他在载玻片上看到的东西显然令他满意，因为不久他便取下那条玻璃片，扔进火里，开了灯。
他从桌上拿起一截剪报，读了起来。
剪报令他兴致盎然，因为那是一则关于奥古斯塔斯·法恩克斯先生暴毙的报道。
报道写道：「故世的那位先生，当时正与著名金融家布莱克上校商谈新钢铁合并的细节，忽然昏倒，未及延医救治，据信死于心力衰竭。」
死因研讯没有开——法恩克斯心脏本来就弱，又一直在一位专科名医手下调治；那位名医的专长正是心脏，所以稍有个由头，便能诊出心脏病的症候来。

===Original===
So that was the end of Fanks. The doctor nodded slowly. Yes, that was the end of him. And now?
He took a letter from his pocket. It was addressed to him in the round sprawling calligraphy of Theodore Sandford.
Essley had met him in the early days when Sandford was on friendly terms with Black. He had been recommended to the ironmaster by the financier, and had treated him for divers ills. “My suburban doctor,” Sandford had called him.
“Though I am not seeing eye to eye with our friend Black,” he wrote, “and we are for the moment at daggers drawn, I trust that this will not affect our relationships, the more so since I wish you to see my daughter.”

===Chinese===
这就是法恩克斯的下场了。博士缓缓点头。是的，他完了。接下来呢？
他从口袋里取出一封信。信封上是西奥多·桑德福德那手圆浑铺张的字。
埃斯利早年限他打过交道，那时桑德福德同布莱克还交好。是那位金融家把他推荐给铁业巨头的，他给他治过五花八门的毛病。「我的郊区大夫」，桑德福德这样称呼他。
「虽然我同咱们那位朋友布莱克看法相左，」信上写道，「一时闹到了剑拔弩张的地步，但我相信这不会影响你我交情，更何况，我想请你看看我的女儿。」

===Original===
Essley remembered having seen her once: a tall girl, with eyes that danced with laughter and a complexion of milk and roses.
He put the letter in his pocket, went into his little surgery and locked the door. When he came out he wore his long overcoat and carried a little satchel. He had just time to catch a train for the City, and at eleven o’clock he found himself in Sandford’s mansion.
“You are a weird man, doctor,” said the ironmaster with a smile, as he greeted his visitor. “Do you visit most of your patients by night?”
“My aristocratic patients,” said the other coolly.

===Chinese===
埃斯利记得见过她一回：高挑的姑娘，一双笑意盈盈的眼睛，牛乳玫瑰般的肤色。
他把信揣进衣袋，走进小诊疗室，锁上门。出来时他已穿上长大衣，提着一只小提包。他刚好赶上去伦敦城的火车，十一点钟，人已到了桑德福德的府上。
「你这个人真是古怪，大夫。」铁业巨头含笑迎客。「你的病人，多半都在夜里出诊吗？」
「我那些贵族病人，是这样。」对方冷冷地说。

===Original===
“A bad job about poor Fanks,” said the other. “He and I were only dining together a few weeks ago. Did he tell you that he met a man who knew you in Australia?”
A shadow of annoyance passed over the other’s face.
“Let us talk about your daughter,” he said brusquely. “What is the matter with her?”
The ironmaster smiled sheepishly.

===Chinese===
「可怜的法恩克斯这事，真是不妙。」这位说。「几个星期前他我还同桌吃饭来着。他跟没跟你说过，他碰见一个在澳大利亚认识你的人？」
一丝恼怒的阴影掠过对方的脸。
「谈谈令嫒吧。」他生硬地说。「她怎么了？」
铁业巨头讪讪一笑。

===Original===
“Nothing, I fear; yet you know, Essley, she is my only child, and I sometimes imagine that she is looking ill. My doctor in Newcastle tells me that there is nothing wrong with her.”
“I see,” said Essley. “Where is she?”
“She is at the theatre,” confessed the father. “You must think I am an awful fool to bring you up to town to discuss the health of a girl who is at the theatre, but something upset her pretty badly last night, and I was today glad to see her take enough interest in life to visit a musical comedy.”
“Most fathers are fools,” said the other. “I will wait till she comes in.” He strolled to the window and looked out.

===Chinese===
「恐怕没什么。可是你知道，埃斯利，她是我唯一的孩子，我有时候总觉得她气色不好。我在纽卡斯尔的医生又说她什么毛病都没有。」
「明白了。」埃斯利说。「她在哪儿？」
「她看戏去了。」做父亲的招认。「你一定觉得我蠢得可以，把你从城里请来，谈一个正泡在戏院里的姑娘的身子骨。可昨晚有个事把她搅得够呛，今天见她还提得起兴致去看一出喜歌剧，我倒高兴。」
「天下的父亲多半是傻瓜。」对方说。「我等她回来。」他踱到窗前，望着外面。

===Original===
“Why have you quarrelled with Black?” he asked suddenly.
The older man frowned.
“Business,” he said shortly. “He is pushing me into a corner. I helped him four years ago﻿—”
“He helped you, too,” interrupted the doctor.

===Chinese===
「你为什么同布莱克闹翻了？」他突然问。
老人皱眉。
「为了买卖。」他简短地说。「他把我逼到墙角了。四年前我帮过他——」
「他也帮过你。」大夫打断道。

===Original===
“But not so much as I helped him,” said the other obstinately. “I gave him his chance. He floated my company and I profited, but he profited more. The business has now grown to such vast proportions that it will not pay me to come in. Nothing will alter my determination.”
“I see.” Essley whistled a little tune as he walked again to the window.
Such men as this must be broken, he thought. Broken! And there was only one way: that daughter of his. He could do nothing tonight, that was evident﻿—nothing.
“I do not think I will wait for your daughter,” he said. “Perhaps I will call in tomorrow evening.”

===Chinese===
「可没我帮他的多。」对方倔强地说。「是我给了他机会。他替我的公司招了股，我赚了，他赚得更多。如今这摊生意做得太大了，我犯不着进去。什么也改不了我的主意。」
「明白了。」埃斯利吹着小调，又踱回窗前。
这种人非得弄碎不可，他想。弄碎！办法只有一个：他那个女儿。今晚他什么也做不成，这很明显——什么也做不成。
「我想不必等令嫒了。」他说。「也许明晚我再来拜访。」

===Original===
“I am so sorry﻿—”
But the doctor silenced him.
“There is no need to be sorry,” he said with acerbity; “you will find my visit charged in my bill.”
The ironmaster laughed as he saw him to the door.

===Chinese===
「真抱歉——」
可大夫抬手止住了他。
「用不着抱歉。」他话里带着酸味。「你会看到，这趟出诊是记在账单上的。」
铁业巨头笑着送他到门口。

===Original===
“You are almost as good a financier as your friend,” he said.
“Almost,” said the doctor dryly.
His waiting taxi dropped him at Charing Cross, and he went straight to the nearest call-office and rang up a Temperance Hotel at Bloomsbury.
He had reasons for wishing to meet a Mr. Weld who knew him in Australia.

===Chinese===
「你快赶上你那位朋友，够当个好金融家了。」他说。
「快赶上了。」大夫干巴巴地说。
候着他的出租车把他放在查令十字，他径直走进最近的公用电话亭，往布卢姆斯伯里那家禁酒旅馆挂了个电话。
他有他的理由，想见一见那位在澳大利亚认识他的韦尔德先生。

===Original===
He had no difficulty in getting the message through. Mr. Weld was in the hotel. He waited whilst the attendant found him. By and by a voice spoke:
“I am Weld﻿—do you want me?”
“Yes; my name is Cole. I knew you in Australia. I have a message for you from a mutual friend. Can you see me tonight?”
“Yes; where?”

===Chinese===
电话一打就通。韦尔德先生在旅馆里。他等着侍者去寻人。过了一会儿，一个声音响了：
「我是韦尔德——找我？」
「对。我姓科尔（Cole）。我在澳大利亚认识你。有位共同的朋友托我带个口信给你。今晚能见一面吗？」
「能。在哪儿？」

===Original===
Dr. Essley had decided the place of meeting.
“Outside the main entrance of the British Museum,” he said. “There are few people about at this time of night, and I am less likely to miss you.”
There was a pause at the other end of the wire.
“Very good,” said the voice, “in a quarter of an hour?”

===Chinese===
会面地点，埃斯利博士早已想好了。
「大英博物馆正门外。」他说。「这个钟点外头人少，我也不容易错过你。」
电话那头停了片刻。
「很好。」那声音说。「一刻钟后？」

===Original===
“That will suit me admirably﻿—goodbye.”
He hung up the receiver. Leaving his satchel at the cloakroom at Charing Cross Station, he set out to walk to Great Russell Street. He would take no cab. There should be no evidence of that description. Black would not like it. He smiled at the thought. Great Russell Street was deserted, save for a constant stream of taxicabs passing and repassing and an occasional pedestrian. He found his man waiting; rather tall and slight, with an intellectual, refined face.
“Dr. Essley?” he asked, coming forward as the other halted.
“That is my﻿—”

===Chinese===
「正合我意——再见。」
他挂上听筒。提包寄存在查令十字车站的寄存处，他步行往大拉塞尔街去。出租车他不坐。这类形迹一星半点也不能留。布莱克会不高兴的。想到这里他笑了。大拉塞尔街上空空荡荡，只有川流不息来来往往的出租车和偶尔一个行人。他要找的人已经在等了：身材瘦高，一张书卷气的清秀脸庞。
「埃斯利博士？」见对方站定，他迎上前来问。
「那正是我的——」

===Original===
Essley stopped.
“My name is Cole,” he said harshly. “What made you think I was Essley?”
“Your voice,” said the other calmly. “After all, it does not matter what you call yourself; I want to see you.”
“And I you,” said Essley.

===Chinese===
埃斯利刹住了。
「我姓科尔。」他声色俱厉地说。「你怎么会以为我是埃斯利？」
「听声音。」对方平静地说。「说到底，你叫什么无关紧要；我要见的是你这个人。」
「我也正要见你。」埃斯利说。

===Original===
They walked along side by side until they came to a side street.
“What do you want of me?” asked the doctor.
The other laughed.
“I wanted to see you. You are not a bit like the Essley I knew. He was slighter and had not your colouring, and I was always under the impression that the Essley who went up into the bush died.”

===Chinese===
两人并肩走着，走进了一条小街。
「你想从我这里要什么？」大夫问。
对方笑了。
「我就是想见见你。你同我认得的那位埃斯利一点儿也不像。他更瘦削，也没有你这样的气色。我一直有个印象：上丛林里去的那位埃斯利，死在了那里。」

===Original===
“It is possible,” said Essley in an absent way. He wanted to gain time. The street was empty. A little way down there was a gateway in which a man might lie unobserved until a policeman came.
In his pocket he had an impregnated feather carefully wrapped up in lint and oiled silk. He drew it from his pocket furtively and with his hands behind him he stripped it of its covering.
“… in fact, Dr. Essley,” the man was saying, “I am under the impression that you are an impostor.”
Essley faced him.

===Chinese===
「有可能。」埃斯利心不在焉地说。他要拖时间。街上空无一人。前面不远处有个门洞，人躺在里面不会被人瞧见，直到警察走过来。
他口袋里藏着一根浸过药的羽毛，用脱脂棉和油绸仔仔细细裹着。他悄悄把它掏出来，双手背在身后，剥去包裹。
「……说实在的，埃斯利博士，」那人正说着，「我的印象是，你是个冒名顶替的骗子。」
埃斯利面向他。

===Original===
“You think too much,” he said in a low voice, “and after all, I do not recognize﻿—turn your face to the light.”
The young man obeyed. It was a moment. Quick as thought the doctor raised the feather﻿ ﻿…
A hand of steel gripped his wrist. As if from the ground, two other men had appeared. Something soft was thrust into his face; a sickly aroma overpowered him. He struggled madly, but the odds were too many, and then a shrill police-whistle sounded and he dropped to the ground﻿ ﻿…
He awoke to find a policeman bending over him. Instinctively he put his hand to his head.

===Chinese===
「你想得太多了。」他低声说，「再说，我并不认——把脸转到亮处来。」
年轻人依言照办。就是这一瞬。快如闪念，大夫举起了羽毛……
一只铁掌扣住了他的手腕。仿佛从地里冒出来似的，又出现了两个人。一团软软的东西捂上他的脸；一股甜腻发闷的香气把他压倒。他疯狂挣扎，可寡不敌众，随后一声尖利的警笛响起，他栽倒在地……
他醒来时，一个警察正俯身看他。他本能地摸了摸头。

===Original===
“Hurt, sir?” asked the man.
“No.”
He struggled to his feet and stood unsteadily.
“Did you capture the men?”

===Chinese===
「受伤了吗，先生？」那人问。
「没有。」
他挣扎着站起来，晃晃悠悠。
「人抓到了吗？」

===Original===
“No, sir, they got away. We just spotted them as they downed you, but, bless your heart, they seemed to be swallowed up by the earth.”
He looked around for the feather: it had disappeared. With some reluctance he gave his name and address to the constable, who called a taxicab.
“You’re sure you’ve lost nothing, sir?” asked the man.
“Nothing,” said Essley testily. “Nothing﻿—look here, constable, do not report this.” He slipped a pound into the man’s hand. “I do not wish this matter to get into the papers.”

===Chinese===
「没有，先生，跑了。他们撂倒您的时候我们刚瞧见，可真是邪了门，他们像被地吞了似的，眨眼就没影了。」
他四下找那根羽毛：不见了。他老大不情愿地向警员报了姓名住址，警员叫来一辆出租车。
「您确定没丢什么吧，先生？」那人问。
「没丢什么。」埃斯利不耐烦地说。「没丢——听我说，警员，这事别上报。」他把一英镑塞进那人手里。「我不想让这事见报。」

===Original===
The constable handed the money back.
“I’m sorry, sir,” he said, “I couldn’t take this even if I was willing.” He looked round quickly and lowered his voice. “I’ve got a gentleman from the Yard with me,” he said, “one of the assistant commissioners.”
Essley followed the direction of the policeman’s eyes. In the shadow of the wall a man was standing.
“He was the chap who saw you first,” said the policeman, young and criminally loquacious.

===Chinese===
警员把钱递了回来。
「对不起，先生，」他说，「就算我肯，这钱也不能收。」他飞快地四下看看，压低了嗓音。「我这儿有位苏格兰场的先生跟着呢，」他说，「助理总监里头的一位。」
埃斯利顺着警察的目光望去。墙影里立着一个人。
「头一个瞧见您的就是这位。」警察说。他年纪轻轻，多话多得不像话。

===Original===
Obeying some impulse he could not define, Essley walked towards the man in the shadow.
“I owe you a debt of gratitude,” he said. “I can only hope that you will add to your kindness by letting the matter drop﻿—I should hate to see the thing referred to in the newspapers.”
“I suppose you would,” said the unknown. He was in evening dress, and the red glow of his cigar rather concealed than defined his face. “But this is a matter, Dr. Essley, where you must allow us full discretion.”
“How do you know my name?” asked the doctor suspiciously.

===Chinese===
受着一股说不清的冲动驱使，埃斯利朝影子里那个人走去。
「我欠您一份人情。」他说。「只盼您好事做到底，就此把这事搁下——我最不愿看见它登上报端。」
「谅必如此。」那不知名的人说。他穿一身晚礼服，雪茄的红光与其说是照亮、不如说是遮暗了他的脸。「不过这件事，埃斯利博士，您得容我们全权处置。」
「你怎么知道我的名字？」大夫疑心地问。

===Original===
The other smiled in the darkness and turned away.
“One moment!”
Essley took a stride forward and peered into the other’s face.
“I seem to recognize your voice,” he said.

===Chinese===
对方在黑暗中笑了笑，转身要走。
「慢着！」
埃斯利抢上一步，细看对方的脸。
「我好像听得出你的嗓音。」他说。

===Original===
“That is possible,” said the other, and pushed him gently, but firmly, away.
Essley gasped. He himself was no weakling, but this man had an arm like steel.
“I think you had better go, sir,” said the police-constable anxiously. He desired neither to offend an obviously influential member of the public nor his superior﻿—that mysterious commissioner who appeared and disappeared in the various divisions and who left behind him innumerable casualties amongst the different members of the force.
“I’ll go,” said the doctor, “but I should like to know this gentleman’s name.”

===Chinese===
「有这个可能。」对方说着，轻而坚决地把他推开。
埃斯利倒抽一口气。他自己并非无缚鸡之力之辈，可这人的一条胳膊像钢打的一般。
「我看您还是走吧，先生。」警员急切地说。他既不想开罪一位显然有身份的公众人士，也不想开罪他的上官——那位在各个分区神出鬼没、身后留下一大批挂彩警员的神秘总监。
「我走。」大夫说，「不过我想知道这位先生的尊姓大名。」

===Original===
“That cannot possibly interest you,” said the stranger, and Essley shrugged his shoulders.
With that he had to be content. He drove home to Forest Hill, thinking, thinking.
Who were these three﻿—what object had they?
Who was the man who had stood in the shadows? Was it possible that his assailants were acting in collusion with the police?

===Chinese===
「那您不会感兴趣的。」陌生人说。埃斯利耸耸肩。
他也只好就此作罢。他坐车回福里斯特山（Forest Hill）的家，一路想，一路想。
这三位是什么人——图的是什么？
立在影子里的那个人又是谁？袭击他的人，会不会同警察串通一气？

===Original===
He was no nearer the solution when he reached his home. He unlocked the door and let himself in. There was nobody in the house but himself and the old woman upstairs.
His comings and goings were so erratic that he had organized a system which allowed him the most perfect freedom of movement.
There must be an end to Dr. Essley, he decided. Essley must disappear from London. He need not warn Black﻿—Black would know.
He would settle the business of the ironmaster and his daughter, and then﻿—there would be a finish.

===Chinese===
到家时，他离答案仍一样远。他开了锁，进门进屋。屋里除了他自己，只有楼上那个老妇人。
他的行止飘忽无常，为此他早安排好一套章法，容他行动来去，自由到极处。
埃斯利博士必须有个了结，他打定了主意。埃斯利必须从伦敦消失。他用不着给布莱克去警告——布莱克自会知道。
他要把铁业巨头同他女儿那桩事办完，然后——就该收场了。

===Original===
He unlocked his study, entered and switched on the lights.
There was a letter on his writing-table, a letter enclosed in a thin grey envelope. He picked it up and examined it. It had been delivered by hand, and bore his name written in a firm hand.
He looked at the writing-table and started back.
*The letter had been written in the room and blotted on the pad!*

===Chinese===
他开了书房的锁，进去，拧亮灯。
写字台上放着一封信，装在一只薄薄的灰色信封里。他拿起来端详。信是专人送来的，信封上以遒劲的笔迹写着他的名字。
他朝写字台看了一眼，猛地退了一步。
*这封信就是在这间屋里写的，墨迹就吸在这块纸垫上！*

===Original===
There was no doubt at all about it. The blotting-paper had been placed there fresh that day, and the reverse of the bold handwriting on the envelope was plain to see.
He looked at the envelope again.
It could not have been a patient: he never admitted patients﻿—he had none worth mentioning. The practice was a blind. Besides, the door had been locked, and he alone had the key. He tore the envelope open and took out the contents. It was a half-sheet of notepaper. The three lines of writing ran﻿—
> “You escaped tonight, and have only seven days to prepare yourself for the fate which awaits you.
> ‘The Four Just Men.’ ”

===Chinese===
这一点毋庸置疑。吸墨纸是当天新换上去的，信封上那手遒劲字迹的反字，在纸垫上看得清清楚楚。
他又看了看信封。
不会是病人：他从不接诊——他的病人也没有值得一提的。行医不过是个幌子。何况门是锁着的，钥匙只他一人有。他撕开信封，抽出信笺。那是半张信纸，三行字这样写道——
> 「你今夜逃脱了。你只有七天，为等着你的命运做准备。
> 『四义士。』」

===Original===
He sank into his chair, crushed by the knowledge.
They were the Just Men﻿—and he had escaped them.
The Just Men! He buried his face in his hands and tried to think. Seven days they gave him. Much could be done in seven days. The terror of death was upon him, he who had without qualm or remorse sent so many on the long journey. But this was he﻿—himself! He clutched at his throat and glared round the room. Essley the poisoner﻿—the expert; a specialist in death﻿—the man who had revived the lost art of the Medicis and had hoodwinked the law. Seven days! Well, he would settle the business of the ironmaster. That was necessary to Black.
He began to make feverish preparations for the future. There were no papers to destroy. He went into the surgery and emptied three bottles down the sink. The fourth he would want. The fourth had been useful to Black: a little green bottle with a glass stopper. He slipped it into his pocket.

===Chinese===
他跌坐在椅子里，被这一认知压垮了。
他们就是义士——而他，竟从他们手里逃脱过。
义士！他把脸埋进手里，竭力思索。他们给了他七天。七天里能做许多事。死亡的恐怖攫住了他——他这个把那么多人送上不归路而从不皱一下眉的人。可如今轮到的是他——他自己！他掐住自己的喉咙，怒目扫视房间。投毒者埃斯利——行家；死亡的专家——复活了美第奇家族失传绝艺、把法律玩弄于股掌之上的人。七天！好，先把铁业巨头的事办了。那对布莱克是必不可少的。
他开始为将来做狂热的准备。没有文件要销毁。他走进诊疗室，把三只瓶子倒进了水池。第四只他还用得着。第四只对布莱克一直大有用处：一只带玻璃塞的小绿瓶。他把它揣进了口袋。

===Original===
He let the tap run to wash away all trace of the drug he had spilt. The bottles he smashed and threw into a waste-bin.
He went upstairs to his room, but he could not sleep. He locked his door and put a chair against it. With a revolver in his hand, he searched the cupboard and beneath the bed. He placed the revolver under his pillow and tried to sleep.
Next morning found him haggard and ill, but none the less he made his toilet with customary care.
Punctually at noon he presented himself at Hampstead and was shown into the drawing-room.

===Chinese===
他放开水龙头，冲走溅出的药液的一切痕迹。瓶子则砸碎，扔进了垃圾桶。
他上楼进了自己的房间，却睡不着。他锁上门，抵上一把椅子。手里握着左轮枪，搜了衣橱和床底。他把枪压在枕头底下，试着入睡。
第二天早晨，他形容枯槁，一身病态，可梳洗起来，仍旧一丝不苟，一如平日的习惯。
正午时分，他分秒不差地在汉普斯特德（Hampstead）登了门，被引进客厅。

===Original===
The girl was alone when he entered. He noted with approval that she was very beautiful.
That May Sandford did not like him he knew by instinct. He saw the cloud come to her pretty face as he came into her presence, and was amused in his cold way.
“My father is out,” she said.
“That is good,” said Essley, “for now we can talk.”

===Chinese===
他进去时，姑娘独自一人。他带着称许注意到，她非常美。
梅·桑德福德（May Sandford）不喜欢他，这一点他凭本能就知道。他看见自己走进时，一朵阴云掠上她俏丽的脸，便以他冷冰冰的方式觉得有趣。
「我父亲出去了。」她说。
「那正好。」埃斯利说，「这下咱们可以谈谈了。」

===Original===
He seated himself without invitation.
“I think it is only right to tell you, Dr. Essley, that my father’s fears regarding me are quite groundless.”
At that moment the ironmaster came in and shook hands warmly with the doctor.
“Well, how do you think she looks?” he asked.

===Chinese===
没人请，他自己坐下了。
「我想应该告诉你，埃斯利博士，我父亲对我的担心全无根据。」
正在这时，铁业巨头走了进来，热情地同大夫握手。
「怎么样，你看她气色如何？」他问。

===Original===
“Looks tell you nothing,” said the other.
It was not the moment for the feather. He had other things to do, and the feather was not the way. He chatted for a while and then rose. “I will send you some medicine,” he said.
She pulled a wry face.
“You need not worry to take it,” he said, with the touch of rancour that was one of his characteristics.

===Chinese===
「气色说明不了什么。」对方说。
现在不是用羽毛的时候。他另有事要办，何况羽毛也非正途。他闲谈了一会儿，起身。「我给你开点药送来。」他说。
她做了个苦脸。
「你放心，用不着真吃。」他说，话里带着一股怨气——那是他的脾性之一。

===Original===
“Can you come to dinner on Tuesday?” asked Sandford.
Essley considered. This was Saturday﻿—three days out of seven, and anything might turn up in the meantime.
“Yes,” he said, “I will come.”
He took a cab to some chambers near the Thames Embankment. He had a most useful room there.

===Chinese===
「星期二能来吃晚饭吗？」桑德福德问。
埃斯利盘算着。今天是星期六——七天里已去了三天，其间什么事都可能冒出来。
「能来。」他说，「我来。」
他叫了辆车，去泰晤士河堤（Thames Embankment）附近的一处公寓套间。他在那儿有一个极有用的房间。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. At a bullfight the seats in the sun are the cheaper, those in the shade being double the price. ↩︎

2. In the story, as it appeared in serial form, the name of the poison occurred. It has been represented to the author (and he agrees) that it is wholly undesirable that the name of this drug should appear in a work of fiction. It is one well known to oculists and its action is faithfully described in these pages. ↩︎

3. Busy: a busy fellow, detective. ↩︎
===Chinese===
1. 斗牛场上，向阳的座位价钱便宜些，背阴的座位则要翻倍。 ↩︎

2. 本篇最初以连载形式发表时，写出了那种毒药的名称。后来有人向作者进言（作者亦表赞同）：这种药物的名称，不宜出现在小说作品里。它是眼科医生都熟知的药物之一，其药效在书中的描述忠实无误。 ↩︎

3. Busy（大忙人）：指忙忙碌碌的家伙，即侦探。 ↩︎

## XVII Justice / 十七 审判

===Original===
“Where are we going?” asked Sir Isaac faintly.

“We are going to Southampton,” growled Black in his ear. “We shall find some friends there.” He grinned in the darkness. Then, leaning forward, he gave instructions in a low tone to the chauffeur.

The car jerked forward and in a few minutes it had crossed Hammersmith Broadway and was speeding towards Barnes.

Scarcely had it cleared the traffic when a long grey racing car cut perilously across the crowded space, dodging with extraordinary agility a number of vehicles, and, unheeding the caustic comments of the drivers, it went on in the same direction as Black’s car had taken.

He had cleared Kingston and was on the Sandown road when he heard the loud purring of a car behind. He turned and looked, expecting to find his second car, but a punctured tyre held Black’s reserve on Putney Heath. Black was a little uneasy, though it was no unusual thing for cars to travel the main Portsmouth road at that hour of the night.
===Chinese===
「我们这是去哪儿？」艾萨克·特兰伯爵士（Sir Isaac Tramber）有气无力地问。

「我们去南安普敦（Southampton）。」布莱克（Black）在他耳边低吼，「到了那儿，自会遇上几位朋友。」他在黑暗中咧嘴一笑，随即俯身向前，低声吩咐司机。

汽车猛地一蹿，不出几分钟便过了哈默史密斯大道（Hammersmith Broadway），朝巴恩斯（Barnes）飞驰而去。

车子刚驶出车流，一辆长长的灰色赛车便凶险地斜插过拥挤的路面，以惊人的灵巧连躲过好几辆车，全不理会司机们的冷嘲热骂，顺着布莱克那辆车去的方向追了下去。

他已过了金斯顿（Kingston），上了桑当（Sandown）路，忽然听得身后一辆汽车低沉的轰鸣。他回头张望，指望看见自己的第二辆车，可那辆车只因爆了一只胎，被撂在了帕特尼荒原（Putney Heath）上。布莱克心里有点发毛，虽说在夜里这个钟点，走朴次茅斯（Portsmouth）大道的汽车本来也不算少。

===Original===
He knew, too, that he could not hope to keep ahead of his pursuer. He caught the unmistakable sound which accompanies the racing car in motion.

“We’ll wait till the road gets a little broader,” he said, “and then we’ll let that chap pass us.”

He conveyed the gist of this intention to the chauffeur.

The car behind showed no disposition to go ahead until Sandown and Cobham had been left behind and the lights of Guildford were almost in sight.

Then, on a lonely stretch of road, two miles from the town, the car, without any perceptible effort, shot level with them and then drew ahead on the off side. Then it slowed, and the touring car had perforce to follow its example.
===Chinese===
他也知道，休想一直甩开追他的人。赛车行驶时特有的那种声响，他听得真真的。

「等路面宽一点，」他说，「咱们再放那家伙过去。」

他把这层打算的大意交代给了司机。

后面那辆车却无意超前，一直挨到桑当和科巴姆（Cobham）都被抛在身后，吉尔福德（Guildford）的灯火几乎在望。

随后，在离城两英里的一段僻静路上，那辆车毫不费力地蹿到与他们并排，又从前头外侧抢出半截。接着它放慢下来，旅行车只得依样减速。

===Original===
Black watched the manoeuvre with some misgiving. Slower and slower went the racing car till it stopped crossways in the road; it stopped, too, in a position which made it impossible for the touring car to pass.

Black’s man drew up with a jerk.

They saw, by the light of their lamps, two men get out of the motor ahead and make what seemed to be a cursory examination of a wheel. Then one walked back, slowly and casually, till he came to where Black and his companion sat.

“Excuse me,” said the stranger. “I think I know you.”

Of a sudden an electric lamp flashed in Black’s face. More to the point, in the spreading rays of the light, clear to be seen was the nickel-plated barrel of a revolver, and it was pointed straight at Black.
===Chinese===
布莱克揣着一肚子疑虑，看完了这套动作。赛车越走越慢，末了横停在路当中；停的位置，也恰恰卡得旅行车无法通过。

布莱克的司机猛地刹住了车。

借着车灯，他们看见前车下来两个人，仿佛漫不经心地查看了一只车轮。然后其中一个往回走来，走得又慢又随意，一直走到布莱克和他同伴的座位旁。

「借光。」陌生人说，「我想我认得你。」

冷不防一支电筒的光打在布莱克脸上。更要紧的是，在散开的光晕里，一管镀镍的左轮枪管看得清清楚楚，正对着布莱克。

===Original===
“You will alight, Mr. Black﻿—you and your companion,” said the unknown calmly.

In the bright light that flooded him, Black could make no move. Without a word he stepped down on to the roadway, his companion following him.

“Go ahead,” said the man with the revolver.

The two obeyed. Another flood of light met them. The driver of the first car was standing up, electric torch in one hand, revolver in the other. He directed them curtly to enter the tonneau. The first of their captors turned to give directions to the chauffeur of the grey touring car, then he sprang into the body in which they sat and took a seat opposite them.

“Put your hands on your knees,” he commanded, as his little lamp played over them.
===Chinese===
「请下车，布莱克先生——你，还有你的同伴。」那来路不明的人平平静静地说。

在泼洒全身的亮光里，布莱克动弹不得。他一言不发地跨到车道上，同伴跟着下来。

「到前面去。」持枪的人说。

两人依言而行。又一片光迎头罩下。头一辆车的司机站起身，一手电筒，一手左轮枪，简短地命他们进后车厢。擒下他们的头一个人转身向灰色旅行车的司机交代了几句，随即跃上他们坐的那节车厢，在他们对面落座。

「手放在膝盖上。」他命令道，手里的小电筒在他们身上扫来扫去。

===Original===
Black brought his gloved hands forward reluctantly. Sir Isaac, half dead with fright, followed his example.

The car moved forward. Their warder, concentrating his lamp upon their knees, kept watch while his companion drove the car forward at a racing pace.

They struck off from the main road and took a narrow country lane which was unfamiliar to Black, and for ten minutes they twisted and turned in what seemed the heart of the country. Then they stopped.

“Get down,” ordered the man with the lamp.

Neither Black nor his friend had spoken one word up till now.
===Chinese===
布莱克不情愿地伸出戴手套的双手。艾萨克爵士吓得半死，也照样做了。

车子开动。看守把电筒光钉在他们的膝盖上，一路监视；他的同伴开着车，以赛车的速度向前疾驰。

他们拐下大路，上一条布莱克不熟的窄窄的乡间小道，在仿佛是荒野腹地的地方七拐八绕地走了十分钟。然后车停了。

「下车。」持灯的人命令。

直到这时，布莱克和他的朋友还一个字也没说过。

===Original===
“What is the game?” asked Black.

“Get down,” commanded the other.

With a curse, the big man descended.

There were two other men waiting for them.

“I suppose this is the ‘Four Just Men’ farce,” said Black with a sneer.
===Chinese===
「这是演的哪一出？」布莱克问。

「下车。」对方命令。

大个子咒骂一声，下了车。

另有两个人正等在那里。

「我想，这就是『四义士』（the Four Just Men）的闹剧吧。」布莱克冷笑一声。

===Original===
“That you shall learn,” said one of those who were waiting.

They were conducted by a long, rough path through a field, through a little copse, until ahead of them in the night loomed a small building.

It was in darkness. It gave Black the impression of being a chapel. He had little time to take any note of its construction. He heard Sir Isaac’s quick breathing behind him and the snick of a lock. The hand that held his arm now relaxed.

“Stay where you are,” said a voice.

Black waited. There was growing in his heart a sickly fear of what all this signified.
===Chinese===
「是不是闹剧，你自会知道。」等候的人里有一个说。

有人领着他们走上一条长长的崎岖小径，穿过一块田野，穿过一片小树丛，夜色里，一座小小的建筑在前方隐隐耸现。

楼里一片漆黑。它给布莱克的印象是一座小教堂。他来不及细看它的格局，只听见身后艾萨克爵士急促的呼吸，和一把锁咔哒一响。扣着他手臂的那只手松开了。

「站在原地别动。」一个声音说。

布莱克等着。这一切究竟意味着什么，一股虚弱的恐惧在他心里越涨越高。

===Original===
“Step forward,” said a voice.

Black moved two steps forward and suddenly the big room in which he stood blazed with light. He raised his hand to protect his eyes from the dazzling glow.

The sight he saw was a remarkable one. He was in a chapel; he saw the stained-glass windows, but in place of the altar there was a low platform which ran along one end of the building.

It was draped with black and set with three desks. It reminded him of nothing so much as a judge’s desk, save that the hangings were of purple, the desks of black oak, and the carpet that covered the dais of the same sombre hue.

Three men sat at the desks. They were masked, and a diamond pin in the cravat of one glittered in the light of the huge electrolier which hung from the vaulted roof. Gonsalez had a weakness for jewels.
===Chinese===
「上前一步。」一个声音说。

布莱克向前走了两步，他站立的大屋骤然灯火通明。他抬手遮住眼睛，挡开那炫目的光。

眼前的景象非同寻常。他正身处一座小教堂；他看见了彩绘玻璃窗，可祭坛的位置上，却是一座低台，横贯建筑的一端。

台上蒙着黑幔，摆着三张书案。这让他想起的，莫过于法官的公案台了——只是帷幔是紫色的，书案是黑橡木的，铺在高台上的地毯，也是同样阴沉的颜色。

三张案后坐着三个人。都戴着面罩，其中一人领结上的钻石别针，在拱顶垂下的大吊灯光下熠熠生辉。冈萨雷斯（Gonsalez）素来偏爱珠宝。

===Original===
The remaining member of the Four was to the right of the prisoners.

With the stained-glass windows, the raftered roof, and the solemn character of the architecture, the illusion of the chapel ended. There was no other furniture on the floor; it was tiled and bare of chair or pew.

Black took all this in quickly. He noted a door behind the three, through which they came and apparently made their exit. He could see no means of escape save by the way he had come.

The central figure of the three at the desk spoke in a voice which was harsh and stern and uncompromising.

“Morris Black,” he said solemnly, “what of Fanks?”
===Chinese===
四义士余下的一员，在囚徒的右手边。

彩绘玻璃窗、椽木屋顶，还有那建筑庄严的品格——到这一层为止，小教堂的幻象便完了。地上再无别的陈设；地面铺着砖，没有一把椅子，也没有一条长凳。

布莱克飞快地把这一切收进眼里。他注意到三人身后有一扇门，他们从那里进来，显然也从那里退场。除了进来的那条路，他看不出别的脱身之道。

案后三人居中那位开口了，嗓音严厉、冷硬、不留余地。

「莫里斯·布莱克（Morris Black），」他一字一顿地说，「法恩克斯（Fanks）的下落，你怎么说？」

===Original===
Black shrugged his shoulders and looked round as though weary of a question which he found it impossible to answer.

“What of Jakobs, of Coleman, of a dozen men who have stood in your way and have died?” asked the voice.

Still Black was silent.

His eye took in the situation. Behind him were two doors, and he observed that the key was in the lock. He could see that he was in an old Norman chapel which private enterprise had restored for a purpose.

The door was modern and of the usual “churchy” type.
===Chinese===
布莱克耸耸肩，四下张望，仿佛对一个答不上来的问题感到了厌倦。

「那么雅各布斯（Jakobs）呢，科尔曼（Coleman）呢，还有那一打挡了你的路、又丢了性命的人呢？」那声音问。

布莱克依旧不吭声。

他把形势看在眼里。身后有两扇门，他看见钥匙就插在锁眼里。他看明白了：这是一座古老的诺曼式小教堂，被某种私人营生为了某种目的翻修过。

门倒是现代的，是常见的「教堂派」样式。

===Original===
“Isaac Tramber,” said Number One, “what part have you played?”

“I don’t know,” stammered Sir Isaac. “I am as much in the dark as you are. I think the bucket-shop idea is perfectly beastly. Now look here, is there anything else I can tell you, because I am most anxious to get out of this affair with clean hands?”

He made a step forward and Black reached out a hand to restrain him, but was pulled back by the man at his side.

“Come here,” said Number One. His knees shaking under him, Sir Isaac walked quickly up the aisle floor.

“I’ll do anything I can,” he said eagerly, as he stood like a penitent boy before the master’s figure. “Any information I can give you I shall be most happy to give.”
===Chinese===
「艾萨克·特兰伯，」一号说，「你在这中间扮演了什么角色？」

「我不知道。」艾萨克爵士结结巴巴地说，「我同你们一样，两眼一抹黑。对赌行这档子事，我只觉得恶劣透顶。这么着吧，还有没有别的我能奉告的？因为我一心一意，只想干干净净地从这桩事里脱身。」

他迈前一步，布莱克伸手去拦，却被身旁的人拉了回去。

「到这儿来。」一号说。艾萨克爵士双膝发颤，快步走上通廊。

「凡是我办得到的，我都肯做。」他急切地说，像悔过的学童立在师长跟前，「凡是我知道的消息，我都乐意奉告。」

===Original===
“Stop!” roared Black. His face was livid with rage. “Stop,” he said hoarsely, “you don’t know what you’re doing, Ikey. Keep your mouth shut and stand by me and you’ll not suffer.”

“There is only one thing I know,” Sir Isaac went on, “and that is that Black had a bit of a row with Fanks﻿—”

The words were scarcely out of his mouth when three shots rang out in rapid succession. The Four had not attempted to disarm Black. With lightning-like rapidity he had whipped out his Browning pistol and had fired at the traitor.

In a second he was at the door. An instant later the key was turned and he was through.

“Shoot﻿—shoot, Manfred,” said a voice from the dais. But they were too late﻿—Black had vanished into the darkness. As the two men sprang after him, they stood for a moment silhouetted against the light from the chapel within.
===Chinese===
「住口！」布莱克怒吼，脸气得铁青。「住口，」他嘶哑地说，「你不知道你在干什么，艾基（Ikey）。闭上嘴，站在我这边，你就不会吃苦头。」

「我只知道一件事，」艾萨克爵士接着说，「就是布莱克同法恩克斯拌过一回嘴——」

话音未落，三声枪响接连炸开。四义士没有试图缴布莱克的械。他闪电般抽出勃朗宁手枪，朝那叛徒开了火。

转眼他已扑到门边。再一瞬，钥匙一转，他已夺门而出。

「开枪——开枪，乔治·曼弗雷德（Manfred）！」高台上一个声音喊道。可是迟了——布莱克已经消失在黑暗里。两人跃起追出时，身影在教堂内的灯光衬托下凝立了一瞬。

===Original===
*Crack! crack!* A nickel bullet struck the stone supports of the doorway and covered them with fine dust and splinters of stone.

“Put the lights out and follow,” said Manfred quickly.

He was too late, for Black had a start, and the fear and hatred in him lent him unsuspected speed.

The brute instinct in him led him across the field unerringly. He reached the tiny road, fumed to the left, and found the grey racing car waiting, unattended.

He sprang to the crank and turned it. He was in the driver’s seat in an instant. He had to take risks﻿—there might be ditches on either side of the road, but he turned the wheel over till it almost locked and brought his foot down over the pedal.
===Chinese===
*砰！砰！*一发镍弹打在门道的石柱上，激得石屑纷飞，撒了两人一身。

「灭灯，跟我来。」曼弗雷德急促地说。

还是迟了，布莱克抢了先，心里的恐惧与仇恨，给了他谁也料想不到的速度。

他体内的兽性本能领着他，丝毫不差地穿过田野。他上了那条窄路，向左一拐，看见那辆灰色赛车正候在那里，无人看管。

他扑到摇柄前，摇动了引擎，转眼便坐进驾驶座。他只能冒险——路两边也许有沟，但他把方向盘打到几乎锁死，一脚踩下踏板。

===Original===
The car jumped forward, lurched to the side, recovered itself, and went bumping and crashing along the road.

“It’s no good,” said Manfred. He saw the taillights of the car disappearing. “Let’s get back.”

He had slipped off his mask.

They raced back to the chapel. The lights were on again. Sir Isaac Tramber lay stone-dead on the floor. The bullet had struck him in the left shoulder and had passed through this heart.

But it was not to him they looked. Number One lay still and motionless on the floor in a pool of blood.
===Chinese===
汽车猛蹿出去，歪向一侧，又稳住，一路磕磕撞撞、轰隆作响地沿路狂奔。

「追不上了。」曼弗雷德说。他望着那车的尾灯远去。「回去吧。」

他已经摘下了面罩。

他们奔回小教堂。灯又亮了。艾萨克·特兰伯爵士直挺挺地死在地板上。子弹打进他的左肩，穿透了他的心脏。

可他们顾不上看他。一号一动不动地躺在地板上，身下一摊血。

===Original===
“Look to the injury,” he said, “and unless it is fatal do not unmask me.”

Poiccart and Gonsalez made a brief examination of the wound.

“It’s pretty serious.”

In this terse sentence they summarized their judgment.

“I thought it was,” said the wounded man quietly. “You had better get on to Southampton. He’ll probably pick up Fellowe”﻿—he smiled through his mask﻿—“I suppose I ought to call him Lord Francis Ledborough now. He’s a nephew of mine and a sort of a police-commissioner himself. I wired him to follow me. You might pick up his car and go on together. Manfred can stay with me. Take this mask off.”
===Chinese===
「先看伤，」他说，「只要不是致命的，就别摘我的面罩。」

波伊卡特（Poiccart）和冈萨雷斯草草查验了伤口。

「相当重。」

他们的判断，尽在这简短的一句里。

「我料着也是。」伤者平静地说，「你们最好赶去南安普敦。他多半会接上费洛（Fellowe）」——他隔着面罩笑了笑——「我想，这会儿我该称呼他弗朗西斯·莱德伯勒勋爵（Lord Francis Ledborough）了。他是我一个侄子，自己也算得上一位警察局长。我拍了电报叫他跟着我。你们可以同他的车会合，一道追下去。曼弗雷德留下陪我。把面罩摘了吧。」

===Original===
Gonsalez stooped down and gently removed the silk half-mask. Then he started back.

“Lord Verlond!” he exclaimed with surprise, and Manfred, who knew, nodded.

---

The road was clear of traffic at this hour of the night. It was dark and none too wide in places for a man who had not touched the steering-wheel of a car for some years, but Black, bareheaded, sat and drove the big machine ahead without fear of consequences. Once he went rocking through a little town at racing speed.

A policeman who attempted to hold him up narrowly escaped with his life. Black reached open road again with no injury save a shattered mudguard that had caught a lamppost on a sharp turn. He went through Winchester at top speed﻿—again there was an attempt to stop him. Two big wagons had been drawn up in the main street, but he saw them in time and took a side turning, and cleared the town again more by good luck than otherwise. He knew now that his flight was known to the police. He must change his plans. He admitted to himself that he had few plans to change: he had arranged to leave England by one of two ports, Dover or Southampton.
===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯俯下身，轻轻揭下那半截丝面罩，随即倒抽一口冷气，直起了身子。

「弗隆德勋爵（Lord Verlond）！」他惊呼出声。曼弗雷德早知就里，只点了点头。

---

夜里这个钟点，路上已无车马。天黑，有几段路又嫌窄，对一个多年没碰过方向盘的人来说本不容易，可布莱克光着头，坐着把大车往前开，什么后果都顾不得了。有一回，他摇摇晃晃地以赛车的速度冲过一座小镇。

一个试图拦他的警察险些送了命。布莱克重新上了空旷的大路，只坏了一只挡泥板——急转弯时蹭上了路灯柱——别的毫发无损。他以最高速度冲过温彻斯特（Winchester）——那里又有人试图拦他。两辆大货车横在大街上，好在他及时发现，拐进一条小巷，多半靠运气，才又出了城。他现在明白了：警方已经知道他出逃。他必须改变计划。他也不得不承认，他没什么计划可改：他原打算从两个口岸之一离境——多佛（Dover），或是南安普敦。

===Original===
He had hoped to reach the Havre boat without attracting attention, but that was now out of the question. The boats would be watched, and he had no disguise which would help him.

Eight miles south of Winchester he overtook another car and passed it before he realized that this must be the second car he had hired. With the realization came two reports﻿—the front tyres of his car had punctured.

His foot pressed on the brake and he slowed the car to a standstill.

Here was luck! To come to grief at the very spot where his relief was at hand!

He jumped out of the car and stood revealed in the glare of the lamps of the oncoming car, his arms outstretched.
===Chinese===
他原指望不动声色地赶上勒阿弗尔（Havre）的船，如今想也别想了。各处码头必有盯守，他手里又没有一件帮得上忙的化装。

温彻斯特以南八英里，他追上一辆车，超了过去，随即才醒悟：这必是他雇的第二辆车。念头刚到，两声闷响随之而来——他的前胎爆了。

他一脚踩下刹车，把车缓缓停死。

这不是天助吗！偏偏在救兵近在咫尺的地方抛了锚！

他跳下车，站在对面来车耀眼的灯光里，张开双臂。

===Original===
The car drew up within a few feet of him.

“Take me on to Southampton; I have broken down,” he said, and the chauffeur said something unintelligible.

Black opened the door of the car and stepped in. The door slammed behind him before he was aware that there were other occupants.

“Who﻿—?” he began.
===Chinese===
那车在离他几英尺的地方停住。

「捎我去南安普敦，我的车坏了。」他说。司机咕哝了一句什么，听不真切。

布莱克拉开车门跨进去。车门在他身后砰地关上，他这才发觉车里另有其人。

「谁——？」他刚开口。

===Original===
Then two hands seized him, something cold and hard snapped on his wrists, and a familiar voice said:

“I am Lord Francis Ledborough, an assistant-commissioner of police, and I shall take you into custody on a charge of wilful murder.”

“Ledborough?” repeated Black dully.

“You know me best as Constable Fellowe,” said the voice.
===Chinese===
随即两只手抓住他，一样又冷又硬的东西咔哒扣上手腕，一个熟悉的声音说：

「我是弗朗西斯·莱德伯勒勋爵，警察副总监，我以蓄意谋杀的罪名逮捕你。」

「莱德伯勒？」布莱克木然地重复。

「你平时最熟悉的名字，是费洛警员。」那声音说。

===Original===
---

Black was hanged at Pentonville gaol on the 27th of March, 19﻿—, and Lord Francis Ledborough, sitting by the side of an invalid uncle’s bed, read such meagre descriptions as were given to the press.

“Did you know him, sir?” he asked.

The old earl turned fretfully.
===Chinese===
---

布莱克于 19——年 3 月 27 日在彭顿维尔监狱（Pentonville gaol）被处以绞刑。弗朗西斯·莱德伯勒勋爵坐在久病未愈的叔父床边，读着报上那几行寥寥的报道。

「您认识他吗，爵爷？」他问。

老伯爵烦躁地翻了个身。

===Original===
“Know him?” he snarled. “Of course I knew him; he is the only friend of mine that has ever been hanged.”

“Where did you meet him?” persisted a sceptical A.C. of Police.

“I never met him,” said the old man grimly, “he met me.”

And he made a little grimace, for the wound in his shoulder was still painful.
===Chinese===
「认识？」他咆哮道，「当然认识；他是我朋友里唯一被绞死的。」

「您在哪儿认识他的？」这位满腹狐疑的警察副总监追问。

「我从没认识过他，」老头儿冷冷地说，「是他认识了我。」

说着他做了个小鬼脸——肩上的伤还在疼。

## IX Lord Verlond Gives Dinner / 九 弗隆德勋爵设宴

===Original===
Lord Verlond was an afternoon visitor at the Sandford establishment. He had come for many reasons, not the least of which nobody expected. He was a large shareholder in the Sandford Foundries, and with rumours of amalgamation in the air there was excuse enough for his visit. Doubly so, it seemed, when the first person he met was a large, yellow-faced man, confoundedly genial (in the worst sense of the word) and too ready to fraternize for the old man’s liking.

“I have heard of you, my lord,” said Colonel Black.

“For the love of Heaven, don’t call me ‘my lord’!” snapped the earl. “Man alive, you are asking me to be rude to you!”

But no man of Verlond’s standing could be rude to the colonel, with his mechanical smile and his beaming eye.

“I know a friend of yours, I think,” he said, in that soothing tone which in a certain type of mind passes for deference.
===Chinese===
弗隆德勋爵（Lord Verlond）下午造访了桑德福德府。他来的缘由很多，其中最要紧的一条，谁也没有想到。他是桑德福德铸造厂（Sandford Foundries）的大股东，如今合并的传闻满天飞，他这一趟来，理由尽够了。而他进门遇见的第一个人，是个面色蜡黄的大块头，热络得要命（这词取最坏的意思），亲热得叫老头儿吃不消——如此看来，理由更是加倍地够了。

「久仰大名，爵爷。」布莱克上校（Colonel Black）说。

「看在老天的份上，别叫我『爵爷』！」伯爵厉声道，「老兄，你这是逼我对你失礼！」

可是凭弗隆德的身份，对这个笑容机械、目光灼灼的上校，还真失礼不起来。

「我想，我认识你的一位朋友。」他用那种抚慰的腔调说——在某一种脑子里，这腔调就当作恭敬。

===Original===
“You know Ikey Tramber, which is not the same thing,” said the earl.

Colonel Black made a noise indicating his amusement.

“He always﻿—” he began.

“He always speaks well of me and says what a fine fellow I am, and how the earth loses its savour if he passes a day without seeing me,” assisted Lord Verlond, his eyes alight with pleasant malice, “and he tells you what a good sportsman I am, and what a true and kindly heart beats behind my somewhat unprepossessing exterior, and how if people only knew me they would love me﻿—he says all this, doesn’t he?”

Colonel Black bowed.
===Chinese===
「你认识的是艾基·特兰伯（Ikey Tramber），那可不是一回事。」伯爵说。

布莱克上校发出一声表示愉快的动静。

「他老是——」他起了个头。

「他老是把我说得天花乱坠，说我是个多么好的家伙，说他哪天见不着我，人间便要失了滋味。」弗隆德勋爵替他接了下去，眼里闪着愉快的恶意，「他还告诉你，我是个多么有风度的运动家，说我这副不太讨喜的外表底下，跳动着一颗多么真诚仁厚的心，还说人们只要了解我，就一定会爱我——他说的是这一套，对不对？」

布莱克上校鞠了一躬。

===Original===
“I don’t think!” said Lord Verlond vulgarly.

He looked at the other for a while.

“You shall come to dinner with me tonight﻿—you will meet a lot of people who will dislike you intensely.”

“I shall be delighted,” murmured the colonel.

He was hoping that in the conference which he guessed would be held between Sandford and his lordship he would be invited to participate.
===Chinese===
「我才不信呢！」弗隆德勋爵粗声粗气地说。

他打量了对方一会儿。

「今晚你来我家吃饭——你会遇见一大堆人，个个对你厌恶透顶。」

「不胜荣幸。」上校喃喃道。

他巴望着，桑德福德同爵爷之间想必要开的那场会议，会请他列席。

===Original===
In this, however, he was disappointed. He might have taken his leave there and then, but he chose to stay and discuss art (which he imperfectly understood) with a young and distracted lady who was thinking about something else all the time.

She badly wanted to bring the conversation round to the Metropolitan police force, in the hope that a rising young constable might be mentioned. She would have asked after him, but her pride prevented her. Colonel Black himself did not broach the subject.

He was still discussing lost pictures when Lord Verlond emerged from the study with Sandford.

“Let your daughter come,” the earl was saying.

Sandford was undecided.
===Chinese===
这一点他落了空。他本可以当时就告辞，却宁可留下，同一位年轻而心不在焉的小姐大谈艺术（这门学问他懂得不甚了了），而小姐自始至终想着别的。

她一心想把话头引到首都警察队（Metropolitan police force）上，盼着能提到某位前程看好的年轻警员。她本想打听打听他，无奈自尊不许。布莱克上校自己也没有提起这个话题。

他还在谈论失传名画，弗隆德勋爵同桑德福德一起从书房里出来了。

「让你女儿来吧。」伯爵正说着。

桑德福德犹豫不决。

===Original===
“I’m greatly obliged﻿—I should not like her to go alone.”

Something leapt inside Colonel Black’s bosom. A chance﻿ ﻿… !

“If you are talking of the dinner tonight,” he said with an assumption of carelessness, “I shall be happy to call in my car for you.”

Still Sandford was not easy in his mind. It was May who should make the decision.

“I think I’d like to, daddy,” she said.
===Chinese===
「太承您的好意了——只是我不想让她一个人去。」

布莱克上校心里什么东西猛地一跳。一个机会……！

「要是二位说的是今晚的宴席，」他装出满不在乎的口气说，「我十分乐意开我的车来接你。」

桑德福德心里仍不踏实。该拿主意的是梅（May）。

「我想我愿意去，爸爸。」她说。

===Original===
She did not greatly enjoy the prospect of going anywhere with the colonel, but it would only be a short journey.

“If I could stand *in loco parentis* to the young lady,” said Black, nearly jocular, “I should esteem it an honour.”

He looked round and caught a curious glint in Lord Verlond’s eyes. The earl was watching him closely, eagerly almost, and a sudden and unaccountable fear gripped the financier’s heart.

“Excellent, excellent!” murmured the old man, still watching him through lowered lids. “It isn’t far to go, and I think you’ll stand the journey well.”

The girl smiled, but the grim fixed look on the earl’s face did not relax.
===Chinese===
同上校同路，她并不怎么乐意，好在路不远。

「倘若我能以*代行父职*（in loco parentis）的身份陪小姐同行，」布莱克几乎是打趣的口吻，「那真是我的荣幸。」

他环顾四周，撞见弗隆德勋爵眼里一缕古怪的光。伯爵正紧紧盯着他，几乎是急切地盯着，一阵说不清来由的恐惧，攥住了这位金融家的心。

「好极了，好极了！」老头儿喃喃道，仍眯着眼看他，「路不算远，我想你一路撑得住。」

姑娘笑了，伯爵脸上那副冷硬专注的神情却纹丝未松。

===Original===
“As you are an invalid, young lady,” he went on, despite May’s laughing protest﻿—“as you’re an invalid, young lady, I will have Sir James Bower and Sir Thomas Bigland to meet you﻿—you know those eminent physicians, colonel? Your Dr. Essley will, at any rate﻿—experts both on the action of vegetable alkaloids.”

Great beads of sweat stood on Black’s face, but his features were under perfect control. Fear and rage glowed in his eyes, but he met the other’s gaze defiantly. He smiled even﻿—a slow, laboured smile.

“That puts an end to any objection,” he said almost gaily.

The old man took his leave and was grinning to himself all the way back to town.

The Earl of Verlond was a stickler for punctuality: a grim, bent old man, with a face that, so Society said, told eloquently the story of his life, his bitter tongue was sufficient to maintain for him the respect﻿—or if not the respect, the fear that so ably substitutes respect﻿—of his friends.
===Chinese===
「既然小姐是个病人，」他不理梅笑着抗议，继续说——「既然小姐是个病人，我还要请詹姆斯·鲍尔爵士（Sir James Bower）和托马斯·比格兰爵士（Sir Thomas Bigland）来见见你——上校，你认识这两位名医吧？无论如何，你那位埃斯利博士（Dr. Essley）是认识的——两位都是植物碱作用的权威。」
