大颗大颗的汗珠子立在布莱克脸上，可他的五官控制得纹丝不乱。眼里燃着恐惧和狂怒，他却挑衅地迎住对方的目光。他甚至还笑了笑——一个缓慢的、费劲的笑。

「这下再没什么可推辞的了。」他几乎是欢快地说。

老人告辞而去，一路回城，一路自己咧着嘴乐。

弗隆德伯爵最讲究守时：一个严峻佝偻的老头儿，据社交界说，那张脸把他一生的故事讲得有声有色；他那条毒舌，足以为他在朋友中间维系住敬意——或者说，维系住那种足以漂亮地顶替敬意的畏惧。

===Original===
“Friends” is a word which you would never ordinarily apply to any of the earl’s acquaintances. He had apparently no friends save Sir Isaac Tramber.

“I have people to dine with me,” he had said cynically when this question of friendship was once discussed by one who knew him sufficiently well to deal with so intimate a subject.

That night he was waiting in the big library of Carnarvon Place. The earl was one of those men who observed a rigid timetable every day of his life.

He glanced at his watch; in two minutes he would be on his way to the drawing-room to receive his guests.

Horace Gresham was coming. A curious invitation, Sir Isaac Tramber had thought, and had ventured to remark as much, presuming his friendship.
===Chinese===
「朋友」这个词，你通常绝不会用在伯爵的哪个相识身上。他看起来除了艾萨克·特兰伯爵士（Sir Isaac Tramber），再没有一个朋友。

「我有的是人陪我吃饭。」当年有人同他熟到可以谈论这样贴心的话题，提起友谊二字，他就是这句刻薄的回答。

当晚，他在卡那封广场（Carnarvon Place）宅邸的大书房里候着。伯爵属于那种人：一生每一天都恪守一张铁打的时刻表。

他看了看表；再过两分钟，他就该去客厅迎客了。

霍勒斯·格雷沙姆（Horace Gresham）也要来。艾萨克·特兰伯爵士觉得这请柬发得蹊跷，仗着交情，斗胆把这话说了出来。

===Original===
“When I want your advice as to my invitation list, Ikey,” said the earl, “I will send you a prepaid telegram.”

“I thought you hated him,” grumbled Sir Isaac.

“Hate him! Of course I hate him. I hate everybody. I should hate you, but you are such an insignificant devil,” said the earl. “Have you made your peace with Mary?”

“I don’t know what you mean by ‘making my peace,’ ” said Sir Isaac complainingly. “I tried to be amiable to her, and I only seemed to succeed in making a fool of myself.”

“Ah!” said the nobleman with a little chuckle, “she would like you best natural.”
===Chinese===
「等我请教你我该请谁不该请谁的时候，艾基（Ikey），」伯爵说，「我会给你拍一张预付回费的电报。」

「我以为你讨厌他。」艾萨克爵士嘟囔。

「讨厌他！当然讨厌。我谁都讨厌。我也该讨厌你，可你这家伙实在无足轻重。」伯爵说，「你同玛丽（Mary）讲和了没有？」

「我不懂您说的『讲和』是什么意思。」艾萨克爵士委屈地说，「我尽力对她和颜悦色，结果只显得自己像个傻子。」

「啊！」贵人不由轻笑一声，「她最喜欢的，是你不加修饰的本来面目。」

===Original===
Sir Isaac shot a scowling glance at his patron.

“I suppose you know,” he said, “that I want to marry Mary.”

“I know that you want some money without working for it,” said the earl. “You have told me about it twice. I am not likely to forget it. It is the sort of thing I think about at nights.”

“I wish you wouldn’t pull my leg,” growled the baronet. “Are you waiting for any other guests?”

“No,” snarled the earl, “I am sitting on the top of Mont Blanc eating rice pudding.”
===Chinese===
艾萨克爵士恶狠狠地瞪了庇护人一眼。

「我想您是知道的，」他说，「我想娶玛丽。」

「我知道你想不劳而获弄笔钱。」伯爵说，「这话你同我讲过两遍了。我不至于忘。这正是我夜里翻来覆去想的那类事。」

「您就别拿我寻开心了。」从男爵咆哮道，「您还在等别的客人？」

「不等。」伯爵怒道，「我这会儿正坐在勃朗峰顶上吃大米布丁呢。」

===Original===
There was no retort to this.

“I’ve invited quite an old friend of yours,” said the earl suddenly, “but it doesn’t look as if he was turning up.”

Ikey frowned.

“Old friend?”

The other nodded. “Military gent,” he said laconically. “A colonel in the army, though nobody knows the army.”
===Chinese===
这话没法回嘴。

「我还请了你相当老的一位朋友。」伯爵忽然说，「不过看样子他是不来了。」

艾基皱起眉头。

「老朋友？」

对方点头。「一位军爷。」他言简意赅地说，「说是陆军上校，只是这支陆军谁都没听说过。」

===Original===
Sir Isaac’s jaw dropped.

“Not Black?”

Lord Verlond nodded. He nodded several times, like a gleeful child confessing a fault of which it was inordinately proud.

“Black it is,” he said, but made no mention of the girl.

He looked at his watch again and pulled a little face.
===Chinese===
艾萨克爵士的下巴掉了下来。

「不是布莱克吧？」

弗隆德勋爵点头。他点了好几下，活像个干了坏事还得意洋洋的孩子，正招认那件错处。

「正是布莱克。」他说，对那姑娘却只字不提。

他又看了看表，做了个小鬼脸。

===Original===
“Stay here,” he commanded. “I’m going to telephone.”

“Can I﻿—”

“You can’t!” snapped the earl.

He was gone some time, and when he returned to the library there was a smile on his face.

“Your pal’s not coming,” he said, and offered no explanation either for the inexplicable behaviour of the colonel or for his amusement.
===Chinese===
「在这儿待着。」他吩咐，「我去打个电话。」

「我能——」

「你不能！」伯爵厉声道。

他去了好一会儿，回到书房时，脸上带着笑。

「你那位好朋友不来了。」他说。至于上校这莫名其妙的举动，以及他自己这份高兴，他一概不作解释。

===Original===
At dinner Horace Gresham found himself seated next to the most lovely woman in the world. She was also the kindest and the easiest to amuse. He was content to forget the world, and such of the world who were gathered about the earl, but Lord Verlond had other views.

“Met a friend of yours today,” he said abruptly and addressing Horace.

“Indeed, sir?” The young man was politely interested.

“Sandford﻿—that terribly prosperous gentleman from Newcastle.”

Horace nodded cautiously.
===Chinese===
宴席上，霍勒斯·格雷沙姆发现自己坐在世上最可爱的女人旁边。她也是最和善、最容易逗乐的一个。他心满意足，只想忘掉整个世界，连同聚在伯爵身边的这一小圈世界；可弗隆德勋爵另有一番盘算。

「今天遇见你一位朋友。」他没头没脑地冲霍勒斯说。

「是吗，爵爷？」年轻人礼貌地表示兴趣。

「桑德福德——纽卡斯尔（Newcastle）来的那位发达得吓人的绅士。」

霍勒斯谨慎地点头。

===Original===
“Friend of yours too, ain’t he?” The old man turned swiftly to Sir Isaac. “I asked his daughter to come to dinner﻿—father couldn’t come. She ain’t here.”

He glared round the table for the absent girl.

“In a sense Sandford is a friend of mine,” said Sir Isaac no less cautiously, since he must make a statement in public without exactly knowing how the elder man felt on the subject of the absent guests; “at least, he’s a friend of a friend.”

“Black,” snarled Lord Verlond, “bucket-shop swindler﻿—are you in it?”

“I have practically severed my connection with him,” Sir Isaac hastened to say.
===Chinese===
「也是你的朋友吧，呃？」老头儿倏地转向艾萨克爵士，「我请了他女儿来赴宴——父亲来不了。她也没来。」

他环视满桌，瞪着寻找那位缺席的姑娘。

「从某种意义上说，桑德福德是我的朋友。」艾萨克爵士同样谨慎地说，因为当众表态之前，他还不摸老人对缺席客人这件事的底；「至少，是朋友的朋友。」

「布莱克，」弗隆德勋爵怒道，「对赌行的骗子——你有没有份？」

「我同他实际上早已断了来往。」艾萨克爵士赶忙说。

===Original===
Verlond grinned.

“That means he’s broke,” he said, and turned to Horace. “Sandford’s full of praise for a policeman who’s mad keen on his girl﻿—friend of yours?”

Horace nodded.

“He’s a great friend of mine,” he said quietly.

“Who is he?”
===Chinese===
弗隆德咧嘴一笑。

「那就是说他没钱了。」他说着转向霍勒斯，「桑德福德对一个警察赞不绝口，说那人迷他家闺女迷得发疯——你朋友？」

霍勒斯点头。

「他是我的挚友。」他平静地说。

「他是谁？」

===Original===
“Oh, he’s a policeman,” said Horace.

“And I suppose he’s got two legs and a head and a pair of arms,” said the earl. “You’re too full of information﻿—I know he’s a policeman. Everybody seems to be talking about him. Now, what does he do, where does he come from﻿—what the devil does it all mean?”

“I’m afraid I can’t give you any information,” said Horace. “The only thing that I am absolutely certain about in my own mind is that he is a gentleman.”

“A gentleman and a policeman?” asked the earl incredulously.

Horace nodded.
===Chinese===
「哦，他是个警察。」霍勒斯说。

「我猜他还有两条腿、一颗脑袋、一双手臂。」伯爵说，「你知道的也太多了——我知道他是警察。人人都在议论他。那么，他是干什么的，什么来路——这一切究竟是个什么名堂？」

「恐怕我提供不了什么情况。」霍勒斯说，「我自己心里绝对有把握的只有一条：他是个绅士。」

「绅士，还是警察？」伯爵将信将疑。

霍勒斯点头。

===Original===
“A new profession for the younger son, eh?” remarked Lord Verlond sardonically. “No more running away and joining the army; no more serving before the mast; no more cow-punching on the pampas﻿—”

A look of pain came into Lady Mary’s eyes. The old lord swung round on her.

“Sorry,” he growled. “I wasn’t thinking of that young fool. No more dashing away to the ends of the earth for the younger son; no dying picturesquely in the Cape Mounted Rifles, or turning up at an appropriate hour with a bag of bullion under each arm to save the family from ruin. Join the police force, that’s the game. You ought to write a novel about that: a man who can write letters to the sporting papers can write anything.”

“By the way,” he added, “I am coming down to Lincoln on Tuesday to see that horse of yours lose.”

“You will make your journey in vain,” said Horace. “I have arranged for him to win.”
===Chinese===
「次子们的新行当，呃？」弗隆德勋爵讥诮地说，「再不用离家出走投军了；再不用去当水手了；再不用上南美草原赶牛了——」

玛丽女勋爵（Lady Mary）眼里掠过一丝痛楚。老勋爵猛地转向她。

「对不住。」他瓮声瓮气地说，「我没想着那个小傻子。次子再不用天边海角地乱闯；再不用死在开普山地步枪队里，死得如画似诗；也再不用掐着恰到好处的时辰，腋下各夹一袋金条露面，救全家于倾家荡产。去当警察，这才是正经出路。这题材你该写本小说：能给体育报写稿的人，什么写不出来。」

「对了，」他又说，「星期二我要下林肯（Lincoln）去，看你那匹马吃败仗。」

「您这趟要白跑了。」霍勒斯说，「我已经安排好了，它是要赢的。」

===Original===
He waited later for an opportunity to say a word in private to the old man. It did not come till the end of the dinner, when he found himself alone with the earl.

“By the way,” he said, with an assumption of carelessness, “I want to see you on urgent private business.”

“Want money?” asked the earl, looking at him suspiciously from underneath his shaggy brows.

Horace smiled.

“No, I don’t think I am likely to borrow money,” he said.
===Chinese===
席间他一直寻机会同老头儿单独说句话。直到宴席末了，他才总算同伯爵单独待在一处。

「顺便说一句，」他装出随意的样子，「我有件紧急私事要见您。」

「要钱？」伯爵从乱蓬蓬的眉毛底下狐疑地看着他。

霍勒斯笑了。

「不，我看我不至于借钱。」他说。

===Original===
“Want to marry my niece?” asked the old man with brutal directness.

“That’s it,” said Horace coolly. He could adapt himself to the old man’s mood.

“Well, you can’t,” said the earl. “You have arranged for your horse to win, I have arranged for her to marry Ikey. At least,” he corrected himself, “Ikey has arranged with me.”

“Suppose she doesn’t care for this plan?” asked Horace.

“I don’t suppose she does,” said the old man with a grin. “I can’t imagine anybody liking Ikey, can you? I think he’s a hateful devil. He doesn’t pay his debts, he has no sense of honour, very little sense of decency; his associates, including myself, are the worst men in London.”
===Chinese===
「想娶我外甥女？」老人单刀直入，毫不留情。

「正是。」霍勒斯不动声色。老头儿的脾气，他随得来。

「那可不行。」伯爵说，「你安排了你的马赢，我安排了她嫁艾基。至少，」他更正自己，「是艾基同我安排好的。」

「要是她不喜欢这个安排呢？」霍勒斯问。

「我想她是不喜欢。」老头儿咧嘴一笑，「我想象不出谁会喜欢艾基，你呢？我看他就是个可恶的坏蛋。他欠债不还，没有荣誉感，廉耻也少得可怜；他的狐朋狗友，算上我在内，全是伦敦最坏的男人。」

===Original===
He shook his head suspiciously.

“He’s being virtuous now,” he growled, “told me so confidentially; informed me that he was turning over a new leaf. What a rotten confession for a man of his calibre to make! I mistrust him in his penitent mood.”

He looked up suddenly.

“You go and cut him out,” he said, the tiny flame of malice, which gave his face such an extraordinary character, shining in his eyes. “Good idea, that! Go and cut him out; it struck me Mary was a little keen on you. Damn Ikey! Go along!”

He pushed the astonished youth from him.
===Chinese===
他满腹狐疑地摇摇头。

「他这会儿正发善心呢。」他咆哮道，「还悄悄告诉了我；郑重宣布他要改过自新。他这号人物，居然招出这样烂的口供！他一忏悔，我就信不过。」

他忽然抬起头。

「你去把他挤掉。」他说，那簇给他脸上平添异彩的小小的恶意之火，正在眼里发亮，「好主意，就这么办！去把他挤掉；我瞧着，玛丽对你有点意思呢。艾基见鬼去吧！去吧！」

他把惊得目瞪口呆的年轻人推了出去。

===Original===
Horace found the girl in the conservatory. He was bubbling over with joy. He had never expected to make so easy a conquest of the old man﻿—so easy that he almost felt frightened. It was as if the Earl of Verlond, with that sardonic humour of his, was devising some method of humiliating him. Impulsively he told her all that had happened.

“I can’t believe it,” he cried, “he was so ready, so willing. He was brutal, of course, but that was natural.”

She looked at him with a little glint of amusement in her eyes.

“I don’t think you know uncle,” she said quietly.

“But﻿—but﻿—” he stammered.
===Chinese===
霍勒斯在花房里找到了姑娘。他欢喜得快要溢出来。他从不曾料想征服老人竟这样容易——容易得让他几乎心虚。倒仿佛弗隆德伯爵正凭着他那股讥诮的幽默，在盘算什么法子作践他。他一时冲动，把事情原原本本告诉了她。

「我简直不敢相信。」他嚷道，「他那样爽快，那样情愿。他粗鲁是粗鲁，可那本是他的本性。」

她看着他，眼里闪过一丝笑意。

「我看你并不了解叔叔。」她轻声说。

「可是——可是——」他结结巴巴。

===Original===
“Yes, I know,” she went on, “everybody thinks they do. They think he’s the most horrid old man in the world. Sometimes,” she confessed, “I have shared their opinion. I can never understand why he sent poor Con away.”

“That was your brother?” he asked.

She nodded. Her eyes grew moist.

“Poor boy,” she said softly, “he didn’t understand uncle. I didn’t then. I sometimes think uncle doesn’t understand himself very well,” she said with a sad little smile. “Think of the horrid things he says about people﻿—think of the way he makes enemies﻿—”
===Chinese===
「是啊，我知道。」她接着说，「人人都自以为了解他。他们觉得他是天下最可恶的老头儿。有时候，」她承认，「我也这么想过。可我永远不明白，他为什么把可怜的康（Con）打发走。」

「那是你哥哥？」他问。

她点头。她的眼睛湿润了。

「可怜的哥哥。」她柔声说，「他不了解叔叔。我那时也不了解。我有时想，叔叔对自己也未必很了解。」她带着一点忧伤的浅笑说，「想想他说人的那些难听话——想想他四处树敌的样子——」

===Original===
“And yet, I am ready to believe he is a veritable Gabriel,” said Horace fervently. “He is a benefactor of the human race, a king among men, the distributor of great gifts﻿—”

“Don’t be silly,” she said, and laying her hand on his arm, she led him to the farther end of the big palm court.

Whatever pleasure the old lord brought to Horace, it found no counterpart in his dealings with Sir Isaac.

He alternately patted and kicked him, until the baronet was writhing with rage. The old man seemed to take a malicious pleasure in ruffling the other. That the views he expressed at ten o’clock that night were in absolute contradiction to those that he had put into words at eight o’clock on the same night did not distress him; he would have changed them a dozen times during the course of twenty-four hours if he could have derived any pleasure from so doing.
===Chinese===
「可我现在情愿相信，他就是一位货真价实的加百列。」霍勒斯热切地说，「他是人类的恩人，人中的王者，宏大恩赐的颁赐者——」

「别傻了。」她说着把手搭上他的手臂，领他走向大棕榈厅的尽头。

老勋爵带给霍勒斯多少愉快，他同艾萨克爵士打交道时就给不了多少愉快。

他一会儿抚弄一会儿踢打，折腾得从男爵怒火中烧、坐立难安。老头儿似乎以撩拨对方为乐。当晚十点钟发表的见解，同当晚八点钟说出的话截然相反，这毫不叫他难为情；只要能从中取乐，二十四小时里他肯把主张改上一打回。

===Original===
Sir Isaac was in an evil frame of mind when a servant brought him a note. He looked round for a quiet place in which to read it. He half suspected its origin. But why had Black missed so splendid an opportunity of meeting Lord Verlond? The note would explain, perhaps.

He crossed the room and strolled towards the conservatory, reading the letter carefully. He read it twice, then he folded it up and put it into his pocket; he had occasion to go to that pocket again almost immediately, for he pulled out his watch to see the time.

When he had left the little retreat on his way to the hall, he left behind him a folded slip of paper on the floor.

This an exalted Horace, deliriously happy, discovered on his way back to the cardroom. He handed it to Lord Verlond who, having no scruples, read it﻿—and, reading it in the seclusion of his study, grinned.
===Chinese===
仆人送来一张便条时，艾萨克爵士正一肚子恶气。他四下找了个清静地方去读。他猜到一半是布莱克来的。可布莱克为什么白白放过了同弗隆德勋爵见面的绝好机会？也许便条上说得出缘故。

他穿过屋子，朝花房信步走去，一路细读那封信。他读了两遍，折好放进口袋；紧接着那口袋又有用处——他掏出怀表看时间。

等他离开那处小小的幽角、往门厅去时，一幅折起的纸片落在了身后的地板上。

这纸片，被喜气冲天、乐昏了头的霍勒斯在回牌室的路上拾到了。他把它交给弗隆德勋爵。勋爵毫无顾忌，拿去读了——独自躲进书房里读完，咧嘴笑了。

## XV Sir Isaac’s Fears / 十五 艾萨克爵士的恐惧

===Original===
Under the bright light of a bronze lamp, all that was mortal of Jakobs lay extended upon the operating-table. About the body moved swiftly the shirt-sleeved figures of the doctors.

“I don’t think there is much we can do for him,” said Gonsalez. “He’s had an arterial perforation. It seems to me that he’s bleeding internally.”

They had made a superficial examination of the wound, and Poiccart had taken so serious a view of the man’s condition that he had dispatched a messenger for a magistrate.

Willie was conscious during the examination, but he was too weak and too exhausted to give any account of what had happened.

“There’s just a chance,” said Poiccart, “if we could get a J.P. up in time, that we could give him sufficient strychnine to enable him to tell us who had done this.”
===Chinese===
一盏青铜手术灯的强光下，雅各布斯（Jakobs）那具属于尘世的躯壳直挺挺躺在手术台上。穿衬衫的医生们围着尸体敏捷地忙碌。

「我看我们帮不上他什么了。」冈萨雷斯（Gonsalez）说，「动脉破了孔。我看他是内出血。」

他们对伤口作了初步查验，波伊卡特（Poiccart）对这人伤势的看法十分严重，已经派人去请一位治安官。

查验时威利（Willie）还清醒，可他太虚弱、太衰竭，说不出事情的原委。

「只有一线机会，」波伊卡特说，「要是能及时请来一位治安法官（J.P.），我们还能给他打足士的宁，让他撑着说出是谁下的手。」

===Original===
“It’s murder, I think,” said Gonsalez, “the cut’s a clean one. Look, there’s hardly half an inch of wound. The man who did this used a stiletto, I should say, and used it pretty scientifically. It’s a wonder he wasn’t killed on the spot.”

The hastily-summoned justice of the peace appeared on the scene much sooner than they had anticipated. Gonsalez explained the condition of the man.

“He tried to tell me, after we had got him on the table, who had done it,” he said, “but I couldn’t catch the name.”

“Do you know him?” asked the J.P.

“I know him,” he said, “and I’ve rather an idea as to who has done it, but I can’t give any reasons for my suspicions.”
===Chinese===
「我看这是谋杀。」冈萨雷斯说，「创口干净利落。瞧，伤口还不到半英寸。下手的人用的是细匕首，我敢说，而且使得相当在行。他没当场断气，已是奇迹。」

仓促请来的治安法官到场比他们预想的快得多。冈萨雷斯说明了伤者的情况。

「抬上手术台以后，他想告诉我谁干的。」他说，「可那个名字我没听清。」

「你认识他吗？」治安法官问。

「认识。」他说，「是谁下的手，我也颇有几分想法，只是说不出怀疑的根据。」

===Original===
Jakobs was unconscious, and Gonsalez seized the first opportunity that presented itself of consulting with his colleague.

“I believe this is Black’s work,” he said hurriedly. “Why not send for him? We know Jakobs has been in his employ and was pensioned by him, and that’s sufficient excuse. Possibly, if we can get him down before this poor chap dies, we shall learn something.”

“I’ll get on the telephone,” said the other.

He drew from his pocket a memorandum book and consulted its pages. Black’s movements and his resorts were fairly well tabulated, but the telephone failed to connect the man they wanted.

At a quarter to two in the morning Jakobs died, without having regained consciousness, and it looked as though yet another mystery had been added to a list which was already appallingly large.
===Chinese===
雅各布斯昏了过去，冈萨雷斯抓住头一个机会，同他的同伴商量。

「我看这是布莱克（Black）的手笔。」他急急地说，「何不把他叫来？我们知道雅各布斯在他手下干过事，还领他的养老金——这个理由足够了。也许趁这可怜虫咽气之前把他弄来，我们能问出点东西。」

「我去打电话。」另一个说。

他从口袋里掏出一本备忘录，翻检里面的篇页。布莱克的行踪和他常去的场所都记得相当齐备，可电话怎么也接不通他们要找的那个人。

凌晨一点三刻，雅各布斯死了，始终没有再醒。那本来已长得吓人的悬案名单上，仿佛又添了一桩。

===Original===
The news came to May Sandford that afternoon. The tragedy had occurred too late that night to secure descriptions in the morning papers; but from the earlier editions of the afternoon journals she read with a shock of the man’s terrible fate.

It was only by accident that she learnt of it from this source, for she was still reading of his death in the paper when Black, ostentatiously agitated, called upon her.

“Isn’t it dreadful. Miss Sandford?” he said.

He was quite beside himself with grief, the girl thought.

“I shall give evidence, of course, but I shall take great care to keep your name out of it. I think the poor man had very bad associates indeed,” he said frankly. “I had to discharge him for that reason. Nobody need know he ever came here,” he suggested. “It wouldn’t be pleasant for you to be dragged into a sordid case like this.”
===Chinese===
消息当天下午传到梅·桑德福德（May Sandford）那里。惨案发生得太晚，晨报来不及报道；可从下午报纸的早版上，她读到了那人的可怕下场，心头一震。

她从报上得知纯属偶然，因为正读着他的死讯时，布莱克来访了，一副刻意摆出来的激动不安。

「这事多可怕呀，桑德福德小姐（Miss Sandford）。」他说。

姑娘心想，他简直是悲痛欲绝。

「我当然要出面作证，不过我会十分小心，不让你的名字沾上边。我看这可怜人结交的伙伴实在不象话。」他坦率地说，「我正是为这个才辞退他的。他来过这儿的事，谁都不必知道。」他提议道，「把你卷进这样一桩肮脏案子里，可不是什么愉快的事。」

===Original===
“Oh, no, no,” she said. “I don’t want to be mixed up in it at all. I’m awfully sorry, but I can’t see how my evidence would help.”

“Of course,” agreed Black. It had only occurred to him that morning how damning might be the evidence that this girl was in a position to give, and he had come to her in a panic lest she had already volunteered it.

She thought he looked ill and worried, as indeed he was, for Black had slept very little that night. He knew that he was safe from detection. None had seen him meet the man, and although he had visited the resorts which the man frequented, he had not inquired after him.

Yet Black was obsessed by the knowledge that a net was drawing round him. Who were the hunters he could not guess. There came to him at odd moments a strange feeling of terror.

Nothing was going exactly right with him. Sir Isaac had showed signs of revolt.
===Chinese===
「哦，不，不。」她说，「我根本不想沾这案子。我很抱歉，可我看不出我的证词能帮上什么忙。」

「那是当然。」布莱克附和道。直到那天早晨他才想到，这姑娘手里的证词可能有多致命，于是慌里慌张赶了来，唯恐她已经主动交了出去。

她觉得他看上去又病又烦，他也确实如此——那一夜布莱克几乎没合眼。他知道自己不会败露。没人看见他同那人会面；虽然他去过那人常去的场所，却从没打听过他。

然而布莱克心里挥不去一个念头：一张网正朝他收拢。撒网的是什么人，他猜不出。时不时的，一阵莫名的恐惧会袭上心头。

事事都不顺。艾萨克爵士（Sir Isaac Tramber）露出了反叛的苗头。

===Original===
Before the day was out he found that he had quite enough to bother him without the terrors which the unknown held.

The police had made most strenuous inquiries regarding his whereabouts on the night of the murder. They had even come to him and questioned him with such persistence that he suspected a directing force behind them. He had not bothered overmuch with the “Four Just Men.” He had accepted the word of his informant that the Four had separated for the time being, and the fact that Wilkinson Despard had left for America confirmed all that the man had told him.

He was getting short of money again. The settlement of his bets had left him short. Sandford must be “persuaded.”

Every day it was getting more and more of a necessity.

One morning Sir Isaac had telephoned him asking him to meet him in the park.
===Chinese===
还没挨到天黑他就发现，除去未知带来的恐惧，麻烦已经够他受的了。

警方就凶案当晚他的行踪展开了极其卖力的查问。他们甚至找上门来，追问不休，追得他疑心背后另有主使。「四义士」（the Four Just Men）的事，他原本没太放在心上。线人告诉他，四义士眼下已经散伙，他也信了；威尔金森·德斯帕德（Wilkinson Despard）动身去了美洲，更印证了那人说的一切。

他的钱又见底了。赌账一结，手头更紧。桑德福德（Sandford）非得「劝」服不可。

一天比一天，这更成了非办不可的事。

一天早晨，艾萨克爵士打来电话，约他在公园碰头。

===Original===
“Why not come here?” asked Black.

“No,” said the baronet’s voice. “I’d rather meet you in the park.”

He named the spot, and at the hour Black met him, a little annoyed that his day’s programme should be interrupted by this eccentricity on the part of Sir Isaac Tramber.

The baronet himself did not at once come to the point. He talked around, hummed and hawed, and at last blurted out the truth.

“Look here, Black,” he said, “you and I have been good pals﻿—we’ve been together in some queer adventures, but now I am going to﻿—I want﻿—”
===Chinese===
「干吗不来我这儿？」布莱克问。

「不。」从男爵的声音说，「我宁愿在公园见你。」

他指定了地点。到了钟点，布莱克如约见到了他，心里有点恼：一天的安排，竟被艾萨克·特兰伯这份古怪打断了。

从男爵自己也不直入正题。他绕来绕去，哼哼哈哈，最后才把实话脱口而出。

「我说，布莱克，」他说，「你我一直是好伙伴——我们一起经历过些稀奇古怪的险局，可现在我要——我想——」

===Original===
He stammered and spluttered.

“What do you want?” asked Black with a frown.

“Well, to tell the truth,” said Sir Isaac, with a pathetic attempt to be firm, “I think it is about time that you and I dissolved partnership.”

“What do you mean?” asked Black.

“Well, you know, I’m getting talked about,” said the other disjointedly. “People are spreading lies about me, and one or two chaps recently have asked me what business you and I are engaged in, and﻿—it’s worrying me. Black,” he said with the sudden exasperation of a weak man. “I believe I have lost my chance with Verlond because of my association with you.”
===Chinese===
他结结巴巴，语不成句。

「你想怎样？」布莱克皱着眉问。

「这个，实话说吧，」艾萨克爵士说着，可怜巴巴地想显得坚决，「我看，你我散伙的时候差不多了。」

「你什么意思？」布莱克问。

「唉，你是知道的，人家都在议论我。」对方断断续续地说，「有人四处散播我的谣言，最近还有一两个家伙问我，你我到底做的什么营生——这让我心烦。布莱克，」他忽然发了懦夫才有的那股急火，「我相信，就因为同你搅在一处，我把弗隆德（Verlond）那边的机会丢了。」

===Original===
“I see,” said Black.

It was a favourite expression of his. It meant much; it meant more than usual now.

“I understand,” he said, “that you think the ship is sinking, and, ratlike, you imagine it is time to swim to the shore.”

“Don’t be silly, dear old fellow,” protested the other, “and don’t be unreasonable. You see how it is. When I joined you, you were goin’ to do big things﻿—big amalgamations, big trusts, stuffin’ an’ all that sort of thing. Of course,” he admitted apologetically, “I knew all about the bucket-shop, but that was a sideline.”

Black smiled grimly.
===Chinese===
「我明白了。」布莱克说。

这是他的口头禅。它意味着很多；此刻意味着的，比往常更多。

「我理解，」他说，「你是觉得船要沉了，于是学耗子的样，觉得该游上岸去了。」

「别傻了，亲爱的老伙计。」对方抗议道，「也别不讲道理。情形你是看得见的。当初我入伙的时候，你是要干大事的——大合并，大托拉斯，往盘子里填料，还有诸如此类。当然，」他抱歉地承认，「对赌行那一套我全知道，可那只是个副业。」

布莱克冷冷一笑。

===Original===
“A pretty profitable sideline for you,” he said dryly.

“I know, I know,” said Ikey, patient to an offensive degree, “but it wasn’t a matter of millions an’ all that, now was it?”

Black was thoughtful, biting his nails and looking down at the grass at his feet.

“People are talkin’, dear old fellow,” Tramber went on, “sayin’ the most awful rotten things. You’ve been promisin’ this combination with Sandford’s foundries, you’ve practically issued shares in Amalgamated Foundries of Europe without havin’ the goods.”

“Sandford won’t come in,” said Black, without looking up, “unless I pay him a quarter of a million cash﻿—he’ll take the rest in shares. I want him to take his price in shares.”
===Chinese===
「对你可是桩油水不小的副业。」他干巴巴地说。

「我知道，我知道。」艾基（Ikey）说，耐心得让人恼火，「可那不是几百万上下的大买卖，对不对？」

布莱克若有所思，咬着指甲，低头看脚边的草。

「人家都在说呢，亲爱的老伙计。」特兰伯接着说，「说的那些话烂透了。你一直许诺要同桑德福德的铸造厂联营，『欧洲联合铸造公司』（Amalgamated Foundries of Europe）的股票你差不多都发出来了，货却根本没影儿。」

「桑德福德不肯入伙。」布莱克头也不抬地说，「除非我付他二十五万现款——其余的他肯拿股票抵。我要他全拿股票。」

===Original===
“He’s no mug,” said the baronet coarsely. “Old Sandford isn’t a mug﻿—and I’ll bet he’s got Verlond behind him. He’s no mug either.”

There was a long and awkward silence﻿—awkward for Sir Isaac, who had an unaccountable desire to bolt.

“So you want to sneak out of it, do you?” said Black, meeting his eyes with a cold smile.

“Now, my dear old chap,” said Sir Isaac hastily, “don’t take that uncharitable view. Partnerships are always being dissolved, it’s what they’re for,” he said with an attempt at humour. “And I must confess I don’t like some of your schemes.”

“You don’t like!” Black turned round on him with a savage oath. “Do you like the money you’ve got for it? The money paid in advance for touting new clients? The money given to you to settle your debts at the club? You’ve got to go through with it, Ikey, and if you don’t, I’ll tell the whole truth to Verlond and to every pal you’ve got.”
===Chinese===
「他可不是冤大头。」从男爵粗声粗气地说，「老桑德福德不是冤大头——我敢打赌，他背后有弗隆德撑着。弗隆德也不是冤大头。」

两人沉默了好一阵，尴尬得很——尴尬的是艾萨克爵士，他心里涌起一股说不清的、想撒腿就跑的欲望。

「这么说，你想溜号了？」布莱克迎着他的目光，冷冷一笑。

「哎，亲爱的老伙计。」艾萨克爵士赶忙说，「可别把人往坏处想。合伙嘛，本来就是散了又合、合了又散的。」他试图幽默一下，「再说，恕我直言，你的有些计划，我不喜欢。」

「你不喜欢！」布莱克猛地转身，狠狠咒骂了一句，「分来的钱你喜欢不喜欢？拉新客户预付给你的钱你喜欢不喜欢？替你结清俱乐部账目的那笔钱，你喜欢不喜欢？这买卖你得干到底，艾基；你要是不干，我就把全部真相捅给弗隆德，捅给你每一个朋友。」

===Original===
“They wouldn’t believe you,” said Sir Isaac calmly. “You see, my dear chap, you’ve got such an awful reputation, and the worst of having a bad reputation is that no one believes you. If it came to a question of believing you or believing me, who do you think Society would believe﻿—a man of some position, one in the baronetage of Great Britain, or a man﻿—well, not to put too fine a point on it﻿—like you?”

Black looked at him long and steadily.

“Whatever view you take,” he said slowly, “you’ve got to stand your corner. If, as a result of any of the business we are now engaged in, I am arrested, I shall give information to the police concerning you. We are both in the same boat﻿—we sink or swim together.”

He noticed the slow-spreading alarm on Sir Isaac’s face.

“Look here,” he said, “I’ll arrange to pay you back that money I’ve got. I’ll give you bills﻿—”
===Chinese===
「他们不会信你的。」艾萨克爵士心平气和地说，「你瞧，亲爱的伙计，你的名声坏得出奇，而坏名声最糟的地方，就是没人信你的话。真到了信你还是信我的地步，你说社交界会信谁——信一位有身份的人，大不列颠从男爵册上的人物，还是信一位——嗯，不必说得太委婉——像你这样的人？」

布莱克久久地、直直地看着他。

「随你怎么看。」他慢慢地说，「你的阵地你守到底。眼下咱们做的这摊营生，倘若哪一件叫我被捕，我就把你的事一五一十报给警察。咱们在一条船上——要么一起沉，要么一起浮。」

他注意到艾萨克爵士脸上慢慢漫开的惊慌。

「这么着吧，」他说，「我拿的那笔钱，我安排还你。我给你开票据——」

===Original===
Black laughed.

“You’re an amusing devil,” he said. “You and your bills! I can write bills myself, can’t I? I’d as soon take a crossing-sweeper’s bills as yours. Why, there’s enough of your paper in London to feed Sandford’s furnaces for a week.”

The words suggested a thought.

“Let’s say no more about this matter till after the amalgamation. It’s coming off next week. It may make all the difference in our fortune, Ikey,” he said in gentler tones. “Just drop the idea of ratting.”

“I’m not ratting,” protested the other. “I’m merely﻿—”
===Chinese===
布莱克笑了。

「你这家伙真逗。」他说，「你，还有你的票据！我自己不会开票据吗？你那票据，同扫街佬的也差不了多少。嗐，你散在伦敦的空头票据，够桑德福德的熔炉烧上一个礼拜。」

这话引出一个念头。

「这事到合并办完之前，咱们谁也别再提。下周就办。办成了，咱们的家当也许就大不一样，艾基。」他的语气缓和下来，「把开溜的念头收起来吧。」

「我没想开溜。」对方抗议，「我不过是——」

===Original===
“I know,” said Black. “You’re merely taking precautions﻿—well, that’s all the rats do. You’re in this up to your neck﻿—don’t deceive yourself. You can’t get out of it until I say ‘Go.’ ”

“It will be awkward for me if the game is exposed,” said Sir Isaac, biting his nails. “It will be jolly unpleasant if it is discovered I am standing in with you.”

“It will be more awkward for you,” answered Black ominously, “if, at the psychological moment, you are *not* standing in with me.”

---

Theodore Sandford, a busy man, thrust his untidy grey head into the door of his daughter’s sitting-room.
===Chinese===
「我知道。」布莱克说，「你不过是提防着点——哼，耗子干的也就是这个。这趟浑水你已经没到脖子了——别自己骗自己。我不说『走』，你休想脱身。」

「把戏一旦戳穿，我的日子不好过。」艾萨克爵士咬着指甲说，「要是让人发现我同你是一伙，那才叫真的不痛快。」

「可要是到了节骨眼上，你*不*同我一伙，」布莱克不祥地答道，「那你的日子会更不好过。」

---

西奥多·桑德福德（Theodore Sandford），大忙人一个，把那颗蓬乱的灰白脑袋探进女儿起居室的门。

===Original===
“May,” said he, “don’t forget that I am giving a dinner tonight in your honour﻿—for unless my memory is at fault and the cheque you found on your breakfast-tray was missupplied, you are twenty-two today.”

She blew him a kiss.

“Who is coming?” she asked. “I ought really to have invited everybody myself.”

“Can’t stop to tell you” said her father with a smile. “I’m sorry you quarrelled with young Fellowe. I should like to have asked him.”

She smiled gaily.
===Chinese===
「梅，」他说，「别忘了今晚我特意为你设宴——除非是我记错了，要么就是你早餐盘上那张支票送错了人，今天你可是满二十二岁了。」

她送他一个飞吻。

「都有谁来？」她问，「说起来，客本该我自己去请的。」

「来不及细说了。」她父亲含笑说，「你同小费洛吵了架，我很遗憾。不然我倒想请他的。」

她欢快地笑了。

===Original===
“I shall have to get another policeman,” she said.

He looked at her for a long time.

“Fellowe isn’t an ordinary policeman,” he said quietly. “Do you know that I saw him dining with the Home Secretary the other day?”

Her eyebrows rose.

“In uniform?” she asked.
===Chinese===
「那我只好再找一个警察了。」她说。

他看了她好半天。

「费洛（Fellowe）不是寻常警察。」他淡淡地说，「你知道前几天我看见他同内政大臣一起吃饭吗？」

她扬起了眉毛。

「穿着制服？」她问。

===Original===
He laughed.

“No, you goose,” he chuckled, “in his dressing-gown.”

She followed him down the corridor.

“You’ve learnt that from Lord Verlond,” she said reproachfully.

She waited till the car had carried her father from view, then walked back to her room, happy with the happiness which anticipates happiness.
===Chinese===
他笑了。

「不穿，傻丫头。」他咯咯笑道，「穿着晨衣。」

她跟着他沿走廊走下去。

「这话你是跟弗隆德勋爵（Lord Verlond）学的。」她嗔怪道。

她等到汽车载着父亲出了视线，才走回自己房里，满心是预支幸福的那种幸福。

===Original===
The night before had been a miserable one till, acting on an impulse, she had humbled herself, and found strange joy in the humiliation.

The knowledge that this young man was still her ideal, all she would have him to be, had so absorbed her that for the time being she was oblivious of all else.

She recalled with a little start the occasion of their last meeting, and how they had parted.

The recollection made her supremely miserable again, and, jumping up from her stool, she had opened her little writing-bureau and scribbled a hurried, penitent, autocratic little note, ordering and imploring him to come to her the instant he received it.

Frank came promptly. The maid announced his arrival within ten minutes of Mr. Sandford’s departure.
===Chinese===
前一夜本过得愁惨，直到她凭一时冲动低了头，才在低头里尝到一种奇异的欢喜。

想到这个年轻人依然是她的理想，依然是她盼他成为的一切，她便整个沉溺进去，一时间把旁的都忘了。

她微微一惊，想起两人上次见面的情形，想起他们是怎么分手的。

那段回忆又让她痛苦到极点。她从绣凳上一跃而起，打开小写字柜，匆匆涂了一张既懊悔又霸道的短笺，一半命令一半恳求，叫他一接到便立刻来见她。

弗兰克（Frank）来得飞快。桑德福德先生走后不出十分钟，女仆便通报他到了。

===Original===
May ran lightly downstairs and was seized with a sudden fit of shyness as she reached the library door. She would have paused, but the maid, who was following her, regarded her with so much sympathetic interest that she was obliged to assume a nonchalance that she was far from feeling and enter the room.

Frank was standing with his back to the door, but he turned quickly on hearing the light rustle of her gown.

May closed the door, but she made no effort to move away from it.

“How do you do?” she began.
===Chinese===
梅轻快地跑下楼，到书房门口，忽然一阵害臊。她本想停一停，可跟在后面的女仆正满怀同情地盯着她看，逼得她只好装出一副满不在乎——尽管心里恰恰相反——走进屋去。

弗兰克背对着门站着，听见她衣裙轻轻的窸窣声，忙转过身来。

梅关上门，却没有挪动，就靠在门边。

「你好。」她先开口。

===Original===
The effort she was making to still the wild beating of her heart made her voice sound cold and formal.

“I am very well, thank you.” Frank’s tone reflected her own.

“I﻿—I wanted to see you,” she continued, with an effort to appear natural.

“So I gathered from your note,” he replied.
===Chinese===
她竭力按住心头狂跳，嗓音便显得又冷又客气。

「我很好，谢谢。」弗兰克的腔调映着她的一模一样。

「我——我想见你。」她继续说，努力显得自然。

「你的便笺里看出来了。」他答道。

===Original===
“It was good of you to come,” she went on conventionally. “I hope it has not inconvenienced you at all.”

“Not at all.” Again Frank’s voice was an expressive echo. “I was just on the point of going out, so came at once.”

“Oh, I am sorry﻿—won’t you keep your other appointment first? Any time will suit me; it﻿—it is nothing important.”

“Well, I hadn’t an appointment exactly.” It was the young man’s turn to hesitate. “To tell the truth, I was coming here.”
===Chinese===
「承蒙你肯来。」她照着俗套往下说，「希望没有太耽误你的事。」

「一点也不。」弗兰克的声音又是有声有色的回声。「我正要出门，就直接过来了。」

「哦，真抱歉——要不你先去赴你原先的约？我什么时间都行；那——那不是什么要紧事。」

「其实，也谈不上什么约会。」这回轮到年轻人迟疑了。「不瞒你说，我本来就是要到这儿来。」

===Original===
“Oh, Frank! Were you really?”

“Yes, really and truly, little girl.”

May did not answer, but something Frank saw in her face spoke more plainly than words could do.

Mr. Sandford returned that afternoon to find two happy people sitting in the half-darkness of the drawing-room; and ten members of the Criminal Investigation Department waited at Scotland Yard, alternately swearing and wringing their hands.
===Chinese===
「哦，弗兰克！真的吗？」

「真的，千真万确，小姑娘。」

梅没有作答，可弗兰克在她脸上看见的那样东西，说得比言语还明白。

桑德福德先生当天下午回来，看见两个快活人坐在客厅的半明半暗里；而苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）刑事调查部的十名探员还在苦等，一会儿咒骂，一会儿搓手。

## V The Earl of Verlond / 五 弗隆德勋爵

===Original===
Colonel Black was amused. He was annoyed, too, and the two expressions resulted in a renewed irritation.

His present annoyance rose from another cause. A mysterious tribunal, which had examined his papers, had appeared from and disappeared to nowhere, had annoyed him﻿—had frightened him, if the truth be told; but courage is largely a matter of light with certain temperaments, and strong in the security of the morning sunshine and with the satisfaction that there was nothing tangible for the four men to discover, he was bold enough.

He was sitting in his dressing-gown at breakfast, and his companion was Sir Isaac Tramber.

Colonel Black loved the good things of life, good food and the comforts of civilization. His breakfast was a very ample one.

Sir Isaac’s diet was more simple: a brandy and water and an apple comprised the menu.
===Chinese===
布莱克上校（Colonel Black）觉得好笑。他也觉得恼火，而这两种情绪搅在一处，又生出新的烦躁。

他此刻的恼火另有缘由。一个神秘的法庭查验过他的文件，来无影，去无踪，叫他窝火——说句实话，也叫他害怕；不过对某些性子的人来说，胆量多半是光线的产物，此刻晨光和煦，平安无事，又想到那四个人根本抓不到什么实据，他的胆子便又壮了起来。

他穿着晨衣坐着吃早饭，陪他的是艾萨克·特兰伯爵士（Sir Isaac Tramber）。

布莱克上校贪恋人生的种种享受，讲究美食，讲究文明的舒适。他的早饭十分丰盛。

艾萨克爵士的饮食就简单多了：掺水的白兰地和一个苹果，便是他的全部菜单。

===Original===
“What’s up?” he growled. He had had a late night and was not in the best of tempers.

Black tossed a letter across to him.

“What do you think of that?” he asked. “Here’s a demand from Tangye’s, the brokers, for ten thousand pounds, and a hint that failing its arrival I shall be posted as a defaulter.”

“Pay it,” suggested Sir Isaac languidly, and the other laughed.

“Don’t talk rot,” he said, with offensive good humour. “Where am I going to get ten thousand pounds? I’m nearly broke; you know that, Tramber; we’re both in the same boat. I’ve got two millions on paper, but I don’t think we could raise a couple of hundred ready between us if we tried.”
===Chinese===
「出什么事了？」他咕哝道。他昨夜睡得晚，脾气正不佳。

布莱克把一封信抛给他。

「你看看这个。」他说，「坦奇（Tangye）经纪行来催账，要一万镑，还暗示一句：钱要是不到，就把我列入违约户张榜示众。」

「付了呗。」艾萨克爵士懒洋洋地出主意。对方笑了。

「别说傻话。」他心情好得讨人嫌，「我上哪儿弄一万镑去？我差不多破产了；这你知道，特兰伯；咱们是一条船上的。账面上我有两百万，可要论现钱，咱们俩凑一凑，我看连两三百镑也拿不出来。」

===Original===
The baronet pushed back his plate.

“I say,” he said abruptly, “you don’t mean what you said?”

“About the money?”

“About the money﻿—yes. You nearly gave me an attack of heart disease. My dear chap, we should be pretty awkwardly fixed if money dried up just now.”

Colonel Black smiled.
===Chinese===
从男爵把盘子推开。

「我说，」他没头没脑地问，「你刚才的话是当真的？」

「关于钱？」

「关于钱——是啊。你差点儿害我犯心脏病。亲爱的伙计，这节骨眼上要是断了钱路，咱们的处境可就难看了。」

布莱克上校微微一笑。

===Original===
“That’s just what has happened,” he said. “Fix or no fix, we’re in it. I’m overdrawn in the bank; I’ve got about a hundred pounds in the house, and I suppose you’ve got another hundred.”

“I haven’t a hundred farthings,” said the other.

“Expenses are very heavy,” Black went on; “you know how these things turn up. There are one or two in view, but beyond that we have nothing. If we could bring about the amalgamation of those Northern Foundries we might both sign cheques for a hundred thousand.”

“What about the City?”

The Colonel sliced off the top of his egg without replying. Tramber knew the position in the City as well as he did.
===Chinese===
「正是这么回事。」他说，「难看不难看，咱们都陷在里头了。我在银行透支了；屋里现款约莫一百镑，你手上大概还有一百。」

「我连一百个法寻都没有。」对方说。

「开销大得很。」布莱克接着说，「这类事情说来就来，你是知道的。眼下有一两桩指望，除此之外咱们一无所有。要是能把北方那几家铸造厂（Northern Foundries）合并下来，咱们俩就都能开得出十万镑的支票了。」

「伦敦城（the City）那边呢？」

上校削去鸡蛋顶，没有作答。伦敦城里的处境，特兰伯同他一样清楚。

===Original===
“H’m,” said Sir Isaac, “we’ve got to get money from somewhere, Black.”

“What about your friend?” asked Colonel Black.

He spoke carelessly, but the question was a well-considered one.

“Which friend?” asked Sir Isaac, with a hoarse laugh. “Not that I have so many that you need particularize any. Do you mean Verlond?”

Black nodded.
===Chinese===
「唔，」艾萨克爵士说，「咱们总得从什么地方弄到钱，布莱克。」

「你那位朋友呢？」布莱克上校问。

他话说得漫不经心，这一问却是深思熟虑过的。

「哪位朋友？」艾萨克爵士哑着嗓子笑，「倒不是我朋友多得需要你指名道姓。你是说弗隆德（Verlond）？」

布莱克点点头。

===Original===
“Verlond, my dear chap,” said the baronet, “is the one man I must not go to in this world for money.”

“He is a very rich man,” mused Black.

“He is a very rich man,” said the other grimly, “and he may have to leave his money to me.”

“Isn’t there an heir?” asked the colonel, interested.

“There was,” said the baronet with a grin, “a high-spirited nephew, who ran away from home, and is believed to have been killed on a cattle-ranch in Texas. At any rate, Lord Verlond intends applying to the court to presume his death.”
===Chinese===
「弗隆德嘛，亲爱的伙计，」从男爵说，「是这世上我万万不能开口借钱的人。」

「他是个大富翁。」布莱克若有所思。

「他是个大富翁，」对方阴沉沉地说，「而且他的钱说不定到头来得留给我。」

「没有继承人吗？」上校来了兴致。

「有过一个。」从男爵咧嘴一笑，「一个血气方刚的侄子，从家里跑了，据信死在德克萨斯（Texas）的一座牧牛场上。反正弗隆德勋爵（Lord Verlond）打算向法院申请宣告他死亡。」

===Original===
“That was a blow for the old man,” said Black.

This statement seemed to amuse Sir Isaac. He leant back in his chair and laughed loud and long.

“A blow!” he said. “My dear fellow, he hated the boy worse than poison. You see, the Verlond stock﻿—he’s a member of the cadet branch of the family. The boy was a real Verlond. That’s why the old man hated him. I believe he made his life a little hell. He used to have him up for weekends to bully him, until at last the kid got desperate, collected all his pocket-money and ran away.

“Some friends of the family traced him; the old man didn’t move a step to search for him. They found work for him for a few months in a printer’s shop in London. Then he went abroad﻿—sailed to America on an emigrant’s ticket.

“Some interested people took the trouble to follow his movements. He went out to Texas and got on to a pretty bad ranch. Later, a man after his description was shot in a street fight; it was one of those little ranching towns that you see so graphically portrayed in cinema palaces.”
===Chinese===
「这对老头儿是个打击。」布莱克说。

这话似乎把艾萨克爵士逗乐了。他往椅背上一仰，大笑了好一阵。

「打击！」他说，「亲爱的伙计，他恨那小子恨得比毒药还厉害。你瞧，弗隆德这一族的血脉——他是家族旁支的成员。那小子却是地地道道的嫡派弗隆德。老头儿恨的就是这个。我相信他把那孩子的小日子弄成了小地狱。他常在周末把那孩子叫到府上，好作践他，最后那孩子被逼急了，攒起全部零花钱，跑了。

「家族里的几个朋友追查到了他的下落；老头儿一步也没动身去找。朋友们在伦敦一家印刷所给他寻了几个月的活计。后来他出了国——拿一张移民船票，坐船去了美洲。

「有些好事的人不厌其烦地追踪他的行踪。他去了德克萨斯，进了一家糟糕透顶的牧场。后来，一个相貌同他对得上号的人在一场街斗中吃了枪子儿；就是那种小牧场镇——电影宫里演得活灵活现的地方。」

===Original===
“Who is the heir?” asked Black.

“To the title, nobody. To the money, the boy’s sister. She is quite a nice girl.”

Black was looking at him through half-closed eyes.

The baronet curled his moustache thoughtfully and repeated, as if to himself, “Quite a nice girl.”

“Then you have﻿—er﻿—prospects?” asked Black slowly.
===Chinese===
「继承人是谁？」布莱克问。

「爵位，没人。钱财，归那小子的妹妹。她是个挺不错的姑娘。」

布莱克眯缝着眼打量他。

从男爵若有所思地捻着小胡子，仿佛自言自语地重复道：「挺不错的姑娘。」

「那么你有——呃——指望了？」布莱克慢吞吞地问。

===Original===
“What the devil do you mean, Black?” asked Sir Isaac, sitting up stiffly.

“Just what I say,” said the other. “The man who marries the lady gets a pretty large share of the swag. That’s the position, isn’t it?”

“Something like that,” said Sir Isaac sullenly.

The colonel got up and folded his napkin carefully.

Colonel Black needed ready money so badly that it mattered very little what the City said. If Sandford objected that would be another matter, but Sandford was a good sportsman, though somewhat difficult to manage.
===Chinese===
「你到底是什么意思，布莱克？」艾萨克爵士问，一下子绷直了身子。

「就是我说的话。」对方说，「娶了这位小姐的人，那票横财里就有一大份。情形就是这样，不是吗？」

「差不多吧。」艾萨克爵士沉着脸说。

上校站起身，仔仔细细叠好餐巾。

布莱克上校急需现款，急到伦敦城说什么都无关紧要的地步。若是桑德福德（Sandford）反对，那又当别论，不过桑德福德是个输得起的人物，只是不太好驾驭。

===Original===
He stood for a moment looking down on the baronet thoughtfully.

“Ikey,” he said, “I have noticed in you of late a disposition to look upon our mutual interests as something of which a man might be ashamed﻿—I have struck an unexpected streak of virtue in you, and I confess that I am a little distressed.”

His keen eyes were fixed on the other steadily.

“Oh, it’s nothing,” said the baronet uneasily, “but the fact is, I’ve got to keep my end up in society.”

“You owe me a little,” began Black.
===Chinese===
他站了片刻，若有所思地低头看着从男爵。

「艾基（Ikey），」他说，「我近来注意到，你有一种倾向，把咱们共同的事业看成一件男人该害臊的事——我在你身上挖出了一条出乎意料的正气，恕我直言，我有点痛心。」

他那双锐利的眼睛直勾勾地盯着对方。

「哦，没什么。」从男爵不自在地说，「可实情是，我在交际场上还得撑住场面。」

「你还欠我一点。」布莱克开口道。

===Original===
“Four thousand,” said the other promptly, “and it is secured by a £50,000 policy on my life.”

“The premiums of which I pay,” snarled the colonel grimly; “but I wasn’t thinking of money.”

His absorbed gaze took in the baronet from head to foot.

“Fifty thousand pounds!” he said facetiously. “My dear Ikey, you’re worth much more murdered than alive.”

The baronet shivered.
===Chinese===
「四千。」对方应声答道，「而且有我一份保额 50,000 英镑的人寿保险作抵押。」

「保险费还是我付的呢。」上校狠狠地咆哮，「不过我刚才想的不是钱。」

他那专注的目光把从男爵从头到脚打量了一遍。

「五万镑！」他打趣道，「亲爱的艾基，你这人，死了比活着值钱多啦。」

从男爵打了个寒噤。

===Original===
“Don’t make those rotten jokes,” he said, and finished his brandy at a gulp.

The other nodded.

“I’ll leave you to your letters,” he said.

Colonel Black was a remarkably methodical and neat personage. Wrapped in his elaborate dressing-gown, he made his way through the flat and, reaching his study alone, he closed the door behind him and let it click.

He was disturbed in his mind at this sudden assumption of virtue on the part of his confederate; it was more than disconcerting, it was alarming. Black had no illusions. He did not trust Sir Isaac Tramber any more than he did other men.
===Chinese===
「别开这种烂玩笑。」说着，他把白兰地一饮而尽。

对方点点头。

「你写你的信去吧。」他说。

布莱克上校是个出名的井井有条、一尘不染的人物。他裹着那件做工考究的晨衣，穿过公寓，独自进了书房，随手带上门，由它咔哒一声扣严。

同伙突然摆出一副正气面孔，让他心里七上八下；这不只是让人狼狈，简直是叫人惊慌。布莱克不抱幻想。他对艾萨克·特兰伯爵士的信任，同对旁的人一样：一概没有。

===Original===
It was Black’s money that had, to some extent, rehabilitated the baronet in society; it was Black’s money that had purchased racehorses and paid training bills.

Here again, the man was actuated by no altruistic desire to serve one against whom the doors of society were shut and the hands of decent men were turned.

An outcast, Sir Isaac Tramber was of no value to the colonel: he had even, on one occasion, summarized his relationship with the baronet in a memorable and epigrammatic sentence: “He was the most dilapidated property I have ever handled; but I refurnished him, redecorated him, and today, even if he is not beautiful, he is very letable.”

And very serviceable Sir Isaac had proved﻿—well worth the money spent on him, well worth the share he received from the proceeds of that business he professed to despise.

Sir Isaac Tramber feared Black. That was half the secret of the power which the stronger man wielded over him. When at times he sought to escape from the tyranny his partner had established, there were sleepless nights. During the past few weeks something had happened which made it imperative that he should dissociate himself from the confederacy; that “something” had to do with the brightening of his prospects.
===Chinese===
多亏布莱克的钱，从男爵才在一定程度上在交际场上挽回了体面；是布莱克的钱买来赛马、付清了驯马的开销。

而在这一层上，此人也不是出于什么利他心，才去效力一个被社交界拒之门外、正人君子避之不及的人。

艾萨克·特兰伯爵士一旦沦为逐客，对上校便一文不值。他甚至有一次用一句隽永俏皮的话，概括了他同从男爵的关系：「他是我经手过的最破烂的产业；不过我给他重新置了家具，重新做了装潢，如今，他就算不好看，也租得出好价钱。」

而艾萨克也确实顶用——花在他身上的钱很值，他从那桩他口口声声看不起的营生里分得的红利，也很值。

艾萨克·特兰伯爵士怕布莱克。这便是强者攥在他手心里的权威，一半的秘密。每当他想逃脱同伙立下的霸道，夜里便睡不着。过去几个星期，出了一件事，逼得他非同这合伙营生一刀两断不可；那件「事」，同他日渐光明的前程大有关系。

===Original===
Lady Mary Cassilirs was more of a reality now than she had ever been. With Lady Mary went that which Black in his vulgar way described as “swag.”

The old earl had given him to understand that his addresses would not be unwelcome. Lady Mary was his ward, and perhaps it was because she refused to be terrorized by the wayward old man and his fits of savage moroseness, and because she treated his terrible storms of anger as though they did not exist and never had existed, that in the grim old man’s hard and apparently wicked heart there had kindled a flame of respect for her.

Sir Isaac went back to his own chambers in a thoughtful frame of mind. He would have to cut Black, and his conscience had advanced so few demands on his actions that he felt justified in making an exception in this case.

He felt almost virtuous as he emerged again, dressed for the park, and he was in his brightest mood when he met Lord Verlond and his beautiful ward.

There were rude people who never referred to the Earl of Verlond and his niece except as “Beauty and the Beast.”
===Chinese===
如今，玛丽·卡西利尔斯女勋爵（Lady Mary Cassilirs）比以往任何时候都更像一桩现实。跟玛丽女勋爵一道来的，还有布莱克用他那粗俗的说法称为「赃物」的东西。

老伯爵已让他明白，他的求亲不至于不受欢迎。玛丽女勋爵是他的被监护人。也许正因为她不肯被这个任性的老头儿和他阵阵凶暴的乖戾吓倒，正因为她对待他那可怕的雷霆之怒，就当它不存在、从来不曾存在过，这严厉的老人那颗坚硬而貌似歹毒的心里，才为她燃起了一簇敬重之火。

艾萨克爵士回到自己寓所，一路心事重重。他必须甩掉布莱克，而他的良心向来极少对他的行为提出要求，因此他觉得，在这个案子上破一回例，完全问心无愧。

等他再次出门、穿戴整齐上公园去时，简直觉得自己高尚起来；见到弗隆德勋爵和他那位美丽的被监护人时，他正是兴致最高的时候。

有些嘴上无德的人，提起弗隆德伯爵和他外甥女，从来只有一个称呼：「美女与野兽」。

===Original===
She was a tall girl and typically English﻿—straight of back, clear of skin, and bright of eye. A great mass of chestnut hair, two arched eyebrows, and a resolute little chin made up a face of special attractiveness.

She stood almost head and shoulders above the old man at her side. Verlond had never been a beauty. Age had made his harsh lines still harsher; there was not a line in his face which did not seem as though it had been carved from solid granite, so fixed, so immovable and cold it was.

His lower jaw protruded, his eyes were deep set. He gave you the uncanny impression when you first met him that you had been longer acquainted with his jaw than with his eyes.

He snapped a greeting to Sir Isaac.

“Sit down, Ikey,” he smiled.
===Chinese===
姑娘身材高挑，是典型的英国丽人——背脊挺直，肌肤莹洁，目光明亮。一头浓密的栗色长发，两弯弓形秀眉，一副果敢的小下巴，合成一张格外动人的脸。

她站在老人身边，几乎高出他一头半。弗隆德从来算不上好看。岁月把他本就硬朗的线条刻得更硬朗；他脸上没有一条纹路不像是从整块花岗岩上凿出来的，那样定型，那样纹丝不动、冷冰冰。

他下颌前突，双眼深陷。初次见面，他给你一种说不出的怪印象：他的下巴，你仿佛认识得比他的眼睛还久。

他没好气地冲艾萨克爵士打了声招呼。

「坐下，艾基。」他咧嘴一笑。

===Original===
The girl had given the baronet the slightest of nods, and immediately turned her attention to the passing throng.

“Not riding today?” asked Sir Isaac.

“Yes,” said the peer, “I am at this moment mounted on a grey charger, leading a brigade of cavalry.”

His humour took this one form, and supplied answers to unnecessary questions.

Then suddenly his face went sour, and after a glance round to see whether the girl’s attention had been attracted elsewhere, he leant over towards Sir Isaac and, dropping his voice, said, “Ikey, you’re going to have some difficulty with her.”
===Chinese===
姑娘朝从男爵几不可察地点了点头，随即把注意力转向川流而过的人群。

「今天不骑马？」艾萨克爵士问。

「骑呢。」这位贵族说，「我这会儿正骑在一匹灰色战马上，统领一旅骑兵。」

他的幽默只有这一种形式，专用来回答多余的问题。

忽然他的脸沉了下来，四下瞟了一眼，见姑娘的注意已经转到别处，才俯身朝艾萨克爵士压低嗓音说：「艾基，你在她身上怕是要费些周折。」

===Original===
“I am used to difficulties,” said Sir Isaac airily.

“Not difficulties like this,” said the earl. “Don’t be a fool, Ikey, don’t pretend you’re clever. I know﻿—the difficulties﻿—I have to live in the same house with her. She’s an obstinate devil﻿—there’s no other word for it.”

Sir Isaac looked round cautiously.

“Is there anybody else?” he asked.

He saw the earl’s brows tighten, his eyes were glaring past him, and, following their direction, Sir Isaac saw the figure of a young man coming towards them with a smile that illuminated the whole of his face.
===Chinese===
「周折我见惯了。」艾萨克爵士轻飘飘地说。

「这样的周折你可没见过。」伯爵说，「别犯傻，艾基，别装聪明。我懂——这些难处——我得同她住在一座宅子里。她是个倔魔鬼——再找不出第二个词形容。」

艾萨克爵士小心地四下张望。

「还有别人没有？」他问。

他见伯爵眉头一紧，目光越过自己瞪向前方；顺着那目光望去，艾萨克爵士看见一个年轻男人的身影朝这边走来，满脸放光地笑着。

===Original===
That smile was directed neither to the earl nor to his companion; it was unmistakably intended for the girl, who, with parted lips and a new light in her eyes, beckoned the newcomer forward.

Sir Isaac scowled horribly.

“The accursed cheek of the fellow,” he muttered angrily.

“Good morning,” said Horace Gresham to the earl; “taking the air?”

“No,” growled the old man, “I am bathing, I am deep-sea fishing, I am aeroplaning. Can’t you see what I am doing? I’m sitting here﻿—at the mercy of every jackass that comes along to address his insane questions to me.”
===Chinese===
那笑容既不是给伯爵的，也不是给同伴的；明明白白是冲着姑娘来的。姑娘双唇微启，眼里闪着新的光彩，招手唤来人上前。

艾萨克爵士的脸黑得吓人。

「这小子脸皮厚得该死。」他恨恨地咕哝。

「早安。」霍勒斯·格雷沙姆（Horace Gresham）向伯爵打招呼，「出来透透气？」

「不。」老头儿咆哮道，「我在洗海水浴，我在深海钓鱼，我在开飞机。你看不出我在干什么吗？我坐在这儿——听凭每一头过路的蠢驴，把他那些疯话冲着我来。」

===Original===
Horace laughed. He was genuinely amused. There was just this touch of perverse humour in the old man which saved him from being absolutely repulsive.

Without further ceremony he turned to the girl.

“I expected to find you here,” he said.

“How is that great horse of yours?” she asked.

He shot a smiling glance at Tramber.
===Chinese===
霍勒斯笑了。他是真觉得有趣。老头儿身上恰是这样一点拧巴的幽默，救了他，使他还不至于绝对招人嫌。

他不再客套，转身面向姑娘。

「我就料到能在这儿找到你。」他说。

「你那匹了不起的马怎么样了？」她问。

他笑盈盈地瞥了特兰伯一眼。

===Original===
“Oh, he’ll be fit enough on the day of the race,” he said. “We shall make Timbolino gallop.”

“Mine will beat yours, wherever they finish, for a thousand,” said Sir Isaac angrily.

“I should not like to take your money,” said the young man. “I feel that it would be unfair to you, and unfair to﻿—your friend.”

The last words were said carelessly, but Sir Isaac Tramber recognized the undertone of hostility, and read in the little pause which preceded them the suggestion that this cheery young man knew much more about his affairs than he was prepared for the moment to divulge.

“I am not concerned about my friend,” said the baronet angrily. “I merely made a fair and square sporting offer. Of course, if you do not like to accept it﻿—” He shrugged his shoulders.
===Chinese===
「哦，到比赛那天，它的状态保准好得很。」他说，「到时候叫蒂姆博利诺（Timbolino）撒开蹄子跑。」

「不管跑成什么名次，我的马准赢你的，赌一千。」艾萨克爵士怒冲冲地说。

「我不想要你的钱。」年轻人说，「我觉得那样对你不公平，对——你的朋友也不公平。」

最后几个字说得随随便便，艾萨克·特兰伯爵士却听出了底下那层敌意，并从话前那一点小小的停顿里读出了这样的暗示：这个满面春风的年轻人，对他那些事知道得远比他此刻愿意透露的多。

「我的朋友不用你操心。」从男爵怒道，「我不过是提了一个光明正大的赌约。当然，你要是不愿接——」他耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“Oh, I would accept it all right,” said the other.

He turned deliberately to the girl.

“What’s Gresham getting at?” asked Verlond, with a grin at his friend’s discomfiture.

“I didn’t know he was a friend of yours,” said Sir Isaac; “where did you pick him up?”

Lord Verlond showed his yellow teeth in a grin.
===Chinese===
「哦，接我倒是肯接的。」对方说。

他不慌不忙转向姑娘。

「格雷沙姆这是打的什么主意？」弗隆德问，见朋友狼狈，咧嘴直乐。

「我原先不知道他是你的朋友。」艾萨克爵士说，「你从哪儿捡来的这位？」

弗隆德勋爵咧嘴一笑，露出一口黄牙。

===Original===
“Where one picks up most of one’s undesirable acquaintances,” he said, “in the members’ enclosure. But racing is getting so damned respectable, Ikey, that a real top-notch undesirable is hard to meet.

“The last race-meeting I went to, what do you think I found? The tearoom crammed, you couldn’t get in at the doors; the bar empty. Racing is going to the dogs, Ikey.”

He was on his favourite hobby now, and Sir Isaac shifted uneasily, for the old man was difficult to divert when in the mood for reminiscent chatter.

“You can’t bet nowadays like you used to bet,” the earl went on. “I once backed a horse for five thousand pounds at 20‒1, without altering the price. Where could you do that nowadays?”

“Let us walk about a little,” said the girl.
===Chinese===
「就在人捡到大多数不想结交的熟人的地方。」他说，「会员围场（members' enclosure）。不过赛马这行当如今体面得要命，艾基，真正一流的讨厌人物反倒难碰上了。

「上一回我去看赛马，你猜我瞧见了什么？茶室挤得水泄不通，门口都挤不进去；酒吧间空空荡荡。赛马这门行当完蛋了，艾基。」

他这会儿骑上了心爱的话题，艾萨克爵士坐立不安地挪动着，因为老头儿一旦起了怀旧闲谈的兴致，就很难把他岔开。

「如今下注，再不能像从前那样下了。」伯爵接着说，「想当年，我押一匹马五千镑，赔率 20 比 1，硬是没把盘口押动。如今你上哪儿找这种事去？」

「咱们走走吧。」姑娘说。

===Original===
Lord Verlond was so engrossed in his grievance against racing society that he did not observe the two young people rise and stroll away.

Sir Isaac saw them, and would have interrupted the other’s garrulity, but for the wholesome fear he had of the old man’s savage temper.

“I can’t understand,” said Horace, “how your uncle can stick that bounder.”

The girl smiled.

“Oh, he can ‘stick’ him all right,” she said dryly. “Uncle’s patience with unpleasant people is proverbial.”
===Chinese===
弗隆德勋爵正沉浸在对赛马界的一腔怨气里，竟没有察觉两个年轻人起身踱开去了。

艾萨克爵士看见了，本想打断老头儿的絮叨，只是对老人的火暴脾气心存敬畏，不敢造次。

「我真不明白，」霍勒斯说，「你叔叔怎么受得了那个下流胚。」

姑娘微微一笑。

「哦，『受』他倒是受得住的。」她干巴巴地说，「叔叔容忍讨厌鬼的耐性，是出了名的。」

===Original===
“He’s not very patient with me,” said Mr. Horace Gresham.

She laughed.

“That is because you are not sufficiently unpleasant,” she said. “You have to be hateful to everybody else in the world before uncle likes you.”

“And I’m not that, am I?” he asked eagerly.

She flushed a little.
===Chinese===
「他对我可就不怎么耐心了。」霍勒斯·格雷沙姆先生说。

她笑了。

「那是因为你还不够讨厌。」她说，「你得先把世上其余的人都得罪遍了，叔叔才会喜欢你。」

「而我还不到那份上，对不对？」他急切地问。

她微微红了脸。

===Original===
“No, I wouldn’t say you were that,” she said, glancing at him from under her eyelashes. “I am sure you are a very nice and amiable young man. You must have lots of friends. Ikey, on the other hand, has such queer friends. We saw him at the Blitz the other day, lunching with a perfectly impossible man﻿—do you know him?” she asked.

He shook his head.

“I don’t know any perfectly impossible persons,” he said promptly.

“A Colonel Black?” she suggested.

He nodded.
===Chinese===
「不，我还不能说你到了那份上。」她说着，从睫毛底下瞟了他一眼，「我敢肯定，你是个非常和善可亲的年轻人，朋友一定很多。艾基呢，交的朋友就可怪了。前几天我们在布利茨（the Blitz）看见他，正陪着一个要多别扭有多别扭的男人吃午饭——你认识那人吗？」她问。

他摇摇头。

「别扭到家的人，我一个也不认识。」他脱口答道。

「一位布莱克上校？」她提示道。

他点点头。

===Original===
“I know of him,” he replied.

“Who is this Black?” she asked.

“He is a colonel.”

“In the army?”

“Not in our army,” said Horace with a smile. “He is what they call in America a ‘pipe colonel,’ and he’s﻿—well, he’s a friend of Sir Isaac﻿—” he began, and hesitated.
===Chinese===
「听说过他。」他答道。

「这位布莱克是什么人？」她问。

「他是位上校。」

「陆军里的？」

「不在咱们英国的军队里。」霍勒斯含笑说，「他是美国人所谓的『冒牌上校』（pipe colonel）——再说，他是艾萨克爵士的朋友——」他起了个头，又犹豫了。

===Original===
“That doesn’t tell me very much, except that he can’t be very nice,” she said.

He looked at her eagerly.

“I’m so glad you said that,” he said. “I was afraid﻿—” Again he stopped, and she threw a swift glance at him.

“You were afraid?” she repeated.
===Chinese===
「这可没告诉我多少东西，只告诉我他准不是什么好人。」她说。

他热切地看着她。

「你这么说，我太高兴了。」他说，「我本来担心——」他又停住，她飞快地瞟了他一眼。

「你担心？」她重复道。

===Original===
It was remarkable to see this self-possessed young man embarrassed, as he was now.

“Well,” he went on, a little incoherently, “one hears things﻿—rumours. I know what a scoundrel he is, and I know how sweet you are﻿—the fact is, Mary, I love you better than anything in life.”

She went white and her hand trembled. She had never anticipated such a declaration in a crowd. The unexpectedness of it left her speechless. She looked at his face: he, too, was pale.

“You shouldn’t,” she murmured, “at this time in the morning.”
===Chinese===
这位素来镇定的年轻人此刻竟窘态毕露，实在少见。

「这个，」他有点语无伦次地接着说，「人总会听到些事——风言风语。我知道他是个多么无赖的恶棍，也知道你有多么可爱——事实是，玛丽，我爱你胜过世上的一切。」

她的脸白了，手直发抖。她从没料到会在人群当中听到这样的表白。这突如其来的话让她一时说不出声。她看他的脸：他也是苍白的。

「你不该，」她低声说，「不该在这个大清早。」

## IV The Men Who Sat in Judgment / 四 坐堂问案的人

===Original===
Frank heard a little scuffling in the hall, and knew that the men who had been watching him had gone to cover. He had little fear, though he carried no weapon. He was supremely confident in his own strength and science.

Black, following him in, shut the door behind him. Frank heard the snick of a bolt being shot into its socket in the dark. Black switched on the light.

“We’re playing fair, Constable Fellowe,” he said, with an amiable smile. “You see, we do not try any monkey tricks with you. Everything is straight and aboveboard.”

He led the way up the thickly-carpeted stairs, and Frank followed.

The young man noticed the house was luxuriously furnished. Rich engravings hung on the walls, the curtains that veiled the big stairway window were of silk, cabinets of Chinese porcelain filled the recesses.
===Chinese===
弗兰克（Frank）听见门厅里一阵轻微的窸窣声，知道那些盯着他的人已经隐蔽起来。他虽然没带武器，却毫不畏惧。他对自己的力气和身手有着十足的信心。

布莱克（Black）跟着他走进来，随手把门关上。黑暗中，弗兰克听见门闩咔哒一声插进闩槽。布莱克拧亮了灯。

「我们行事光明，费洛警员（Constable Fellowe）。」他脸上带着和气的微笑说道，「你瞧，我们没跟你玩什么鬼花招。一切都摆在明面上。」

他在前头引路，走上铺着厚地毯的楼梯，弗兰克跟在后面。

年轻人留意到，这宅子陈设豪华。墙上挂着名贵的版画，遮住大楼梯间窗户的是绸缎窗帘，壁龛里摆满了一柜柜中国瓷器。

===Original===
Black led the way to a room on the first floor. It was not the room in which the board meeting had been held, but a small one which led off from the boardroom.

Here the luxury was less apparent. Two desks formed the sole furniture of the room; the carpet under foot was of the commonplace type to be found in the average office. A great panel of tapestry﻿—the one touch of luxury﻿—covered one wall, and a cluster of lights in the ceiling afforded light to the room.

A little fire was burning in the grate. On a small table near one of the desks supper had been laid for two. Frank noticed this, and Black, inwardly cursing his own stupidity, smiled.

“It looks as though I expected you,” he said easily, “though, as a matter of fact, I have some friends here tonight, and one of them is staying to supper.”

Frank nodded. He knew the significance of that supper-table and the white paper pads ready for use.
===Chinese===
布莱克把他领到楼上的一间屋子里。那不是先前开董事会的房间，而是从董事会室隔壁通进去的一间小屋。

这里的奢华就不那么显眼了。两张书桌是屋里仅有的家具；脚下的地毯是普通办公室里常见的那种货色。一幅巨大的花毯——屋里唯一的奢华点缀——遮住了整整一面墙，天花板上一组灯给房间照明。

壁炉里燃着一点小火。其中一张书桌旁的小桌上，摆好了两人的夜宵。弗兰克注意到了，布莱克心里暗骂自己蠢，脸上却堆起笑。

「看样子倒像我早料到你要来。」他若无其事地说，「不过说实在的，今晚我有几位朋友在这里，其中一位要留下吃夜宵。」

弗兰克点点头。他明白那张夜宵桌和备好待用的白纸本子意味着什么。

===Original===
“Sit down,” said Black, and himself sat at one of the desks. Frank seated himself slowly at some distance from the other, half turning to face the man whom he had set himself to ruin.

“Now, let us get to business,” said Black briskly. “There is no reason in the world why you and I should not have an understanding. I’m a business man, you’re a business man, and a smart young man too,” he said approvingly.

Frank made no reply. He knew what was coming.

“Now suppose,” Black continued reflectively, “suppose we make an arrangement like this. You imagine that I am engaged in a most obnoxious type of business. Oh, I know!” he went on deprecatingly, “I know! You’re under the impression that I’m making huge profits, that I’m robbing people by bucket-shop methods. I needn’t tell you, constable, that I am most grieved and indignant that you should have entertained so low an opinion of my character.”

His voice was neither grieved nor indignant. Indeed, the tone he employed was a cheerful admission of fault.
===Chinese===
「坐吧。」布莱克说着，自己在一张书桌后坐下。弗兰克慢吞吞地在离他不远不近的地方落座，侧过半个身子，正对着这个他决意要搞垮的人。

「好，咱们谈正事吧。」布莱克爽快地说，「天底下没有理由说你我之间不能达成谅解。我是生意人，你也是生意人，而且还是个精明的年轻人。」他赞许地说。

弗兰克不作声。他知道接下来是什么。

「这么说吧，」布莱克若有所思地继续道，「假设咱们做成这样一笔交易。你以为我干的是一桩顶顶惹人厌恶的营生。哦，我知道！」他一脸委屈地接着说，「我知道！你认定我在牟取暴利，认定我在用对赌行（bucket-shop）的手法坑人钱财。警员先生，你对我的为人竟抱有如此卑下的看法，我有多痛心、多愤慨，就不必对你明说了。」

他的语气既不痛心也不愤慨。实际上，他用的那腔调，倒像是在快快活活地认错。

===Original===
“Now, I am quite content you should investigate my affairs first hand. You know we receive a large number of accounts from all over the Continent and that we pay away enormous sums to clients who﻿—well, shall we say﻿—gamble on margins?”

“You can say what you like,” said Frank.

“Now,” said Black, “suppose you go to Paris, constable, you can easily get leave, or go into the provinces, to any of the big towns in Great Britain where our clients reside, and interview them for yourself as to our honesty. Question them﻿—I’ll give you a list of them.

“I don’t want you to do this at your own expense,” his big hands were outstretched in protest. “I don’t suppose you have plenty of money to waste on that variety of excursion.

“Now, I will hand you tonight, if you like, a couple of hundred pounds, and you shall use this just as you like to further your investigations. How does that strike you?”
===Chinese===
「这么说吧，你尽管放手去查我的账，我求之不得。你知道，我们从欧洲大陆各地接收大量账户，也向客户们支付巨额款项——嗯，姑且这么说吧——那些做保证金投机的客户？」

「你爱怎么说都行。」弗兰克说。

「这么着，」布莱克说，「假设你去一趟巴黎——警员先生，你休个假不难——或者去外省，去大不列颠任何一座住着我们客户的大城，亲自见见他们，问问他们我们做得地道不地道。去盘问他们吧——我给你开一份名单。

「我不想让你自掏腰包办这事，」他摊开两只大手，作出抗议的姿态，「我看你也没有多少闲钱，经得起那样一种游历的糟蹋。

「这样吧，只要你愿意，今晚我就先交给你两百来镑，这笔钱你随便怎么用，全看你查案的需要。你觉得这个提议如何？」

===Original===
Frank smiled.

“It strikes me as devilish ingenious,” he said. “I take the couple of hundred, and I can either use it for the purpose you mention or I can put it to my own account, and no questions will be asked. Do I understand aright?”

Colonel Black smiled and nodded. His strong, yellow face puckered in internal amusement. “You are a singularly sharp young man,” he said.

Frank rose.

“There’s nothing doing,” he said.
===Chinese===
弗兰克笑了。

「我觉得这主意刁钻得很。」他说，「我收下这两百来镑，既可以照你说的用途去花，也可以干脆划进我自己的户头，而且没人过问。我理解得对不对？」

布莱克上校（Colonel Black）微笑点头。那张结实的黄脸皱起来，透着心里的得意。「你这个年轻人，精明得少见。」他说。

弗兰克站起身。

「免谈。」他说。

===Original===
Colonel Black frowned.

“You mean you refuse?” he said.

Frank nodded.

“I refuse,” he said, “absolutely. You can’t bribe me with two hundred pounds, or with two thousand pounds, Black. I am not to be bought. I believe you are one of the most dangerous people society knows. I believe that both here and in the City you are running on crooked lines; I shall not rest until I have you in prison.”

Black rose slowly to his feet.
===Chinese===
布莱克上校皱起眉头。

「你是说，你拒绝？」他说。

弗兰克点点头。

「我拒绝，」他说，「断然拒绝。两百镑收买不了我，两千镑也收买不了我，布莱克。我是买不通的。我相信你是社会所知最危险的人物之一。我相信无论在这里还是在伦敦城（the City），你走的都是歪路；不把你送进监狱，我不会罢休。」

布莱克缓缓站起身来。

===Original===
“So that’s it, is it?” he said.

There was menace and malignity in his tones. A look of implacable hatred met Fellowe’s steady gaze.

“You will regret this,” he went on gratingly. “I have given you a chance that most young men would jump at. I could make that three hundred﻿—”

“If you were to make it thirty-three hundred, or thirty-three thousand,” said Frank impatiently, “there would be no business done. I know you too well, Black. I know more about you than you think I know.”

He took up his hat and examined the interior thoughtfully.
===Chinese===
「原来如此，是吧？」他说。

他的话音里带着威胁与狠毒。一股难以化解的仇恨，迎上费洛坚定的目光。

「你会后悔的。」他咬着牙继续说，「我给了你一个大多数年轻人求之不得的机会。我可以把数目加到三百——」

「就算你加到三千三，加到三万三，」弗兰克不耐烦地说，「这买卖也做不成。我太了解你了，布莱克。我了解你的底细，比你想的要深。」

他拿起帽子，若有所思地查看帽子里面。

===Original===
“There is a man wanted in France﻿—an ingenious man who initiated Get-rich-quick banks all over the country, particularly in Lyons and the South﻿—his name is Olloroff,” he said carefully. “There’s quite a big reward offered for him He had a partner who died suddenly﻿—”

Black’s face went white. The hand that rose to his lips shook a little.

“You know too much, I think,” he said.

He turned swiftly and left the room. Frank sprang back to the door. He suspected treachery, but before he could reach it the door closed with a click.

He turned the handle and pulled, but it was fast.
===Chinese===
「法国正在通缉一个人——一个别出心裁的家伙，在全国各地开设『快速致富银行』，里昂（Lyons）和南方尤其多——他叫奥洛罗夫（Olloroff）。」他字斟句酌地说，「悬赏他的赏格不小。他有过一个合伙人，死得突然——」

布莱克的脸白了。抬到唇边的那只手微微发抖。

「你知道得太多了，我看。」他说。

他猛地转身出了房间。弗兰克一个箭步冲回门边。他疑心有诈，可还没等他赶到，门已经咔哒一声关上了。

他转动把手，用力拉了拉，门纹丝不动。

===Original===
He looked round the room, and saw another door at the farther end. He was halfway towards this when all the lights in the room went out. He was in complete darkness.

What he had thought to be a window at one end turned out to be a blank wall, so cunningly draped with curtains and ingeniously-shaped blinds as to delude the observer.

The real window, which looked out on to the street below, was heavily shuttered.

The absence of light was no inconvenience to him. He had a small electric lamp in his pocket, which he flashed round the room. It had been a tactical error on his part to put Black on his guard, but the temptation to give the big man a fright had been too great.

He realized that he was in a position of considerable danger. Save the young man he had seen in the street, and who in such an extraordinary manner had recognized him, nobody knew of his presence in that house.
===Chinese===
他环顾房间，看见另一头还有一扇门。他刚走到半路，屋里所有的灯忽然熄灭。他陷入了彻底的黑暗。

他原以为是一端的那扇窗，其实是堵实墙，只是帘幔高明、遮帘的形状巧妙，骗过了旁观者的眼睛。

真正临街的那扇窗，则钉着厚厚的窗板。

没有灯光对他并无不便。他衣袋里有一支小电筒，拿出来朝屋里四下照了照。让布莱克起了戒心，这在战术上是步错棋，可是要吓一吓这个大人物的诱惑实在太大了。

他明白自己的处境相当危险。除了他在街上见到的、以那种离奇方式认出他来的那个年轻人，谁也不知道他在这宅子里。

===Original===
He made a swift search of the room and listened intently at both doors, but he could hear nothing.

On the landing outside the door through which he had entered there were a number of antique Eastern arms hung on the wall. He had a slight hope that this scheme of decoration might have been continued in the room, but before he began his search he knew his quest was hopeless.

There would be no weapons here. He made a careful examination of the floor; he wanted to be on his guard against traps and pitfalls.

There was no danger from this. He sat down on the edge of a desk and waited.

He waited half an hour before the enemy gave a sign. Then, close to his ear, it seemed, a voice asked:
===Chinese===
他把房间迅速搜了一遍，又凑在两扇门上屏息细听，什么也听不见。

他进来的那扇门外面的楼梯平台上，墙上挂着一批古旧的东方兵器。他还存着一点指望，盼着这路装饰或许也延伸到了这间屋里，可还没动手搜，他就知道这指望要落空。

这里不会有武器。他把地板细细检查了一遍；他得提防陷阱和圈套。

地板这边倒没有危险。他在一张书桌边上坐下，等着。

他等了半个钟头，对方才有了动静。随后，仿佛就在他耳边，一个声音问道：

===Original===
“Are you going to be sensible, constable?”

Frank flashed the rays of his lamp in the direction from which the voice came.

He saw what appeared to him to be a hanging Eastern lantern. He had already observed that the stem from which it hung was unusually thick; now he realized that the bell-shaped lamp was the end of a speaking-tube.

He guessed, and probably correctly, that the device had been hung rather to allow Black to overhear than for the purpose of communicating with the occupants of the room.

He made no reply. Again the question was repeated, and he raised his head and answered.
===Chinese===
「你打算放聪明点吗，警员先生？」

弗兰克把电筒的光柱朝声音来的方向照去。

他看见一件东西，像是一盏悬着的东方吊灯。他早先就留意到，吊它的那根吊杆粗得出奇；此刻才明白，那盏钟形的灯是一根传话筒的末端。

他猜想——多半猜得不错——安这么个玩意儿，与其说是为了同屋里的人通话，不如说是方便布莱克偷听。

他不答话。那问题又重复了一遍，他这才抬起头回答。

===Original===
“Come and see,” he challenged.

All the time he had been waiting in the darkness his attention had been divided between the two doors. He was on the alert for the thin pencil of light which would show him the stealthy opening.

In some extraordinary manner he omitted to take into consideration the possibility of the outside lights being extinguished.

He was walking up and down the carpeted centre of the room, which was free of any impediment, when a slight noise behind him arrested his attention.

He had half turned when a noose was slipped over his body, a pair of desperate arms encircled his legs, and he was thrown violently to the floor.
===Chinese===
「有本事过来看。」他挑衅地说。

在黑暗中等待的这段时间，他的注意力一直分在两扇门上。他警惕着那一线细细的铅笔似的灯光——那会向他暴露门在悄悄打开。

不知怎么的，他偏偏漏想了一种可能：屋外的灯会被熄掉。

他正在屋子中间铺地毯的空当里来回踱步——那里没有任何障碍——忽然，身后一点轻微的响动攫住了他的注意。

他刚转过半个身子，一个绳圈已经套上他的身体，两条凶狠的手臂抱住他的双腿，他被人猛地掼倒在地板上。

===Original===
He struggled, but it was against uneven odds. The lasso which had pinioned him prevented the free use of his arms. He found himself lying face downwards upon the carpet.

A handkerchief was thrust into his mouth, something cold and hard encircled his wrists and pulled them together. He heard a click, and knew that he was handcuffed behind.

“Pull him up,” said Black’s voice.

At that moment the lights in the room went on. Frank staggered to his feet, assisted ungently by Jakobs.

Black was there. Sparks was there, and a stranger Frank had seen enter the house was also there, but a silk handkerchief was fastened over the lower half of his face, and all that Frank could see was the upper half of a florid countenance and a pair of light blue eyes that twinkled shiftily.
===Chinese===
他挣扎，但敌众我寡。套住他的那根套索缚得他双臂动弹不得。他发现自己脸朝下趴在地毯上。

一块手帕塞进他嘴里，一样又冷又硬的东西箍住他的两个手腕，把它们拉拢到一处。他听见咔哒一声，知道自己在背后被铐上了手铐。

「把他拉起来。」是布莱克的声音。

这时屋里的灯亮了。弗兰克踉跄着爬起来，雅各布斯（Jakobs）粗手粗脚地帮了他一把。

布莱克在场。斯帕克斯（Sparks）也在场，弗兰克曾看见进宅来的那个陌生人也在，只是那人下半张脸蒙着一条丝手帕，弗兰克看得见的，只有一张红脸膛的上半部和一对贼光闪烁、滴溜乱转的淡蓝色眼睛。

===Original===
“Put him on that sofa,” said Black. “Now,” he said, when his prisoner had been placed according to instructions, “I think you are going to listen to reason.”

It was impossible for Frank Fellowe to reply. The handkerchief in his mouth was an effective bar to any retort that might have risen in his mind, but his eyes, clear, unwavering, spoke in unmistakable language to the smiling man who faced him.

“My proposition is very simple,” said Black: “you’re to hold your tongue, mind your own business, accept a couple of hundred on account, and you will not be further molested. Refuse, and I’m going to put you where I can think about you.”

He smiled crookedly. “There are some five cellars in this house,” said Black; “if you are a student of history, as I am, Mr. Fellowe, you should read the *History of the Rhine Barons*. You would recognize then that I have an excellent substitute for the donjon keeps of old. You will be chained there by the legs, you will be fed according to the whims of a trusted custodian, who, I may tell you, is a very absentminded man, and there you will remain until you are either mad or glad﻿—glad to accept our terms﻿—or mad enough to be incarcerated in some convenient asylum, where nobody will take your accusations very seriously.”

Black turned his head.
===Chinese===
「把他放到那张沙发上。」布莱克说。「好了，」等囚徒按吩咐安置妥当，他又开口，「我想，这回你该听得进道理了。」

弗兰克·费洛想回话也回不成。嘴里那块手帕把一切涌到嘴边的反驳都堵了个严实，可他那双清澈、毫不游移的眼睛，用再明白不过的语言，对着面前这个笑眯眯的人说话。

「我的条件非常简单。」布莱克说，「闭上你的嘴，少管闲事，先收两百镑作定金，往后就再没人来搅扰你。要是拒绝，我就要把你搁到一个我能惦记着你的地方去。」

他歪嘴一笑。「这宅子里大约有五间地窖。」布莱克说，「费洛先生（Mr. Fellowe），你要是同我一样是个研读历史的人，就该读读《莱茵男爵史》（History of the Rhine Barons）。到那时你就会承认，我为古时候的城堡地牢找到了一样顶好的替代品。你会被用链子锁住双腿关在里面，吃不吃得着饭，全看一位受信赖的看守的兴致——我不妨告诉你，那是个非常健忘的人——你就待在那儿，直到你发了疯，或者乐意的地步——乐意接受我们的条件——要么就疯得足以被送进哪家方便的疯人院，在那里，没有人会把你那些指控当真。」

布莱克转过头去。

===Original===
“Take that gag out,” he said; “we will bring him into the other room. I do not think that his voice will be heard, however loudly he shouts, in there.”

Jakobs pulled the handkerchief roughly from Frank’s mouth. He was half pushed, half led, to the door of the boardroom, which was in darkness.

Black went ahead and fumbled for the switch, the others standing in the doorway.

He found the light at last, and then he stepped back with a cry of horror.

Well he might, for four strangers sat at the board﻿—four masked men.
===Chinese===
「把他嘴里的东西取出来。」他说，「咱们把他弄到隔壁屋里去。我看在那屋里，任他喊破嗓子也没人听得见。」

雅各布斯一把扯出弗兰克嘴里的手帕。他被人半推半带，弄到董事会室的门口，那屋里一片漆黑。

布莱克走在头里，摸索电灯开关，其余的人站在门口。

他终于摸到了开关，灯一亮，他惊叫一声，倒退了一步。

他这一惊也在情理之中：董事会桌旁坐着四个陌生人——四个蒙面人。

===Original===
The door leading into the boardroom was a wide one. The three men with their prisoner stood grouped in the centre, petrified into immobility.

The four who sat at the table uttered no sound.

Black was the first to recover his self-possession. He started forward, then stopped. His face worked, his mouth opened, but he could frame no words.

“What﻿—what?” he gasped.

The masked man who sat at the head of the table turned his bright eyes upon the proprietor of the establishment.
===Chinese===
通董事会室的门很宽。三个人押着他们的囚徒，扎堆立在屋子中央，僵得一动也不能动。

桌边坐着的四个人一声不响。

布莱克第一个回过神来。他抢前一步，又停住。脸皮抽动，嘴巴张开，却吐不出半个字。

「什么——什么？」他喘着气说。

坐在桌上首的那个蒙面人把明亮的目光转到这家买卖的主人身上。

===Original===
“You did not expect me, Mr. Olloroff?” he said bluntly.

“My name is Black,” said the other violently. “What are you doing here?”

“That you shall discover,” said the masked man. “There are seats.”

Then Black saw that seats had been arranged at the farther end of the table.

“First of all,” the masked man went on, “I will relieve you of your prisoner. You take those handcuffs off, Sparks.”
===Chinese===
「没想到我会来吧，奥洛罗夫先生？」他直截了当地说。

「我叫布莱克。」对方恶声恶气地说，「你们到这里来干什么？」

「这个你慢慢就会明白。」蒙面人说，「座位都备好了。」

布莱克这才看见，桌子另一头确实摆好了几把椅子。

「首先，」蒙面人接着说，「我要请你把囚徒交出来。斯帕克斯，把手铐解了。」

===Original===
The man fumbled in his pocket for the key, but not in his waistcoat pocket﻿—his hand went farther down.

“Keep your hand up,” said the man at the table, sharply. He made a little gesture with his hand, and Black’s servant saw the gleam of a pistol.

“You need have no fear,” he went on, “our little business will have no tragic sequence tonight﻿—tonight!” he repeated significantly. “You have had three warnings from us, and we have come to deliver the last in person.”

Black was fast recovering his presence of mind.

“Why not report to the police?” he scoffed.
===Chinese===
那人伸手到口袋里摸钥匙，却不是摸背心口袋——他的手往更下面去了。

「手放在上面别动。」桌边那人厉声喝道。他略一抬手做了个小小的手势，布莱克的仆人便看见手枪一闪。

「不必害怕。」他接着说，「我们这桩小小的买卖，今晚不会闹出人命——今晚！」他意味深长地重复了一遍，「你们已经从我们手里接过三回警告，这一回，我们亲自来送最后一回。」

布莱克的镇定正飞快地回笼。

「怎么不去报警啊？」他讥笑道。

===Original===
“That we shall do in good time,” was the polite reply, “but I warn you personally, Black, that you have almost reached the end of your tether.”

In some ways Black was no coward. With an oath, he whipped out a revolver and sprang into the room.

As he did so the room went dark, and Frank found himself seized by a pair of strong hands and wrenched from the loose grip of his captor.

He was pushed forward, a door slammed behind him. He found himself tumbling down the carpeted stairs into the hall below. Quick hands removed the handcuffs from his wrists, the street door was opened by somebody who evidently knew the ways of the house, and he found himself, a little bewildered, in the open street, with two men in evening dress by his side.

They still wore their masks. There was nothing to distinguish either of them from the ordinary man in the street.
===Chinese===
「时机一到，自然会报。」回答客客气气，「不过我亲自警告你，布莱克，你折腾到头的日子差不多到了。」

布莱克在某些方面倒不是懦夫。他咒骂一声，拔出左轮手枪，跃进屋来。

就在这一瞬，屋里灯灭，弗兰克只觉一双有力的手抓住自己，把他从看守松松的钳制里硬拽了出去。

他被推着往前走，身后一扇门砰地关上。他跌跌撞撞下了铺地毯的楼梯，进了楼下的门厅。几只麻利的手解掉他腕上的手铐，临街的大门被一个显然熟门熟路的人打开，他晕晕乎乎地发现自己已经站在空旷的街上，身边是两个穿晚礼服的人。

他们脸上还蒙着面。随便哪一个，同街上寻常路人都没有分别。

===Original===
“This is your way, Mr. Fellowe,” said one, and he pointed up the street in the direction of Victoria.

Frank hesitated. He was keen to see the end of this adventure. Where were the other two of this vigilant four? Why had they been left behind? What were they doing?

His liberators must have guessed his thoughts, for one of them said, “Our friends are safe, do not trouble about them. You will oblige us, constable, by going very quickly.”

With a word of thanks, Frank Fellowe turned and walked quickly up the street. He looked back once, but the two men had disappeared into the darkness.
===Chinese===
「您这边走，费洛先生。」其中一人说，朝街那头维多利亚（Victoria）的方向指了指。

弗兰克犹豫了一下。他很想看看这场冒险的结局。这警觉的四人帮，另外两个在哪儿？为什么把他们留在了后面？他们在干什么？

救他的人想必猜中了他的心思，因为其中一个说：「我们的朋友平安无事，不必为他们操心。警员先生，劳驾，请您快些走。」

弗兰克·费洛道了声谢，转身快步沿街走去。他回头看了一眼，那两个人已经消失在黑暗里。

## VI The Policeman and a Lady / 六 警察与淑女

===Original===
Frank Fellowe was agitating a punchball in one of the upper rooms of his little cottage, and with good reason.

He was “taking out” of the ball all the grievances he had against the petty irritants of life.

Sergeant Gurden had bothered him with a dozen and one forms of petty annoyance. He had been given the least congenial of jobs; he had been put upon melancholy point work; and he seemed to be getting more than his share of extra duty. And, in addition, he had the extra worry of checking, at the same time, the work of Black’s organization. He might, had he wished, put away all the restrictions which hampered his movements, but that was not his way. The frustration of Black’s plans was one of Frank’s absorbing passions. If he had other passions which threatened to be equally absorbing, he had the sense to check them﻿—for a while﻿—

The daughter of a millionaire, violently introduced, subsequently met with heart-flutterings on the one side and not a little perturbation on the other; her gratitude and admiration began on a wayward two-seater with defective brakes, and progressed by way of the Zoo, for which she sent him a Sunday ticket﻿—for she was anxious to see just what he was like.

She went in some fear of disillusionment, because an heroic constable in uniform, whose face is neatly arranged by helmet-peak and chin-strap, may be less heroic in clothes of his own choosing, to say nothing of cravats and shoes.
===Chinese===
弗兰克·费洛（Frank Fellowe）正在自家小屋楼上一间屋里捶打一只拳击吊球，而且理由十足。

他正把生活里那些鸡毛蒜皮的窝火，一股脑「发泄」在这只吊球上。

格登巡佐（Sergeant Gurden）用一大堆零零碎碎的烦扰折腾他。最不对脾气的差事派给他；枯燥的点岗让他站；额外的勤务，他摊上的似乎也总比旁人多。除此以外，他还添了一重心事：同时暗中盯查布莱克（Black）那套班底的动静。他只要愿意，本可以甩开这一切束缚手脚的条条框框，可那不是他的做派。搅了布莱克的局，是弗兰克的一大痴念。他若还有别的痴念眼看要同样地入骨，他倒有自知之明，晓得按住——暂且按住——

一位百万富翁的女儿，同他结识得惊险激烈，随后一个心旌摇曳，另一个也不无意乱情迷；她的感激与倾慕，始于一辆刹车失灵、横冲直撞的双座小车，又经动物园一路升温——她还给他寄了一张星期日的门票——因为她急欲看看，他究竟是个什么样的人。

她去时多少怕幻灭，因为穿制服的英勇警员，有头盔帽檐和下巴系带把一张脸收拾得规规矩矩，一旦换上自家挑的衣服，那份英武未必还在，更别提领带和皮鞋了。

===Original===
But she braced herself for the humiliation of discovering that one who could save her life could also wear a ready-made tie. She was terribly self-conscious, kept to the unfrequented walks of the Zoo, and was found by a very good-looking gentleman who was dressed irreproachably in something that suggested neither the butcher’s boy at a beanfeast nor a plumber at a funeral.

She showed him the inmates of exactly two cages, then he took her in hand and told her things about wild beasts that she had never known before. He showed her the subtle distinction between five varieties of lynx, and gave her little anecdotes of the jungle fellowship that left her breathless with admiration. Moreover, he took her to the most unlikely places﻿—to rooms where the sick and lame of the animal kingdom were nursed to health. It would appear that there was no need to have sent him the ticket, because he was a Fellow of the Society.

There was too much to be seen on one day. She went again and yet again; rode with him over Hampstead Heath in the early hours of the morning. She gathered that he jobbed his horse, yet it was not always the same animal he rode.

“How many horses have you in your stable?” she asked banteringly one morning.

“Six,” he said readily. “You see,” he added hastily, “I do a lot of hunting in the season﻿—”
===Chinese===
但她还是鼓足了劲，预备承受那份难堪——万一发现救过她命的人，居然也系现成的成品领带。她局促得要命，专拣动物园里人迹罕至的小径走，结果被一位十分英俊的先生找到了；他衣着无可挑剔，既不像赴宴的肉铺伙计，也不像葬礼上的水电工。

她拢共给他看了两座笼子，随后便由他主导，听他讲起种种她从未听过的野兽门道。他指给她看五种猞猁之间的微妙差别，讲起丛林里伙伴交情的小掌故，听得她惊叹连连。不但如此，他还带她去了最想不到的地方——那些养护着动物王国的伤病员的屋子。看来那张门票原是多余寄的，因为他自己就是动物学会的会员。

一天看不完的东西太多了。她去了一趟又一趟；清晨最早的时辰，同他一起骑马跑过汉普斯特德荒原（Hampstead Heath）。她听得出来，他的马是租的，可骑的又不总是同一匹。

「你马厩里到底有几匹马？」一天早晨，她打趣地问。

「六匹。」他随口便答。「你瞧，」他又赶忙补充，「打猎的季节我常去打猎——」

===Original===
He stopped, realizing that he was further in the mire.

“But you are a constable﻿—a policeman!” she stammered. “I mean﻿—forgive me if I’m rude.”

He turned in his saddle, and there was a twinkle in his eye.

“I have a little money of my own,” he said. “You see, I have only been a constable for twelve months; previous to that I﻿—I wasn’t a constable!”

He was not very lucid: by this time he was apparently embarrassed, and she changed the subject, wondering and absurdly pleased.
===Chinese===
他顿住了，明白自己越描越黑。

「可你是个警员——是个警察呀！」她结结巴巴地说，「我是说——若有冒昧，请原谅。」

他在马鞍上侧过身，眼里闪着一丝狡黠。

「我自己有一点钱。」他说，「你瞧，我当警员才十二个月；在那之前，我——我还不是警员呢！」

他说得不很明白：到这时他显然窘了，她便岔开话题，心里又惊奇，又莫名其妙地欢喜。

===Original===
It was inconsistent of her to realize after the ride that these meetings were wrong. They were wrong before, surely? Was it worse to ride with a man who had revealed himself to be a member of one’s own class than with a policeman? Nevertheless, she knew it was wrong and met him﻿—and that is where Constable Fellowe and Miss Sandford became “May” and “Frank” to one another. There had been nothing clandestine in their meetings.

Theodore Sandford, a hardheaded man, was immensely democratic.

He joked about May’s policeman, made ponderous references to stolen visits to his palatial kitchen in search of rabbit-pie, and then there arose from a jesting nothing the question of Frank’s remaining in the force. He had admitted that he had independent means. Why remain a ridiculous policeman?

From jest it had passed into a very serious discussion and the presentation of an ultimatum, furiously written, furiously posted, and as furiously regretted.

Theodore Sandford looked up from his writing-table with an amused smile.
===Chinese===
