Essley made the superficial tests. There was not even this invariable symptom.

A dark figure, pressed close to the wall outside, listened. He was standing by the shuttered window. He held to his ear a little ebonite tube with a microphonic receiver, and the rubber which covered the bell-like end was pressed against the shutter.

For half an hour he stood thus, almost motionless, then he withdrew silently and disappeared into the shadows of the orange grove that grew in the centre of the long garden.
===Chinese===
「未必。」埃斯利说，「瞳孔会收缩，那是不会变的征兆。」

「那也尽管找。」老头得意洋洋地说。

埃斯利做了几项粗浅的检验。连这不会变的征兆也没有。

外头一条黑影紧贴着墙，正在听。他站在关着窗板的窗子旁，耳边举着一支带微音听筒的胶木管，喇叭口状的橡皮头贴在窗板上。

他这样站了半个钟头，几乎纹丝不动，然后悄悄退走，没入长院中央那片橘林的暗影里。
===Original===
As he did so, the door of the house opened and, with lantern in hand, Cajalos showed his visitor into the street.

“The devils are greener than ever,” chuckled the old man. “Hey! there will be happenings, my brother!”

Essley said nothing. He wanted to be in the street again. He stood quivering with nervous impatience as the old man unfastened the heavy door, and when it swung open he almost leapt into the street outside.

“Goodbye,” he said.

“Go with God,” said the old man, and the door closed noiselessly.
===Chinese===
就在这时，屋门开了，卡哈洛斯提着灯笼，送客人到街上。

「魔鬼们比往常更绿了。」老头咯咯笑道，「嘿！要出大事了，我的兄弟！」

埃斯利一言不发。他只想赶紧回到街上。老头开那扇沉重的大门时，他站在一边，紧张得不耐烦地发着抖；门一荡开，他几乎是一跃跳到了外头街上。

「再见。」他说。

「愿上帝与你同行。」老头说，门无声地关上了。

## XI To Lincoln Races / 十一 赴林肯赛马场

===Original===
Sir Isaac Tramber went to Lincoln in an evil frame of mind. He had reserved a compartment, and cursed his luck when he discovered that his reservation adjoined that of Horace Gresham.

He paced the long platform at King’s Cross, waiting for his guests. The Earl of Verlond had promised to go down with him and to bring Lady Mary, and it was no joy to Sir Isaac to observe on the adjoining carriage the label, “Reserved for Mr. Horace Gresham and party.”

Horace came along about five minutes before the train started. He was as cheerful as the noonday sun, in striking contrast to Sir Isaac, whose night had not been too wisely spent. He nodded carelessly to Sir Isaac’s almost imperceptible greeting.

The baronet glanced at his watch and inwardly swore at the old earl and his caprices. It wanted three minutes to the hour at which the train left. His tongue was framing a bitter indictment of the old man when he caught a glimpse of his tall, angular figure striding along the platform.

“Thought we weren’t coming. I suppose?” asked the earl, as he made his way to the compartment.
===Chinese===
艾萨克·特兰伯爵士（Sir Isaac Tramber）去林肯时，一肚子恶气。他订了包厢，一发现订的包厢紧挨着霍勒斯·格雷沙姆（Horace Gresham）的，便咒骂自己的运气。

他在国王十字车站（King's Cross）长长的站台上踱来踱去，等他的客人。弗隆德勋爵（Lord Verlond）答应同他一道下乡，还带上玛丽女勋爵（Lady Mary）；而看见隔壁车厢上贴着「霍勒斯·格雷沙姆先生暨亲友专用」的标签，艾萨克爵士心里实在不是滋味。

开车前约莫五分钟，霍勒斯来了。他快活得像正午的太阳，同艾萨克爵士恰成对照——爵士这一夜过得可不怎么高明。艾萨克爵士几乎察觉不出地颔首致意，他漫不经心地点头回礼。

从男爵看了看表，心里暗骂老伯爵和他的怪脾气。离开车时刻只差三分钟。他舌尖上正酿着对老头儿的一篇毒辣檄文，一眼瞥见那瘦长多棱的身影正沿站台大步走来。

「以为我们不来了，是不是？」伯爵边朝包厢走边问。

===Original===
“I say, you thought we weren’t coming?” he repeated, as Lady Mary entered the compartment, assisted with awkward solicitude by Sir Isaac.

“Well, I didn’t expect you to be late.”

“We are not late,” said the earl.

He settled himself comfortably in a corner seat﻿—the seat which Sir Isaac had specially arranged for the girl. Friends of his and of the old man who passed nodded. An indiscreet few came up to speak.

“Going up to Lincoln, Lord Verlond?” asked one idle youth.
===Chinese===
「我说，你们以为我们不来了？」他重复一遍。这时玛丽女勋爵进了包厢，艾萨克爵士殷勤相扶，动作却笨手笨脚。

「呃，我没料到你们会迟到。」

「我们没有迟到。」伯爵说。

他舒舒服服地在角落座位里安顿下来——那正是艾萨克爵士特意为姑娘安排的座位。过路的朋友，他俩各自的熟人，纷纷点头。有几个不知轻重的还凑上来搭话。

「去林肯吗，弗隆德勋爵？」一个闲得发慌的年轻人问。

===Original===
“No,” said the earl sweetly, “I am going to bed with the mumps.” He snarled the last word, and the young seeker after information fled.

“You can sit by me, Ikey﻿—leave Mary alone,” said the old man sharply. “I want to know all about this horse. I have £150 on this thoroughbred of yours; it is far more important than those fatuous inquiries you intend making of my niece.”

“Inquiries?” grumbled Sir Isaac resentfully.

“Inquiries!” repeated the other. “You want to know whether she slept last night; whether she finds it too warm in this carriage; whether she would like a corner seat or a middle seat, her back to the engine or her face to the engine. Leave her alone, leave her alone, Ikey. She’ll decide all that. I know her better than you.”

He glared, with that amusing glint in his eyes, across at the girl.
===Chinese===
「不，」伯爵甜滋滋地说，「我这是得了腮腺炎，上床躺着去。」最后一个词他是吼出来的，那位打探消息的年轻人落荒而逃。

「你坐我旁边，艾基（Ikey）——别缠着玛丽。」老头儿厉声道，「我要知道这匹马的一切。我在你这匹纯种马上押了 150 镑；这可比你打算问我外甥女的那堆蠢话要紧得多。」

「蠢话？」艾萨克爵士愤愤地嘟囔。

「蠢话！」对方重复道，「你想知道她昨晚睡得好不好；她觉不觉得这车厢太热；她愿意坐角落还是坐中间，背朝车头还是脸朝车头。别缠她了，别缠她了，艾基。这些她自己会拿主意。我比你懂她。」

他朝姑娘瞪过去，眼里闪着那点逗人的光。

===Original===
“Young Gresham is in the next carriage. Go and tap at the window and bring him out. Go along!”

“He’s got some friends there, I think, uncle,” said the girl.

“Never mind about his friends,” said Verlond irritably. “What the devil does it matter about his friends? Aren’t you a friend? Go and tap at the door and bring him out.”

Sir Isaac was fuming.

“I don’t want him in here,” he said loudly. “You seem to forget, Verlond, that if you want to talk about horses, this is the very chap who should know nothing about Timbolino.”
===Chinese===
「小格雷沙姆在隔壁车厢。去敲敲窗，把他叫出来。去吧！」

「我想他那儿还有朋友呢，叔叔。」姑娘说。

「管他什么朋友。」弗隆德烦躁地说，「他的朋友关我们什么事？你不就是他的朋友？去敲敲门，把他叫出来。」

艾萨克爵士直冒烟。

「我不要他到这儿来。」他大声说，「你好像忘了，弗隆德，咱们要谈马，而这家伙恰恰正是不该对蒂姆博利诺（Timbolino）知道半点风声的人。」

===Original===
“Ach!” said the earl testily, “don’t you suppose he knows all there is to be known. What do you think sporting papers are for?”

“Sporting papers can’t tell a man what the owner knows,” said Sir Isaac importantly.

“They tell me more than he knows,” he said. “Your horse was favourite yesterday morning﻿—it isn’t favourite any more, Ikey.”

“I can’t control the investments of silly asses,” grumbled Sir Isaac.

“Except one,” said the earl rudely. “But these silly asses you refer to do not throw their money away﻿—remember that, Ikey. When you have had as much racing as I have had, and won as much money as I have won, you’ll take no notice of what owners think of their horses. You might as well ask a mother to give a candid opinion of her own daughter’s charms as to ask an owner for unbiased information about his own horse.”
===Chinese===
「啊！」伯爵不耐烦地说，「你以为他不知道？该知道的他全知道。你说体育报是干什么用的？」

「体育报可告诉不了人马主才知道的事。」艾萨克爵士郑重其事地说。

「它们告诉我的，比他知道的多。」他说，「你那匹马昨天早晨还是热门——如今不是了，艾基。」

「蠢驴们怎么投钱，我管不着。」艾萨克爵士嘟囔。

「有一头你管得着。」伯爵粗鲁地说，「不过你说的这帮蠢驴，钱可不是乱扔的——记住这一点，艾基。等你赛马赛得同我一样多，赢钱赢得同我一样多，你就不会把马主对自己马的看法当回事了。要马主对自己的马说句公道话，同要一位母亲客观评价亲闺女的姿色，是一码事。」

===Original===
The train had slipped through the grimy purlieus of London and was now speeding through green fields to Hatfield. It was a glorious spring day, mellow with sunlight: such a day as a man at peace with the world might live with complete enjoyment.

Sir Isaac was not in this happy position, nor was he in a mood to discuss either the probity of racing men or the general question of the sport itself.

He observed with an inward curse the girl rise and walk, apparently carelessly, into the corridor. He could have sworn he heard a tap at the window of the next compartment, but in this, of course, he was wrong. She merely moved across the vision of the little coterie who sat laughing and talking, and in an instant Horace had come out.

“It is not my fault this, really,” she greeted him, with a little flush in her cheeks. “It was uncle’s idea.”

“Your uncle is an admirable old gentleman,” said Horace fervently. “I retract anything I may have said to his discredit.”
===Chinese===
火车早已溜过伦敦灰扑扑的边缘，此刻正穿过绿野，向哈特菲尔德（Hatfield）疾驰。这是个大好的春日，阳光醇厚：这样的日子，一个与世界相安无事的人，尽可以活得畅快淋漓。

艾萨克爵士既没有这份安然，也没有雅兴去讨论赛马人的操守，或赛马这桩运动本身的大道理。

他眼看着姑娘起身，作出全不在意的样子走进过道，心里暗骂了一句。他几乎可以发誓，自己听见隔壁包厢的窗上响了一下敲击——这一点当然是他想岔了。姑娘不过从那一小圈谈笑的人的视野里走过了一趟，转眼间霍勒斯就出来了。

「真的，这可不能怪我。」她迎上去打招呼，颊上微微泛红，「是叔叔的主意。」

「令叔是一位可敬的老先生。」霍勒斯热诚地说，「我从前说过他什么不敬的话，一律收回。」

===Original===
“I will tell him,” she said, with mock gravity.

“No, no,” cried Horace, “I don’t want you to do that exactly.”

“I want to talk to you seriously,” said she suddenly. “Come into our compartment. Uncle and Sir Isaac are so busy discussing the merits of Timbolino﻿—is that the right name?” He nodded, his lips twitching with amusement.

“That they won’t notice anything we have to say,” she concluded.

The old earl gave him a curt nod. Sir Isaac only vouchsafed a scowl. It was difficult to maintain anything like a confidential character in their conversation, but by manoeuvring so that they spoke only of the more important things when Sir Isaac and his truculent guest were at the most heated point of their argument, she was able to unburden the anxiety of her mind.
===Chinese===
「我要告诉他的。」她装出一本正经。

「别，别。」霍勒斯嚷道，「这个嘛，倒不必真去告诉他。」

「我想同你正经谈谈。」她忽然说，「到我们包厢来。叔叔和艾萨克爵士正忙着议论蒂姆博利诺的优点——是叫这个名字吧？」他点点头，嘴角忍着笑直抽动。

「忙得顾不上留意我们说什么。」她补完这句。

老伯爵朝他敷衍地点了点头。艾萨克爵士只赏了一记怒目。两人的谈话要维持几分私密，实在不易；好在姑娘调度得当，专挑艾萨克爵士同他那位凶巴巴的客人吵到最热闹的当口，才说那些更要紧的话，总算把心里的焦灼卸了下来。

===Original===
“I am worried about uncle,” she said in a low tone.

“Is he ill?” asked Horace.

She shook her head.

“No, it isn’t his illness﻿—yet it may be. But he is so contradictory; I am so afraid that it might react to our disadvantage. You know how willing he was that you should﻿ ﻿…”

She hesitated, and his hand sought hers under the cover of an open newspaper.
===Chinese===
「我在替叔叔担心。」她低声说。

「他病了？」霍勒斯问。

她摇头。

「不，不是病——可也许就成病。他就是这么反复无常；我真怕他一个反扑，反倒坏了我们的事。你知道，他是多么情愿你……」

她迟疑了。他的一只手在一张摊开的报纸掩护下，摸索到她的手。

===Original===
“It was marvellous,” he whispered, “wasn’t it? I never expected for one moment that the old dev﻿—that your dear uncle,” he corrected himself, “would have been so amenable.”

She nodded again.

“You see,” she said, taking advantage of another heated passage between the old man and the irritated baronet, “what he does so impetuously he can undo just as easily. I am so afraid he will turn and rend you.”

“Let him try,” said Horace. “I am not easily rent.”

Their conversation was cut short abruptly by the intervention of the man they were discussing.
===Chinese===
「简直神了。」他悄声说，「是不是？我压根儿没想到，那老恶——你亲爱的叔叔，」他更正自己，「会这样好说话。」

她又点头。

「你瞧，」她趁老人同恼怒的从男爵又吵起来的当口说，「他一时兴起办的事，也能一时兴起就翻掉。我真怕他回过头来把你撕了。」

「让他试试。」霍勒斯说，「我可不是轻易撕得烂的。」

两人的谈话，冷不丁被他们正议论的那个人打断了。

===Original===
“Look here, Gresham,” snapped the earl shortly, “you’re one of the cognoscenti, and I suppose you know everything. Who are the ‘Four Just Men’ I hear people talking about?”

Horace was conscious of the fact that the eyes of Sir Isaac Tramber were fixed on him curiously. He was a man who made no disguise of his suspicion.

“I know no more than you,” said Horace. “They seem to me to be an admirable body of people who go about correcting social evils.”

“Who are they to judge what is and what is not evil?” growled the earl, scowling from under his heavy eyebrows. “Infernal cheek! What do we pay judges and jurymen and coroners and policemen and people of that sort for, eh? What do we pay taxes for, and rent for, and police rates, and gas rates, and water rates, and every kind of dam’ rate that the devilish ingenuity of man can devise? Do we do it that these jackanapes can come along and interfere with the course of justice? It’s absurd! It’s ridiculous!” he stormed.

Horace threw out a protesting hand.
===Chinese===
「我说，格雷沙姆。」伯爵没好气地打断，「你是行家，想必无所不知。大家嘴里念叨的『四义士』（the Four Just Men），都是些什么人？」

霍勒斯察觉到，艾萨克·特兰伯爵士的两只眼睛正好奇地盯着自己。这人从不掩饰自己的疑心。

「我知道的不比你多。」霍勒斯说，「依我看，那是一群可敬的人物，四处纠察社会罪恶。」

「轮得到他们来断定什么是恶、什么不是恶吗？」伯爵咆哮，浓眉底下怒目圆睁，「胆大包天！我们养着法官、陪审员、验尸官、警察，还有那一号人，是为的什么，呃？我们缴税、缴租、缴治安捐、缴煤气捐、缴水捐，缴人类魔鬼般的巧思想得出来的每一种混账捐，是为的什么？是为了让这几个跳梁小丑跑出来，搅扰司法的进程吗？荒唐！可笑！」他大发雷霆。

霍勒斯摊开一只手，以示抗议。

===Original===
“Don’t blame me,” he said.

“But you approve of them,” accused the earl. “Ikey says you do, and Ikey knows everything﻿—don’t you, Ikey?”

Sir Isaac shifted uncomfortably in his seat.

“I didn’t say Gresham knew anything about it,” he began lamely.

“Why do you lie, Ikey; why do you lie?” asked the old man testily. “You just told me that you were perfectly sure that Gresham was one of the leading spirits of the gang.”
===Chinese===
「别怪我。」他说。

「可你赞成他们。」伯爵控诉道，「艾基说的，艾基无所不知——是不是，艾基？」

艾萨克爵士在座位上不自在地挪了挪。

「我没说格雷沙姆知道内情啊。」他无力地辩解。

「你为什么撒谎，艾基？为什么撒谎？」老头儿不耐烦地问，「你刚才还说，你拿得稳格雷沙姆就是那伙人的头领之一。」

===Original===
Sir Isaac, inured as he was to the brutal indiscretions of his friends, went a dull red.

“Oh, I didn’t mean that exactly,” he said awkwardly and a little angrily. “Dash it, Lord Verlond, don’t embarrass a fellow by rendering him liable to heavy damages an all that sort of thing.”

Horace was unperturbed by the other’s confusion.

“You needn’t bother yourself,” he said coolly. “I should never think of taking you to a court of justice.”

He turned again to the girl, and the earl claimed the baronet’s attention. The old man had a trick of striking off at a tangent; from one subject to another he leapt like a will-o’-the-wisp. Before Horace had framed half a dozen words the old man was dragging his unwilling victim along a piscatorial road, and Sir Isaac was floundering out of his depths in a morass﻿—if the metaphor be excused﻿—of salmon-fishing, trout-poaching, pike-fishing﻿—a sport on which Sir Isaac Tramber could by no means deem himself an authority.
===Chinese===
艾萨克爵士纵然习惯了朋友们的口无遮拦，也涨红了脸，红得发暗。

「哦，我不是那个意思。」他又窘又恼地说，「该死，弗隆德勋爵，别害人背上巨额诽谤赔偿什么的，叫人下不来台。」

对方的狼狈，霍勒斯毫不动容。

「您不必担心。」他冷冷地说，「把您告上法庭这种事，我连想都不会想。」

他重新转向姑娘，伯爵则把从男爵的注意力据为己有。老头儿有个本事，说着说着就斜刺里杀出去；一个话题跳到另一个话题，活像鬼火。霍勒斯这边还没凑齐半打字，老头儿已经拖着他那不情愿的猎物奔上了捕鱼的大道，艾萨克爵士则在一片泥沼里越陷越深——恕我打个比方——鲑鱼垂钓、鳟鱼偷猎、狗鱼钓捕，样样都是艾萨克·特兰伯爵士万万不敢自居权威的学问。

===Original===
It was soon after lunch that the train pulled into Lincoln. Horace usually rented a house outside the town, but this year he had arranged to go and return to London on the same night. At the station he parted with the girl.

“I shall see you on the course,” he said. “What are your arrangements? Do you go back to town tonight?”

She nodded.

“Is this a very important race for you to win?” she asked, a little anxiously.

He shook his head.
===Chinese===
午饭后不久，火车进了林肯站。往年霍勒斯照例在城外租一幢宅子，今年他却安排妥当，当晚便回伦敦。站台上，他同姑娘分了手。

「赛马场上见。」他说，「你们怎么安排？今晚回城吗？」

她点头。

「这场比赛，你赢下来很要紧吗？」她有点不放心地问。

他摇头。

===Original===
“Nobody really bothers overmuch about the Lincolnshire Handicap,” he said. “You see, it’s too early in the season for even the gamblers to put their money down with any assurance. One doesn’t know much, and it is almost impossible to tell what horses are in form. I verily believe that Nemesis will win but everything is against her.

“You see, the Lincoln,” continued Horace doubtfully, “is a race which is not usually won by a filly, and then, too, she is a sprinter. I know sprinters have won the race before, and every year have been confidently expected to win it again; but the averages are all against a horse like Nemesis.”

“But I thought,” she said in wonder, “that you were so confident about her.”

He laughed a little.

“Well, you know, one is awfully confident on Monday and full of doubts on Tuesday. That is part of the game; the form of horses is not half as inconsistent as the form of owners. I shall probably meet a man this morning who will tell me that some horse is an absolute certainty for the last race of the day. He will hold me by the buttonhole and he will drum into me the fact that this is the most extraordinarily easy method of picking up money that was ever invented since racing started. When I meet him after the last race he will coolly inform me that he did not back that horse, but had some tip at the last moment from an obscure individual who knew the owner’s aunt’s sister. You mustn’t expect one to be consistent.
===Chinese===
「林肯郡让磅赛（Lincolnshire Handicap），其实谁都不太当回事。」他说，「你瞧，赛季才开头，连赌客们押起钱来都没有几分把握。各马的底细人所知甚少，哪些马正在状态，几乎无从判断。我真心相信『复仇女神』（Nemesis）会赢——可一切都对它不利。

「你瞧，林肯这场赛，」霍勒斯迟疑地接着说，「向来不是小母马赢的赛，何况它还是匹短跑马。我知道短跑马从前赢过，而且年年都有人信心十足地指望它再赢；可种种平均概率，都不站在『复仇女神』这样的马一边。」

「可我还以为，」她惊奇地说，「你对它十拿九稳呢。」

他笑了笑。

「唉，你是知道的，人星期一信心百倍，星期二满腹疑云。这也是这一行的常事；马的状态反反复复，还抵不上马主状态的一半。今天上午我说不定就会碰上一位先生，告诉我某匹马在当天最后一场里是板上钉钉。他会揪住我的纽扣眼，向我死灌一通：这是自有赛马以来发明过的天下第一等容易的捡钱法子。等最后一场跑完我再碰见他，他会凉凉地通知我，那匹马他没押，临了倒是听了哪位无名氏的内线消息——那人认识马主姨妈的妹妹。你别指望人前后一致。

===Original===
“I still think Nemesis will win,” he went on, “but I am not so confident as I was. The most cocksure of students gets a little glum in the face of the examiner.”

The earl had joined them and was listening to the conversation with a certain amount of grim amusement.

“Ikey is certain Timbolino will win,” he said, “even in the face of the examiner. Somebody has just told me that the examiner is rather soft under foot.”

“You mean the course?” asked Horace, a little anxiously.

The earl nodded.
===Chinese===
「我仍旧认为『复仇女神』会赢。」他接着说，「可我不像先前那么笃定了。再自负的学生，到了考官面前，也要蔫上几分。」

伯爵已踱到他们这边，正带着几分冷冷的有趣听这场谈话。

「艾基拿稳了蒂姆博利诺会赢。」他说，「连考官面前也不含糊。刚有人告诉我，这考官脚下发软。」

「您是说赛道？」霍勒斯问，有点着急。

伯爵点头。

===Original===
“It won’t suit yours, my friend,” he said. “A sprinter essaying the Lincolnshire wants good going. I can see myself taking £1,500 back to London today.”

“Have you backed Timbolino?”

“Don’t ask impertinent questions,” said the earl curtly. “And unnecessary questions,” he went on. “You know infernally well I’ve backed Timbolino. Don’t you believe me? I’ve backed it and I’m afraid I’m not going to win.”

“Afraid?”

Whatever faults the old man had, Horace knew him for a good loser.
===Chinese===
「对你那匹可不合意，我的朋友。」他说，「短跑马闯林肯郡，要的是好跑道。我仿佛看见自己今天揣着 1,500 镑回伦敦了。」

「您押了蒂姆博利诺？」

「别问放肆的问题。」伯爵简短地说，「也别问多余的问题。」他接着说，「我押没押蒂姆博利诺，你清楚得很。不信？我押了，可惜怕是赢不了。」

「怕是？」

老头儿纵有千般不是，霍勒斯知道，他输得起。

===Original===
The earl nodded.

He was not amused now. He had dropped like a cloak the assumption of that little unpleasant leering attitude. He was, Horace saw for the first time, a singularly good-looking old man. The firm lines of the mouth were straight, and the pale face, in repose, looked a little sad.

“Yes, I’m afraid,” he said. His voice was even and without the bitter quality of cynicism which was his everlasting pose.

“This race makes a lot of difference to some people. It doesn’t affect me very much,” he said, and the corner of his mouth twitched a little. “But there are people,” he went on seriously, “to whom this race makes a difference between life and death.” There was a sudden return to his usual abrupt manner. “Eh? How does that strike you for good melodrama, Mr. Gresham?”

Horace shook his head in bewilderment.
===Chinese===
伯爵点点头。

他此刻毫无玩笑的意思。那副佞笑的不快嘴脸，像斗篷一样从他身上褪了下去。霍勒斯头一回看出，这是个出奇端正好看的老头儿。嘴角的坚线端端正正，苍白的脸一静下来，竟带着几分哀愁。

「是的，我怕赢不了。」他说。嗓音平平整整，不带他常年摆惯的那股刻薄的愤世味道。「这场比赛，对有些人关系重大。对我，倒无甚要紧。」他说着，嘴角微微一抽。「可是有些人，」他正色接着说，「这场比赛对他们，就是生死之别。」他忽然又回到平常那副突兀的做派。「呃？拿这个当耸人听闻的情节剧台词，够不够味，格雷沙姆先生？」

霍勒斯困惑地摇头。

「我怕我完全没听懂，弗隆德勋爵。」

===Original===
“I’m afraid I don’t follow you at all, Lord Verlond.”

“You may follow me in another way,” said the earl briskly. “Here is my car. Good morning.”

Horace watched him out of sight and then made his way to the racecourse.

The old man had puzzled him not a little. He bore, as Horace knew, a reputation which, if not unsavoury, was at least unpleasant. He was credited with having the most malicious tongue in London. But when Horace came to think, as he did, walking along the banks of the river on his way to the course, there was little that the old man had ever said which would injure or hurt innocent people. His cynicism was in the main directed against his own class, his savageness most manifested against notorious sinners. Men like Sir Isaac Tramber felt the lash of his tongue.

His treatment of his heir was, of course, inexcusable. The earl himself never excused it; he persistently avoided the subject, and it would be a bold man who would dare to raise so unpleasant a topic against the earl’s wishes.
===Chinese===
「那你可以换个法子跟着我。」伯爵干脆地说，「我的车到了。早安。」

霍勒斯望着他走远，随后自往赛马场去。

老头儿让他着实纳闷。此人名声在外，霍勒斯知道，虽说不上臭，至少惹人嫌。据说他长着伦敦最毒的一条舌头。可霍勒斯一路沿河岸走向赛场，细细想来，老头儿说过的话里，伤得着无辜之人的，其实寥寥。他的愤世多半对着本阶级放，他的狠辣最常落在声名狼藉的罪人身上。像艾萨克·特兰伯爵士那样的人，才尝得到他舌头的鞭子。

他对自己继承人的那套做法，当然无可宽宥。伯爵自己也从不宽宥；他一味避开这个话题，谁敢违背伯爵的意思提起这桩不快，那人可得有好大的胆子。

众所周知，他富得流油；霍勒斯·格雷沙姆也有理由庆幸，上苍偏偏也厚赏了他身外之财。否则他的前程就算不上光明。他本人豪富这一点，堵死了一切闲话（而这闲话本是免不了的）——说他觊觎玛丽女勋爵的家财。她究竟继承弗隆德的百万家产，还是两手空空地嫁给他，在他全然无关紧要。

===Original===
He was known to be extraordinarily wealthy, and Horace Gresham had reason for congratulating himself that he had been specially blessed with this world’s goods. Otherwise his prospects would not have been of the brightest. That he was himself enormously rich precluded any suggestion (and the suggestion would have been inevitable) that he hunted Lady Mary’s fortune. It was a matter of supreme indifference to himself whether she inherited the Verlond millions or whether she came to him empty-handed.

There were other people in Lincoln that day who did not take so philosophical a view of the situation.

Sir Isaac had driven straight to the house on the hill leading to the Minster, which Black had engaged for two days. He was in a very bad temper when at last he reached his destination. Black was sitting at lunch.

Black looked up as the other entered. “Hullo, Ikey,” he said, “come and sit down.”

Sir Isaac looked at the menu with some disfavour.
===Chinese===
那天在林肯，另有一些人，看局势可没有这般哲人风度。

艾萨克爵士径直驱车到了通往大教堂的那条山坡路上的宅子——布莱克（Black）租下了两天。抵达目的地时，他心情坏得很。布莱克正坐着吃午饭。

布莱克见人进来，抬头。「喂，艾基。」他说，「来坐下。」

艾萨克爵士不大满意地看了看菜单。

「谢了。」他没好气地说，「我在车上吃过了。我有话同你说。」

===Original===
“Thanks,” he said shortly, “I’ve lunched on the train. I want to talk to you.”

“Talk away,” said Black, helping himself to another cutlet. He was a good trencherman﻿—a man who found exquisite enjoyment in his meals.

“Look here. Black,” said Isaac, “things are pretty desperate. Unless that infernal horse of mine wins today I shall not know what to do for money.”

“I know one thing you won’t be able to do,” said Black coolly, “and that is, come to me. I am in as great straits as you.”
===Chinese===
「说吧。」布莱克说着，又给自己添了一块炸肉排。他是位好食客——一个能在饭食里品出至味的人。

「我说，布莱克。」艾萨克开口，「事情相当不妙。除非我那匹该死的马今天赢了，否则我真不知道上哪儿弄钱去。」

「有一件事你准干不成。」布莱克冷冷地说，「就是来找我。我的窘迫，不输给你。」

他推开盘子，从口袋里掏出雪茄匣。

===Original===
He pushed back his plate and took a cigar-case from his pocket.

“What do we stand to win on this Timbolino of yours?”

“About £25,000,” said Sir Isaac moodily. “I don’t know if the infernal thing will win. It would be just my luck if it doesn’t. I am afraid of this horse of Gresham’s.”

Black laughed softly.
===Chinese===
「你这匹蒂姆博利诺，咱们能有指望赢多少？」

「大约 25,000 镑。」艾萨克爵士愁眉苦脸地说，「这该死的畜生赢不赢，我没底。要是输了，正合我这运气。格雷沙姆那匹马，我心里发怵。」

布莱克轻轻笑了。

「这恐惧倒是新添的。」他说，「我记得从前没听你说过。」

===Original===
“That’s a new fear of yours,” he said. “I don’t remember having heard it before.”

“It’s no laughing matter,” said the other. “I had my trainer, Tubbs, down watching her work. She is immensely fast. The only thing is whether she can stay the distance.”

“Can’t she be got at?” asked Black.

“Got at!” said the other impatiently. “The race will be run in three hours’ time! Where do you get your idea of racing from?” he asked irritably. “You can’t poison horses at three hours’ notice. You can’t even poison them at three days’ notice, unless you’ve got the trainer in with you. And trainers of that kind only live in novels.”
===Chinese===
「这可不是好笑的事。」对方说，「我派了我的驯马师塔布斯（Tubbs）下去看它操练。它快得惊人。唯一的问题，是它撑不撑得住全程。」

「就不能做做它的手脚？」布莱克问。

「做手脚！」对方不耐烦地说，「三个钟头后就要开跑！你这套赛马的概念打哪儿来的？」他气冲冲地问，「三个钟头的预备，你毒不了马。三天的预备你也毒不了，除非驯马师是你一伙的。而那样的驯马师，只活在小说里。」

布莱克正细心地剪着雪茄头。

===Original===
Black was carefully cutting the end of his cigar.

“So if your horse loses we shall be in High Street, Hellboro’?” he reflected. “I have backed it to save my life.” He said this in grim earnest.

He rang a bell. The servant came in.

“Tell them to bring round the carriage,” he said. He looked at his watch. “I am not particularly keen on racing, but I think I shall enjoy this day in the open. It gives one a chance of thinking.”
===Chinese===
「这么说，你那马要是输了，咱们就只好上地狱镇的大街去挂牌了？」他寻思道，「我押它，押的是我的命。」这话他说得咬牙切齿，一字一板。

他按铃。仆人进来。

「叫他们把马车备过来。」他说。他看了看表。

「我对赛马本没什么特别的兴致，不过我想，今天这趟野外消磨，我会喜欢的。这正给人一个动脑子的机会。」

## XIII Who Are the Four? / 十三 四人是谁
===Original===
Lord Verlond sat at breakfast behind an open copy of *The Times*. Breakfast was ever an unsociable meal at Verlond House. Lady Mary, in her neat morning dress, was content to read her letters and her papers without expecting conversation from the old man.

He looked across at her. His face was thoughtful. In repose she had always thought it rather fine, and now his grave eyes were watching her with an expression she did not remember having seen before.

“Mary,” he asked abruptly, “are you prepared for a shock?”

She smiled, though somewhat uneasily. These shocks were often literal facts.

“I think I can survive it,” she said.

There was a long pause, during which his eyes did not leave her face.
===Chinese===
弗隆德勋爵坐在早餐桌前，面前摊着一份《泰晤士报》。在弗隆德宅邸，早餐从来不是用于交际的一餐。玛丽小姐穿着一身整洁的晨装，自顾自读着信件和报纸，并不指望同老人搭话。

他望向她。他一脸沉思。她一向觉得，他静下来的时候那张脸相当耐看，而此刻，他那双严肃的眼睛正带着一种她不记得见过的神情注视着她。

「玛丽，」他突如其来地问，「你做好受惊的准备了吗？」

她笑了，只是笑得有些不安。这类「惊吓」往往是有名有实的真事。

「我想我挺得住。」她说。

好一阵沉默，他的目光始终没有离开她的脸。
===Original===
“Would you be startled to know that that young demon of a brother of yours is still alive?”

“Alive!” she exclaimed, starting to her feet.

There was no need for the old man to ask exactly how she viewed the news. Her face was flushed with pleasure﻿—joy shone in her eyes.

“Oh, is it really true?” she cried.

“It’s true enough,” said the old man moodily. “Very curious how things turn out. I thought the young beggar was dead, didn’t you?”
===Chinese===
「倘若知道你那个小魔王弟弟还活着，你会吃惊吗？」

「还活着！」她惊呼一声，霍地站了起来。

老人无须再问她对这个消息究竟作何感想。她的脸高兴得通红——喜悦在她眼中闪闪发亮。

「哦，这是真的吗？」她叫道。

「千真万确。」老人闷闷不乐地说。「事情的演变真是奇怪得很。我还当那小无赖已经死了呢，你不也一样？」
===Original===
“Oh, don’t talk like that, uncle, you don’t mean it.”

“I mean it all right,” snapped the earl. “Why shouldn’t I? He was infernally rude to me. Do you know what he called me before he left?”

“But that was sixteen years ago,” said the girl.

“Sixteen grandmothers,” said the old man. “It doesn’t make any difference to me if it was sixteen hundred years﻿—he still said it. He called me a tiresome old bore﻿—what do you think of that?”

She laughed, and a responsive gleam came to the old man’s face.

“It’s all very well for you to laugh,” he said, “but it’s rather a serious business for a member of the House of Lords to be called a tiresome old bore by a youthful Etonian. Naturally, remembering his parting words and the fact that he had gone to America, added to the very important fact that I am a Churchman and a regular subscriber to Church institutions, I thought he was dead. After all, one expects some reward from an All-wise Providence.”
===Chinese===
「哦，别这么说，叔叔，你并不是当真的。」

「我就是当真的。」伯爵厉声说道。「我为什么不能当真？他对我的粗野简直无以复加。你知道他临走之前骂我什么吗？」

「可那是十六年前的事了。」姑娘说。

「十六个祖母也没用。」老人说。「哪怕过了一千六百年，对我也毫无分别——他总是说了那句话。他骂我是个招人嫌的老厌物——你说说，这算什么话？」

她笑了起来，老人脸上也随之掠过一丝会意的光。

「你笑倒容易，」他说，「可堂堂一个上议院议员，被一个伊顿毛头小子骂作招人嫌的老厌物，这可不是闹着玩的。自然，一想起他临别的那些话，想起他去了美国，再加上一条顶要紧的事实——我是国教徒，又常年捐输教会事业——我便认定他已经死了。说到底，人对全知全能的天意，总该指望一点回报。」
===Original===
“Where is he?” she asked.

“I don’t know,” said the earl. “I traced him to Texas﻿—apparently he was on a farm there until he was twenty-one. After that his movements seem to have been somewhat difficult to trace.”

“Why,” she said suddenly, pointing an accusing finger at him, “you’ve been trying to trace him.”

For a fraction of a second the old man looked confused.

“I’ve done nothing of the sort,” he snarled. “Do you think I’d spend my money to trace a rascal who﻿—”

“Oh, you have,” she went on. “I know you have. Why do you pretend to be such an awful old man?”
===Chinese===
「他在哪儿？」她问。

「我不知道。」伯爵说。「我追查到得克萨斯——他显然在那边一座农场待到二十一岁。此后他的行踪就不大好追了。」

「哎呀，」她突然说道，手指直直指着他，一副兴师问罪的架势，「你一直在设法找他。」

有一瞬工夫，老人显出了慌乱。

「我绝没有干过这种事。」他咆哮道。「你以为我会花钱去查一个混账——」

「哦，你就是查了。」她继续说。「我知道你查了。你为什么偏要装成这么一个可怕的老头儿？」
===Original===
“Anyway, I think he’s found out,” he complained. “It takes away a great deal of the fortune which would have come to you. I don’t suppose Gresham will want you now.”

She smiled. He rose from the table and went to the door.

“Tell that infernal villain﻿—”

“Which one?”

“James,” he replied, “that I’m not to be disturbed. I’m going to my study. I’m not to be disturbed by anyone for any reason; do you understand?”
===Chinese===
「不管怎么说，我看他是给人找着了。」他抱怨道。「这可去掉了本该落到你名下的一大笔财产。我看格雷沙姆如今怕是不会要你了。」

她微微一笑。他从桌边起身，朝门口走去。

「告诉那个十恶不赦的坏蛋——」

「哪一个？」

「詹姆斯，」他答道，「说我不许人打搅。我要去书房。无论何人、无论何事，都不许打搅我；明白吗？」
===Original===
If it was a busy morning for his lordship, it was no less so for Black and his friend, for it was Monday, and settling day, and in numerous clubs in London expectant bookmakers, in whose volumes the names of Black and Sir Isaac were freely inscribed, examined their watches with feelings that bordered upon apprehension.

But, to the surprise of everybody who knew the men, the settlements were made.

An accession of wealth had come to the “firm.”

Sir Isaac Tramber spent that afternoon pleasantly. He was raised from the depths of despair to the heights of exaltation. His debts of honour were paid; he felt it was possible for him to look the world in the face. As a taxi drove him swiftly to Black’s office, he was whistling gaily, and smiling at the politely veiled surprise of one of his suspicious bookmakers.

The big man was not at his office, and Sir Isaac, who had taken the precaution of instructing his driver to wait, redirected him to the Chelsea flat.
===Chinese===
如果说这天上午勋爵大人忙得不可开交，那么布莱克和他的朋友也毫不清闲——这天是星期一，是结账的日子，伦敦各家俱乐部里，一众心怀期盼的赌注登记人——他们的账簿上密密麻麻记着布莱克和艾萨克爵士的名字——频频看表，心情近乎惶恐。

然而出乎所有熟人意料的是，账竟然结清了。

这家「商号」进了一笔财。

艾萨克·特兰伯爵士那天下午过得快活得很。他从绝望的深渊被抬上了狂喜的巅峰。信誉债都还清了；他觉得自己又可以昂首挺胸见人了。出租汽车载着他飞快驶向布莱克的办公室，一路上他愉快地吹着口哨，冲着他那位满腹狐疑的赌注登记人礼貌而含蓄的惊讶微笑致意。

那大个子不在办公室，艾萨克爵士早有防备，吩咐过司机等候，便让他改道去切尔西的公寓。
===Original===
Black was dressing for dinner when Sir Isaac arrived.

“Hullo!” he said, motioning him to a seat. “You’re the man I want. I’ve got a piece of information that will please you. You are the sort of chap who is scared by these ‘Four Just Men.’ Well, you needn’t be any more. I’ve found out all about them. It’s cost me £200 to make the discovery, but it’s worth every penny.”

He looked at a sheet of paper lying before him.

“Here is the list of their names. A curious collection, eh? You wouldn’t suspect a Wesleyan of taking such steps as these chaps have taken. A bank manager in South London﻿—Mr. Charles Grimburd﻿—you’ve heard of him: he’s the art connoisseur, an unexpected person, eh? And Wilkinson Despard﻿—he’s the fellow I suspected most of all. I’ve been watching the papers very carefully. The *Post Herald*, the journal he writes for, has always been very well informed upon these outrages of the Four. They seem to know more about it than any other paper, and then, in addition, this man Despard has been writing pretty vigorously on social problems. He’s got a place in Jermyn Street. I put a man on to straighten his servant, who had been betting. He had lost money. My man has been at him for a couple of weeks. There they are.” He tossed the sheet across. “Less awe-inspiring than when they stick to their masks and their funny titles.”

Sir Isaac studied the list with interest.

“But there are only three here,” he said. “Who is the fourth?”
===Chinese===
艾萨克爵士赶到时，布莱克正在换晚礼服。

「喂！」他说着，示意他坐下。「我正要找你。我得到了一个准让你高兴的消息。你这种人，正是被那帮『四义士』吓破了胆的那种。好了，往后用不着再怕了。我把他们的底细全查清了。查出这个花了我 200 英镑，可这钱一个子儿都不冤枉。」

他看了看摊在面前的一页纸。

「这份就是他们的名单。凑在一起真是古怪透顶，嗯？你怎么也想不到一个卫斯理宗教徒会干出这帮家伙干的事。一位南伦敦的银行经理——查尔斯·格里姆伯德先生——你听说过他：就是那位艺术鉴赏家，想不到吧？还有威尔金森·德斯帕德——这个人我疑心最重。我一直非常留心各家报纸。《邮报先驱》，他撰稿的那家报纸，对四义士的这些暴行向来消息灵通，仿佛比任何一家报纸知道的都多；再说，这位德斯帕德先生对社会问题也一直笔锋很劲。他在杰明街有个住处。我派了个人去摆平他的仆人——那人好赌，输了钱。我的人盯了他两个来星期。喏，都在这儿了。」他把那页纸抛了过去。「比起他们死死戴着面具、顶着那些滑稽头衔的时候，可就没那么吓人了。」

艾萨克爵士饶有兴味地研究起这份名单。

「可这儿只有三个人，」他说，「第四个是谁？」
===Original===
“The fourth is the leader: can’t you guess who it is? Gresham, of course.”

“Gresham?”

“I haven’t any proof,” said Black; “it’s only surmise. But I would stake all I have in the world that I’m right. He is the very type of man to be in this﻿—to organize it, to arrange the details.”

“Are you sure the fourth is Gresham?” asked Sir Isaac again.

“Pretty sure,” said Black.

He had finished his dressing and was brushing his dress-coat carefully with a whisk brush.
===Chinese===
「第四个是首领：你猜不到是谁吗？当然是格雷沙姆。」

「格雷沙姆？」

「我没有实证，」布莱克说，「只是推测。但我敢拿我在这个世界上的一切打赌，我猜得没错。干这种事——出面组织、安排细节——他正是那号人。」

「你肯定第四个是格雷沙姆？」艾萨克爵士又问了一遍。

「相当肯定。」布莱克说。

他已穿戴停当，正用衣刷细细刷着礼服上衣。
===Original===
“Where are you going?” asked Sir Isaac.

“I have a little business tonight,” replied the other. “I don’t think it would interest you very much.”

He stopped his brushing. For a moment he seemed deep in thought.

“On consideration,” he said slowly, “perhaps it will interest you. Come along to the office with me. Have you dined?”

“No, not yet.”
===Chinese===
「你要去哪儿？」艾萨克爵士问。

「今晚我有点小事。」对方答道，「我想你不大会感兴趣。」

他停下手里的刷子。一时似乎陷入了沉思。

「再想了想，」他慢吞吞地说，「也许你会感兴趣。跟我一起到办公室去。你用过晚餐了吗？」

「没有，还没吃。」
===Original===
“I’m sorry I can’t dine you,” said Black. “I have an important engagement after this which is taking all my attention at present. You’re not dressed,” he continued. “That’s good. We’re going to a place where people do not as a rule dress for dinner.”

Over his own evening suit he drew a long overcoat, which he buttoned to the neck. He selected a soft felt hat from the wardrobe in the room and put it on before the looking-glass.

“Now, come along,” he said.
===Chinese===
「抱歉不能请你吃饭。」布莱克说，「过后我有个要紧的约会，眼下它占去了我全部心思。你没穿礼服，」他接着说，「那倒好。我们要去的地方，人们照例是不穿礼服吃晚饭的。」

他在晚礼服外面罩了一件长外套，一直扣到领口。他从房内的衣柜里挑出一顶软毡帽，对着镜子戴上。

「好了，走吧。」他说。
===Original===
It was dusk, and the wind which howled through the deserted street justified the wrapping he had provided. He did not immediately call a cab, but walked until they came to Vauxhall Bridge Road. By this time Sir Isaac’s patience and powers of pedestrianism were almost exhausted.

“Oh, Lord!” he said irritably, “this is not the kind of job I like particularly.”

“Have a little patience,” said Black. “You don’t expect me to call cabs in Chelsea and give my directions for half a dozen people to hear. You don’t seem to realize, Ikey, that you and I are being very closely watched.”

“Well, they could be watching us now,” said Sir Isaac with truth.

“They may be, but the chances are that nobody will be near enough when we give directions to the driver as to our exact destination.”

Even Sir Isaac did not catch it, so low was the voice of Black instructing the driver.
===Chinese===
时已黄昏，呼啸着扫过空寂街道的寒风，证明他那身裹严的装束并非多余。他没有马上叫车，而是一路步行，直到他们来到沃克斯豪尔桥路。这时艾萨克爵士的耐心和脚力都快耗尽了。

「哦，老天！」他不耐烦地说，「这种差事我可真不怎么喜欢。」

「耐心点儿。」布莱克说，「你总不能指望我在切尔西当街叫车，把去处讲给半打人听吧。艾基，你好像还没意识到，你我眼下正被人紧紧盯着呢。」

「哼，他们这会儿说不定就在盯着我们。」艾萨克爵士说得倒也在理。

「也许罢，可等我们把确切的目的地交代给车夫时，附近多半不会有人近得听得见。」

连艾萨克爵士也没听清——布莱克向车夫交代时，声音压得那么低。
===Original===
Through the little pane at the back of the cab Black scrutinized the vehicles following their route.

“I don’t think there is anybody after us at present,” he said. “It isn’t a very important matter, but if the information came to the Four that their plans were being checkmated it might make it rather awkward for us.”

The cab passed down the winding road which leads from the Oval to Kennington Green. It threaded a way through the traffic and struck the Camberwell Road. Halfway down, Black put out his head, and the cab turned sharply to the left. Then he tapped at the window and it stopped.

He got out, followed by Sir Isaac.

“Just wait for me at the end of the street,” he said to the driver.

He handed the man some money as a guarantee of his bona fides, and the two moved off. The street was one of very poor artisan houses, and Black had recourse to an electric lamp which he carried in his pocket to discover the number he wanted. At last he came to a small house with a tiny patch of garden in front and knocked.
===Chinese===
布莱克透过马车后部的小玻璃窗，仔细察看沿同一条路跟来的车辆。

「我看眼下没有人跟着我们。」他说，「这也不是什么了不得的大事，可万一四义士得到风声，知道他们的计划正在被人将死，那我们可就相当被动了。」

马车顺着从奥瓦尔到肯宁顿绿地那条蜿蜒的路驶去。它在车流中穿行，转上坎伯韦尔路。走到半路，布莱克探出头来，马车猛地向左一拐。接着他敲了敲车窗，车便停了。

他下了车，艾萨克爵士跟着下来。

「就在街尾等我。」他对车夫说。

他塞给那人几张钞票，作为自己诚意的担保，两人便走开了。这条街上全是穷苦工匠的住屋，布莱克只得掏出随身带的电筒，照着寻找他要的门牌。终于他来到一栋小房子前，房前有巴掌大的一块园地，他敲了敲门。
===Original===
A little girl opened the door.

“Is Mr. Farmer in?” said Black.

“Yes, sir,” said the little girl, “will you go up?”

She led the way up the carpeted stairs and knocked at a small door on the left. A voice bade them come in. The two men entered. Seated by the table in a poorly-furnished room, lit only by the fire, was a man. He rose as they entered.
===Chinese===
一个小姑娘开了门。

「法默先生在家吗？」布莱克问。

「在，先生。」小姑娘说，「您请上楼好吗？」

她在前头引路，上着铺了地毯的楼梯，敲了敲左边一扇小门。一个声音请他们进去。两人走进屋。一间陈设寒酸的房间里只有炉火照明，桌边坐着一个人。他们进屋时，那人站起身来。
===Original===
“I must explain,” said Black, “that Mr. Farmer has rented this room for a couple of weeks. He only comes here occasionally to meet his friends. This,” he went on, motioning to Sir Isaac, “is a great friend of mine.”

He closed the door, and waited till the little girl’s footsteps on the stairs had died away.

“The advantage of meeting in this kind of house,” said the man called Farmer, “is that the slightest movement shakes the edifice from roof to basement.”
===Chinese===
「我必须说明一下，」布莱克说，「法默先生租下这间屋子已有两个星期。他只是偶尔来这儿会会朋友。这位，」他朝艾萨克爵士摆了摆手，接着说，「是我的一位至交。」

他关上门，等着小姑娘的脚步声在楼梯上消失。

「在这种房子里会面的好处，」那个被称作法默的人说，「就是稍有动静，整栋楼从屋顶到地窖都会晃悠。」
===Original===
He spoke with what might be described as a “mock-culture” voice. It was the voice of a common man who had been much in the company of gentlemen, and who endeavoured to imitate their intonation without attempting to acquire their vocabulary.

“You can speak freely, Mr. Farmer,” said Black. “This gentleman is in my confidence. We are both interested in this ridiculous organization. I understand you have now left Mr. Wilkinson Despard’s employment?”

The man nodded.

“Yes, sir,” he said, with a little embarrassed cough. “I left him yesterday.”
===Chinese===
他说话带着一种不妨称为「冒充斯文」的腔调。那是一个粗人久与绅士们厮混、一味模仿人家声调却无意学到人家辞令的声音。

「你尽管放心说，法默先生。」布莱克说，「这位先生是我的心腹。我们两个都对这个可笑的组织感兴趣。据我所知，你已经辞了威尔金森·德斯帕德先生的差事？」

那人点了点头。

「是的，先生。」他说，尴尬地轻咳了一声。「我昨天离开他的。」
===Original===
“Now, have you found out who the fourth is?”

The man hesitated.

“I am not sure, sir. It is only fair to tell you that I am not absolutely certain. But I think you could gamble on the fact that the fourth gentleman is Mr. Horace Gresham.”
===Chinese===
「那么，你查清第四个人是谁了吗？」

那人犹豫起来。

「我没有把握，先生。先向您说明，我并非完全确定，这样才公道。不过我想，第四位先生是霍勒斯·格雷沙姆先生，这一点您尽可以放心去赌。」
===Original===
“You didn’t say that,” said Black, “until I suggested the name myself.”

The man did not flinch at the suspicion involved in the comment. His voice was even as he replied:

“That I admit, sir. But the other three gentlemen I knew. I had nothing to do with the fourth. He used to come to Mr. Despard’s late at night, and I admitted him. I never saw his face and never heard his voice. He went straight to Mr. Despard’s study, and if you knew how the house was portioned out you would realize that it was next to impossible to hear anything!”

“How did you come to know that these men were the Four?” asked Black.

“Well, sir,” said the other, obviously ill at ease, “by the way servants generally find things out﻿—I listened.”

“And yet you never found out who the leader was?”
===Chinese===
「这话你可没说过，」布莱克说，「直到我自己说出这个名字，你才附和上来。」

那人对这话里的疑心并未退缩。他答话时声调平稳：

「这我承认，先生。可另外三位先生我是认识的。我同第四位没有打过交道。他向来深更半夜到德斯帕德先生家来，是我放他进门的。我从没见过他的脸，也没听见过他的声音。他径直就进德斯帕德先生的书房，您若是知道那房子的格局，就会明白想偷听到点什么简直难上加难！」

「那你是怎么知道这几个人就是四义士的？」布莱克问。

「这个嘛，先生，」对方说，显然很不自在，「用的就是仆人们通用的法子——我偷听来着。」

「可你始终没打听出首领是谁？」
===Original===
“No, sir.”

“Have you discovered anything else of which I am not aware?”

“Yes, sir,” said the man eagerly. “I discovered before I left Mr. Despard’s employ that they’ve got you set. That’s an old army term which means that they’ve marked you down for punishment.”

“Oh, they have, have they?” said Black.

“I overheard that last night. You see, the meeting generally consisted of four. The fourth very seldom turned up unless there was something to do. But he was always the leading spirit. It was he who found the money when money was necessary. It was he who directed the Four to their various occupations. And it was he who invariably chose the people who had to be punished. He has chosen you, I know, sir. They had a meeting, the night before last. They were discussing various people, and I heard your name.”
===Chinese===
「没有，先生。」

「你还发现过什么我不知道的事吗？」

「有的，先生。」那人急切地说。「我离开德斯帕德先生家之前就发现，他们已经把您给盯上了。那是句军队里的老话，意思是他们把您记下，要加以惩办了。」

「哦，是吗，他们盯上我了？」布莱克说。

「那是我昨晚偷听来的。您瞧，与会的一般是四个人。第四位很少露面，除非有事要办。可他始终是主心骨。需要钱的时候，筹钱的总是他。指派四义士各行其是的，也是他。还有，该惩罚哪些人，向来也是由他选定。他选中了您，这我知道，先生。前天夜里他们开了一个会，正议论各色人等，我听见了您的名字。」
===Original===
“How could you hear?”

“I was in the next room, sir. There’s a dressing-room leading out of Mr. Despard’s room, where these conferences were held. I had a duplicate key.”

Black rose as if to go.

“It almost seems a pity you have left that Johnnie. Did they ever speak about me?” asked Sir Isaac, who had been an attentive listener.

“I don’t know your name, sir,” said the servant deferentially.
===Chinese===
「你怎么能听见？」

「我在隔壁房间，先生。德斯帕德先生那间屋——也就是开会的地方——连着一间梳妆室，我手里有一把配的钥匙。」

布莱克站起身，像是要走了。

「你离开那位爷，几乎可惜了。他们提起过我吗？」一直听得仔仔细细的艾萨克爵士问道。

「我不知道您的名字，先生。」仆人必恭必敬地说。
===Original===
“No, and you jolly well won’t,” answered the baronet promptly.

“I hope, gentlemen,” said the man, “that now I have lost my employment you’ll do whatever you can to find me another place. If either of you gentlemen want a reliable manservant﻿—”

He looked inquiringly at Sir Isaac, as being the more likely of the two.

“Not me,” said the other brutally. “I find all my work cut out to keep my own secrets, without having any dam’ eavesdropping man on the premises to spy on me.”

The man against whom this was directed did not seem particularly hurt by the bluntness of the other. He merely bowed his head and made no reply.

Black took a flat case from his inside pocket, opened it and extracted two notes.
===Chinese===
「不知道，往后也最好别知道。」从男爵立刻答道。

「我希望，两位先生，」那人说，「如今我丢了差事，你们能不能想想法子，给我另寻一个位置。两位先生中若有哪位要一名可靠的男仆——」

他探询地望着艾萨克爵士，认定两人中他更可能有此需要。

「别找我。」对方毫不客气地说。「守住我自己的秘密已经够我忙的了，可不想宅子里再添一个该死的偷听家伙来监视我。」

这话所指的人似乎并不因对方的直率而怎么见怪。他只是低下头，没有作声。

布莱克从内袋里掏出一只扁平的皮夹，打开来抽出两张钞票。
===Original===
“Here are twenty pounds,” he said, “which makes £220 you have had from me. Now, if you can find out anything else worth knowing I don’t mind making it up to £300﻿—but it has got to be something good. Keep in with the servants. You know the rest of them. Is there any reason why you shouldn’t go back to the flat?”

“No, sir,” said the man. “I was merely discharged for carelessness.”

“Very good,” said Black. “You know my address and where to find me. If anything turns up let me know.”

“Yes, sir.”

“By the way,” said Black, as he made a move to go, “do the Four contemplate taking any action in the immediate future?”

“No, sir,” said the man eagerly. “I am particularly sure of that. I heard them discussing the advisability of parting. One gentleman wanted to go to the Continent for a month, and another wanted to go to America to see about his mining property. By the way, they all agreed there was no necessity to meet for a month. I gathered that for the time being they were doing nothing.”
===Chinese===
「这里是二十英镑。」他说，「这样你从我手里一共拿了 220 英镑。你要是再打听出什么值得一听的事，我不介意加到 300 英镑——不过得是真有价值的东西。跟仆人们搞好关系，其余的人你都熟。回那套公寓去，可有什么不方便？」

「没有，先生。」那人说，「我不过是因为做事不细心被辞退的。」

「很好。」布莱克说，「你知道我的地址，也知道上哪儿找我。有什么动静就告诉我。」

「是，先生。」

「顺便问一句，」布莱克说着起身要走，「四义士打算在近期有什么举动吗？」

「没有，先生。」那人急切地说，「这一点我特别有把握。我听见他们商量过散伙是否妥当。一位先生想到欧洲大陆去一个月，另一位要去美国照看他的矿业产业。对了，他们一致同意一个月内没有会面的必要。照我的理解，他们眼下是什么事也不干。」
===Original===
“Excellent!” said Black.

He shook hands with the servant and departed.

“Pretty beastly sort of man to have about the house,” said Sir Isaac as they walked back to the cab.

“Yes,” said Black, good-humouredly, “but it isn’t my house, and I feel no scruples in the matter. I do not,” he added virtuously, “approve of tapping servants for information about their masters and mistresses, but there are occasions when this line of conduct is perfectly justified.”
===Chinese===
「好极了！」布莱克说。

他同那仆人握了握手，走了。

「家里养这么一号人，可真够呛。」走回马车去的路上，艾萨克爵士说。

「是啊，」布莱克和颜悦色地说，「可那不是我的宅子，这事我良心上毫无不安。」他又一脸正气地补充道，「我并不赞成收买仆人、探听他们男女主人的事，但有些时候，这种行事路数是完全正当的。」

## XIV Willie Jakobs Tells / 十四 威利·雅各布斯开口
===Original===
Left alone, the man whom they had called Farmer waited a few minutes. Then he took down his coat, which hung behind the door, put on his hat and gloves deliberately and thoughtfully, and left the house.

He walked in the direction which Black and Sir Isaac had taken, but their taxicab was flying northward long before he reached the spot where it had waited.

He pursued his way into the Camberwell Road and boarded a tramcar. The street lamps and the lights in the shop windows revealed him to be a good-looking man, a little above the average height, with a pale refined face. He was dressed quietly, but well.

He alighted near the Elephant and Castle and strode rapidly along the New Kent Road, turning into one of the poorer streets which lead to a labyrinth of smaller and more poverty-stricken thoroughfares in that district which is bounded on the west by East Street and on the east by the New Kent Road. A little way along, some of the old houses had been pulled down and new buildings in yellow brick had been erected. A big red lamp outside a broad entrance notified the neighbourhood that this was the free dispensary, though none who lived within a radius of five miles needed any information as to the existence of this institution.
===Chinese===
屋里只剩那个被他们称作法默的人，他等了几分钟。然后他取下挂在门后的外套，从容而若有所思地戴上帽子和手套，离开了这栋房子。

他朝布莱克和艾萨克爵士去的方向走去，可等他走到车先前等候的地方，那辆出租汽车早已向北飞驰而去了。

他一路走到坎伯韦尔路，上了电车。街灯和店铺橱窗的灯光映出他的模样：一个相貌堂堂的男人，比中等身材略高，脸色苍白而清秀。衣着素净，却很讲究。

他在象堡附近下车，快步走上新肯特路，拐进一条较穷的街——这一带往里去，便是一大片更窄更穷的巷陌迷宫，西起东街，东到新肯特路。走进去不多远，几栋老屋已被拆掉，立起了黄砖的新楼。宽敞的大门上挂着一盏红色大灯，告诉街坊这是免费施药所——不过方圆五英里之内，谁也不需要人告知这机构的存在。
===Original===
In the hallway was a board containing the names of three doctors, and against them a little sliding panel, which enabled them to inform their visitors whether they were in or out. He paused before the board.

The little indicator against the first name said “Out.”

Farmer put up his hand and slid the panel along to show the word “In.” Then he passed through the door, through the large waiting-room into a small room, which bore the name “Dr. Wilson Graille.”

He closed the door behind him and slipped a catch. He took off his hat and coat and hung them up. Then he touched a bell, and a servant appeared.
===Chinese===
门厅里挂着一块牌子，写着三位医生的姓名，每个名字旁配一个小滑牌，好让来客知道人在不在。他在牌子前停了停。

第一个名字旁边的小滑牌标着「出诊」。

法默伸手把滑牌拨过去，露出「在」字。随后他穿过那道门，穿过大候诊室，走进一间小屋，门上写着「威尔逊·格雷尔博士」。

他关上身后的门，扣上搭扣。他脱下帽子和外套挂好，然后按了按铃，一个仆人应声而来。
===Original===
“Is Dr. O’Hara in?” he asked.

“Yes, doctor,” replied the man.

“Ask him to come along to me, will you, please?”

In a few minutes a man of middle height, but powerfully built, came in and closed the door behind him.

“Well, how did you get on?” he inquired, and, uninvited, drew up a chair to the table.

“They jumped at the bait,” said Gonsalez with a little laugh. “I think they have got something on. They were most anxious to know whether we were moving at all. You had better notify Manfred. We’ll have a meeting tonight. What about Despard? Do you think he would object to having his name used?”
===Chinese===
「奥哈拉博士在吗？」

「在，大夫。」那人答道。

「请他到我这边来一趟，劳驾。」

几分钟后，一个中等身材却十分壮实的男人走了进来，随手关上门。

「怎么样，事情办得顺吗？」他问道，也不等人让，自己拖了把椅子到桌前。

「他们一口吞了饵。」冈萨雷斯轻笑道，「我看他们心里有鬼。他们急得不得了，想知道我们到底有没有动静。你最好知会曼弗雷德一声。今晚我们开个会。德斯帕德呢？你看他会介意借他的名字一用吗？」
===Original===
His voice lacked the mock culture which had so deceived Black.

“Not a bit. I chose him purposely because I knew he was going abroad tonight.”

“And the others?”

“With the exception of the art man, they are nonexistent.”

“Suppose he investigates?”

“Not he. He will be satisfied to take the most prominent of the four﻿—Despard, and the other chap whose name I have forgotten. Despard leaves tonight, and the other on Wednesday for America. You see, that fits in with what I told Black.”
===Chinese===
他的嗓音里没有了那副骗过布莱克的假斯文。

「一点也不。我是特意挑的他，因为我知道他今夜要出国。」

「其余几个呢？」

「除了那位艺术鉴赏家，其余都是子虚乌有。」

「万一他去查证呢？」

「他才不会。他自会把四个人里最扎眼的当真——德斯帕德，还有另一个我忘了名字的家伙。德斯帕德今夜动身，另一个星期三去美国。你瞧，这同我告诉布莱克的对得上号。」
===Original===
He took from his pocket the two ten-pound notes and laid them on the table.

“Twenty pounds,” he said, and handed them to the other man. “You ought to be able to do something with that.”

The other stuffed them into his waistcoat pocket.

“I shall send those two Brady children to the seaside,” he said. “It probably won’t save their lives, but it will give the little devils some conception of what joy life holds﻿—for a month or so.”

The same thought seemed to occur to both, and they laughed.

“Black would not like to know to what base use his good money is being put,” said Graille, or Farmer, or Gonsalez﻿—call him what you will﻿—with a twinkle in his blue eyes.
===Chinese===
他从口袋里掏出那两张十英镑的钞票，摆在桌上。

「二十英镑。」他说着递给对方，「有这些，你总能办点事了。」

对方把钱塞进马甲口袋。

「我要把布雷迪家那两个孩子送去海边。」他说，「这多半救不了他们的命，可好歹能让两个小鬼尝尝生活里还有多少乐子——个把月吧。」

两人似乎同时想起了什么，一齐笑出声来。

「布莱克若是知道他这笔干净钱派了多么下作的用场，怕是不会高兴。」格雷尔也好，法默也好，冈萨雷斯也罢——随你怎么叫——说着，蓝眼睛里闪过一丝狡黠。
===Original===
“Were they anxious to know who was the fourth man?” asked Poiccart.

“Most keen on it,” he said. “But I wondered if they would have believed me if I had confessed myself to be one of the four, and had I at the same time confessed that I was as much in the dark as to the identity of the fourth as they themselves.”

Poiccart rose and stood irresolutely, with his hands stuffed into his trousers pockets, looking into the fire.

“I often wonder,” he said, “who it is. Don’t you?”

“I’ve got over those sensations of curiosity,” said Gonsalez. “Whoever he is, I am of course satisfied that he is a large-hearted man, working with a singleness of purpose.”

The other nodded in agreement.
===Chinese===
「他们急着知道第四个人是谁吗？」波伊卡特问。

「急得很。」他说，「不过我当时就想，倘若我自认是四义士之一，同时又坦白说，对第四个人的身份，我同他们一样蒙在鼓里，他们肯信吗？」

波伊卡特站起身，双手插在裤袋里，望着炉火出神。

「我常常纳闷，」他说，「那个人到底是谁。你不纳闷？」

「这种好奇心我早熬过去了。」冈萨雷斯说，「不管他是谁，我当然信得过：他是个胸怀博大的人，做事一心一意。」

对方点头赞同。
===Original===
“I am sure,” said Graille enthusiastically, “that he has done great work, justifiable work, and honourable work.”

Poiccart nodded gravely.

“By the way,” said the other, “I went to old Lord Verlond﻿—you remember, No. 4 suggested our trying him. He’s a pretty bitter sort of person with a sharp tongue.”

Poiccart smiled.

“What did he do? Tell you to go to the devil?”

“Something of the sort,” said Dr. Gonsalez. “I only got a grudging half-guinea from him, and he regaled me all the time with more than half a guinea’s worth of amusement.”
===Chinese===
「我确信，」格雷尔热忱地说，「他干的是大事业，正当的事业，光荣的事业。」

波伊卡特郑重地点头。

「对了，」对方说，「我去见过老弗隆德勋爵——你记得的，是四号提议咱们去试探他。那是个相当刻薄的人物，嘴上不饶人。」

波伊卡特笑了。

「他怎么样？叫你见鬼去了？」

「差不多吧。」冈萨雷斯博士说，「我好不容易才从他那儿抠出半个几尼，他却一路拿足几个几尼的逗乐回敬我。」
===Original===
“But it wasn’t for this work,” said the other.

Gonsalez shook his head.

“No, for another department,” he said with a smile.

They had little more time for conversation. Patients began to come in, and within a quarter of an hour the two men were as busy as men could be attending to the injuries, the diseases and the complaints of the people of this overcrowded neighbourhood.

This great dispensary owed its erection and its continuance to the munificence of three doctors who appeared from nowhere. Who the man was who had contributed £5,000 to the upkeep, and who had afterwards appeared in person, masked and cloaked, and had propounded to three earnest workers for humanity his desire to be included in the organization, nobody knew, unless it was Manfred. It was Manfred the wise who accepted not only the offer, but the bona fides of the stranger﻿—Manfred who accepted him as a copartner.

Casual observers described the three earnest medicos not only as cranks, but fanatics. They were attached to no organization; they gave no sign to the world that they could be in any way associated with any of the religious organizations engaged in medical work. It is an indisputable fact that they possessed the qualifications to practise, and that one﻿—Leon Gonsalez﻿—was in addition a brilliant chemist.
===Chinese===
「可那不是为了这摊事。」对方说。

冈萨雷斯摇头。

「不是，是另一摊。」他微笑道。

他们没工夫多聊了。病人开始上门，一刻钟不到，两人便忙得不可开交，照料起这片人烟稠密街区的种种外伤、疾病和病痛。

这座大施药所，全靠三位不知从何处冒出来的医生慷慨解囊，才得以建立并维持。那位捐出 5,000 英镑充作经费、后来又亲自登门、蒙面披篷、向三位恳切的人道主义者提出希望加入这个组织的人究竟是谁，没人知道——除非曼弗雷德例外。正是睿智的曼弗雷德，不仅接受了这位陌生人的请求，也信纳了他的诚意——是曼弗雷德接纳他做了合伙人。

闲看热闹的人说这三位恳切的医生不光是怪人，还是狂热分子。他们不隶属任何团体；也没有向外界露出半点迹象，表明他们同任何从事医疗事业的宗教团体沾边。无可争辩的事实是：他们行医资格俱全，其中一位——莱昂·冈萨雷斯——还是位出色的化学家。
===Original===
No man ever remembered their going to church, or urging attendance at any place of worship. The religious bodies that laboured in the neighbourhood were themselves astonished.

One by one they had nibbled at the sectarian question. Some had asked directly to what religious organization these men were attached. No answer was offered satisfactory to the inquirers.

It was nearly eleven o’clock that night when the work of the two dispensers had finished. The last patient had been dismissed, the last fretful whimper of an ailing child had died away; the door had been locked, the sweepers were engaged in cleaning up the big waiting-room.

The two men sat in the office﻿—tired, but cheerful. The room was well furnished; it was the common room of the three. A bright fire burnt in the fireplace, big roomy armchairs and settees were in evidence. The floor was carpeted thickly, and two or three rare prints hung on the distempered walls.

They were sitting discussing the events of the evening﻿—comparing notes, retailing particulars of interest in cases which had come under their notice. Manfred had gone out earlier in the evening and had not returned.
===Chinese===
谁也不记得他们上过教堂，或是劝人去过哪处礼拜场所。在这一带奔走的各宗教团体，自己都摸不着头脑。

他们一个接一个地试探过宗派问题。有的直截了当问过，这几个人究竟归属哪个宗教团体。没有一个回答能让问的人满意。

那天夜里快十一点，两位施药医师才忙完手头的活。最后一个病人已经打发走，病孩最后一声烦躁的啼哭也已消歇；大门上了锁，清扫工正在打扫大候诊室。

两人坐在办公室里——疲惫，却快活。屋子陈设讲究，是三人共用的起居室。壁炉里炉火明亮，宽大的扶手椅和长靠椅一望即见。地板铺着厚厚的地毯，粉墙挂着两三幅珍版版画。

两人坐着谈论今晚的种种——交换见闻，细数各自经手病例里有趣的细节。曼弗雷德傍晚先出去了，还没回来。
===Original===
Then a bell rang shrilly.

Leon looked up at the indicator.

“That is the dispensary door,” he said in Spanish. “I suppose we’d better see who it is.”

“It will be a small girl,” said Poiccart. “ ‘Please will you come to father; he’s either dead or drunk.’ ”

There was a little laugh at this reminiscence of an incident which had actually happened.

Poiccart opened the door. A man stood in the entrance. “There’s a bad accident just round the corner,” he said. “Can I bring him in here, doctor?”
===Chinese===
忽然铃声响得刺耳。

莱昂抬头看了看指示牌。

「是施药所的门。」他用西班牙语说，「咱们最好去看看是谁。」

「准是个小姑娘，」波伊卡特说，「『求您去看看爸爸，他不是死了就是喝醉了。』」

想起这件确曾发生过的事，两人轻轻一笑。

波伊卡特开了门。门口站着一个人。「就在拐角上出了桩严重的事故。」他说，「大夫，能把他抬进来吗？」
===Original===
“What sort of an accident?” said Poiccart.

“A man has been knifed.”

“Bring him in,” said Poiccart.

He went quickly to the common room.

“It’s a stabbing case,” he said. “Will you have him in your surgery, Leon?”

The young man rose swiftly.
===Chinese===
「什么样的事故？」波伊卡特说。

「有个人叫人捅了刀子。」

「抬进来。」波伊卡特说。

他快步走回起居室。

「是件刺伤案。」他说，「莱昂，把他安置到你的手术室行吗？」

年轻人霍地起身。
===Original===
“Yes,” he said; “I’ll get the table ready.”

In a few minutes half a dozen men bore in the unconscious form of the victim. It was a face familiar to the two.

They laid him tenderly upon the surgical table, and with deft hands ripped away the clothing from the wound, whilst the policeman who had accompanied the party pushed back the crowd from the surgery door.

The two men were alone with the unconscious man.

They exchanged glances.

“Unless I am mistaken” said carefully, “this is the late Mr. Willie Jakobs.”
===Chinese===
「行。」他说，「我去把手术台备好。」

几分钟后，六条汉子抬进来那不省人事的伤者。那是一张两人都熟的脸。

他们把他轻轻放上手术台，两双手麻利地撕开创口周围的衣裳，随行来的警察则在手术室门外拦住围观的人。

屋里只剩他们两个和那个昏迷的人。

两人交换了一个眼色。

「如果我没认错，」他斟酌着说，「这位就是已故的威利·雅各布斯先生。」

---
===Original===
That evening May Sandford sat alone in her room reading. Her father, when he had come in to say goodbye to May before going to a directors’ dinner, had left her ostensibly studying an improving book, but the volume now lay unheeded at her side.

That afternoon she had received an urgent note from Black, asking her to meet him “on a matter of the greatest importance.” It concerned her father, and it was very secret. She was alarmed, and not a little puzzled. The urgency and the secrecy of the note distressed her unaccountably.

For the twentieth time she began to read the improving plays of Monsieur Molière, when a knock at the door made her hastily conceal the paper.

“There is a man who wishes to see you,” said the girl who had entered in response to her “Come in.”
===Chinese===
当晚，梅·桑德福德独自坐在房里看书。父亲出门赴董事晚宴前来同梅道别时，她还在装模作样研读一本进德修业的书，此刻那书早已被丢在一边，无人理会。

那天下午她收到布莱克一封急信，请她前去见他，说是「为了一件最最重要的大事」。事情牵涉她父亲，而且万分机密。她又惊又疑，那封信的急切与诡秘，让她莫名地心神不宁。

她第二十次拿起莫里哀先生那些陶冶性情的剧作读起来，一阵敲门声让她慌忙把信藏起。

「有位先生想见您。」应着她那声「进来」进屋来的女仆说道。
===Original===
“What sort of man?”

“A common-looking man,” said the maid.

She hesitated. The butler was in the house, otherwise she would not have seen the visitor.

“Show him into father’s study,” she said. “Tell Thomas this man is here and ask him to be handy in case I ring for him.”

She had never seen the man whom she found waiting. Instinctively she distrusted his face, though there was something about him which compelled her sympathy.

He was white and haggard, black shadows encircled his eyes, and his hands, by no means clean, shook.
===Chinese===
「什么样的人？」

「一个看着挺寒碜的男人。」女仆说。

她犹豫了。管家在家，不然她本不会见这位访客。

「带他去父亲的书房。」她说，「告诉托马斯这人来了，让他别走远，说不定我要按铃叫他。」

在书房里等她的那个人，她从未见过。她本能地不信那张脸，可他身上又有什么东西逼得她生出怜意。

他面色惨白，形容枯槁，眼圈发黑，一双手算不上干净，还在发抖。
===Original===
“I am sorry to bother you, miss,” he said, “but this is important.”

“It is rather a late hour,” she said. “What is it you want?”

He fumbled with his hat and looked at the waiting girl. At a nod from May she left the room.

“This is rather important to you, miss,” said the man again. “Black treated me pretty badly.”

For a moment an unworthy suspicion flashed through her mind. Had Frank sent his man to her to shake her faith in Black? A feeling of resentment arose against her visitor and the man she thought was his employer.
===Chinese===
「对不起，打扰您了，小姐。」他说，「可这事要紧。」

「这钟点可实在不早了。」她说，「你有什么事？」

他摆弄着帽子，看了看侍立一旁的女仆。梅一点头，女仆便退了出去。

「这事对您相当要紧，小姐。」那人又说，「布莱克待我太狠了。」

一瞬间，一个不光彩的疑念掠过她心头。莫非是弗兰克派了他的人来，想动摇她对布莱克的信任？一股怨气油然而生，冲着这位访客，也冲着她以为是他雇主的那个人。
===Original===
“You may save your breath,” she said coolly, “and you can go back to the gentleman who sent you and tell him﻿—”

“Nobody sent me, miss,” he said eagerly. “I come on my own. I tell you they’ve done me a bad turn. I’ve kept my mouth shut for Black for years, and now he’s turned me down. I’m ill, miss, you can see that for yourself,” he said, throwing out his arms in despair. “I’ve been almost starving and they haven’t given me a bean. I went to Black’s house today and he wouldn’t see me.”

He almost whimpered in his helpless anger.

“He’s done me a bad turn and I’m going to do him one,” he said fiercely. “You know what his game is?”

“I do not want to know,” she said again, the old suspicion obscuring her vision. “You will gain nothing by speaking against Colonel Black.”

“Don’t be foolish, miss,” he pleaded, “don’t think I’ve come for money. I don’t expect money﻿—I don’t want it. I dare say I can get help from Mr. Fellowe.”
===Chinese===
「你不必白费口舌。」她冷冷地说，「尽管回去告诉派你来的那位先生——」

「没人派我来，小姐。」他急切地说，「我自己来的。我告诉你，他们坑了我。我替布莱克守了这么多年的口，如今他把我一脚踢开。我病了，小姐，您自己看得出来。」他绝望地摊开双臂。「我差点饿死，他们一个小钱也没给。今天我上布莱克家去，他连见都不见。」

他那无可奈何的怒火几乎化作了呜咽。

「他坑了我，我也要坑他一回。」他狠狠地说，「你知道他干的什么勾当吗？」

「我不想知道。」她又说了一遍，旧日的疑心蒙住了她的眼睛。「说布莱克上校的坏话，对你没有半点好处。」

「别犯傻，小姐。」他恳求道，「别以为我是来要钱的。我不指望钱——我也不要。我想，费洛先生那儿总能帮我一把。」
===Original===
“Ah!” she said, “so you know Mr. Fellowe: it was he who sent you. I will not hear another word,” she went on hotly. “I know now where you come from﻿—I’ve heard all this before.”

She walked determinedly across the room and rang the bell. The butler came in.

“Show this man out,” said May.

The man looked at her sorrowfully.

“You’ve had your chance, miss,” he said ominously. “Black’s Essley, that’s all!”
===Chinese===
「啊！」她说，「原来你认识费洛先生：是他派你来的。别的话我一个字也不要听了。」她怒冲冲地说下去，「现在我可知道你是从哪儿来的——这一套我早听过了。」

她毅然穿过房间，按响了铃。管家进来。

「送这位先生出去。」梅说。

那人悲戚地望着她。

「机会给您过了，小姐。」他不祥地说，「布莱克就是埃斯利，就这么一句！」
===Original===
With this parting shot he shuffled through the hall, down the steps into the night.

Left alone, the girl shrank into her chair. She was shaking from head to foot with indignation and bewilderment. It must have been Frank who sent this man. How mean, how inexpressibly mean!

“How dare he? How dare he?” she asked.

It was the policeman in Frank which made him so horrid, she thought. He always believed horrid things of everybody. It was only natural. He had lived his life amongst criminals; he had thought of nothing but breaches of the law. She looked at the clock: it was a quarter to ten. He had wasted her evening, this visitor. She did not know exactly what to do. She could not read; it was too early to go to bed. She would have liked to have gone for a little walk, but there was nobody to take her. It was absurd asking the butler to walk behind her; she smiled at the thought.

Then she started. She had heard the distant ring of the front-door bell. Who could it be?
===Chinese===
甩下这句临别狠话，他拖着脚步穿过门厅，走下台阶，没入夜色。

屋里只剩下姑娘，她缩进椅子里，气得又是愤慨又是惶惑，浑身发抖。一定是弗兰克派这人来的。多么下作，多么说不出口的下作！

「他怎么敢？他怎么敢？」她自问。

是弗兰克身上那股警察气，才让他这么讨人嫌，她想。他总把人人往坏处想。这也自然。他一辈子混在罪犯堆里，满脑子无非是作奸犯科。她看看钟：十点差一刻。这位访客白白糟蹋了她的晚上。她不知道该做什么好。书看不进；睡觉又太早。她本想出去散一小会儿步，可没人陪她。让管家跟在后头又太可笑；想到这儿她笑了。

她猛地一惊。她听见远处前门的铃声响了。会是谁呢？
===Original===
She had not long to wait in doubt. A few minutes afterwards the girl had announced Colonel Black. He was in evening dress and very cheerful.

“Forgive this visit,” he said, with that heartiness of voice which carried conviction of his sincerity. “I happened to be passing and I thought I’d drop in.”

This was not exactly true. Black had carefully planned this call. He knew her father was out; knew also, so bitter had been a discussion of that afternoon, that he would not have sanctioned the visit.

May gave him her hand, and he grasped it warmly.

She came straight to the point.

“I’m so glad you’ve come,” she said. “I’ve been awfully bothered.”
===Chinese===
她没疑惑多久。几分钟后，女仆进来通报布莱克上校到。他一身晚礼服，兴致很高。

「原谅我冒昧来访。」他说，那热络的嗓音自有一股叫人信服的真诚。「我恰好路过，心想就进来坐坐。」

这话不太真。这次拜访是布莱克精心筹划的。他知道她父亲外出；也知道那天下午那场话不投机的争论之后，她父亲绝不会准他登门。

梅把手伸给他，他热切地一握。

她开门见山。

「你来了我真高兴。」她说，「我心里烦得不得了。」
===Original===
He nodded sympathetically, though a little at sea.

“And now this man has come?”

“This man﻿—which man?” he asked sharply.

“I forget his name﻿—he came this evening. In fact, he’s only been gone a little time. And he looked awfully ill. You know him, I think?”

“Not Jakobs?” he breathed.

She nodded.
===Chinese===
他同情地点头，心里却有点摸不着头脑。

「现在，那个人来过了？」

「那个人——哪个人？」他急忙问。

「名字我忘了——他今晚来过。其实走了还没多大工夫。他看上去病得不轻。你认识他的，我想？」

「不会是雅各布斯吧？」他屏着气问。

她点头。
===Original===
“I think that is the name,” she said.

“Jakobs?” he repeated, and his face went a little white. “What did he say?” he asked quickly.

She repeated the conversation as nearly as she could remember it. When she had finished he rose.

“You’re not going?” she said in astonishment.

“I’m afraid I must,” he said. “I’ve a rather important engagement and﻿—er﻿—I only called in passing. Which way did this man go? Did he give you any idea as to his destination?”

She shook her head.
===Chinese===
「我记得是这个名字。」她说。

「雅各布斯？」他又念了一遍，脸色白了一分。「他说了什么？」他赶紧问。

她凭记忆把那段对话尽量原样复述了一遍。她刚说完，他便站起身。

「你要走了？」她讶然道。

「恐怕必须走了。」他说，「我还有个相当要紧的约会——呃——我本是路过顺便进来的。这人往哪边走了？他说没说过要去哪儿？」

她摇头。
===Original===
“No. All that he said was that there were people who would be glad of the information he could give about you.”

“He did, did he?” said Black, with an heroic attempt at a smile. “I never thought Jakobs was that kind of man. Of course, there is nothing that I should mind everybody knowing, but one has business secrets, you know. Miss Sandford. He is a discharged employee of mine who has stolen some contracts. You need not worry about the matter.”

He smiled confidently at her as he left the room.

He drove straight from the house to his city office. The place was in darkness, but he knew his way without the necessity of lighting up. He ran upstairs into the boardroom.

There was a little door in one corner of the room, concealed from view by a hanging curtain.
===Chinese===
「没有。他只说，有人会巴不得听到他能讲出的关于你的事。」

「他是这么说的？」布莱克说，硬撑出一个笑，「我万没想到雅各布斯是那种人。当然，我的事没有什么见不得人的，不过人嘛，总有点生意上的秘密，桑德福德小姐。他是我辞退的一名雇员，偷了几份合同。这事您不必挂心。」

出房时，他冲她自信地一笑。

他从宅子直接驱车去城里的办公室。那里一片漆黑，但他熟门熟路，无须开灯。他跑上楼进了董事会会议室。

屋子一角有扇小门，一道悬垂的帘子遮着，不引人注意。
===Original===
He closed the shutters and pulled down the blinds before he switched on the light. He pushed the curtain aside and examined the face of the door. There was no sign that it had been forced. Jakobs knew of the existence of this little retiring-room, and had, in his indiscretion, mentioned its existence in one of his letters of demand.

Black drew from his pocket a small bunch of keys attached to a silver chain. The door of the room opened easily. There was a smaller room disclosed﻿—no larger than a big cupboard. A single incandescent electric burner slung from the ceiling supplied all the light necessary. There was a dressing-table, a chair, a big looking-glass, and a number of hooks from which were suspended a dozen articles of attire. Air was admitted through two ventilators let into the wall and communicating with the main ventilating shaft of the building.

He opened the door of the dressing-table and drew out a number of wigs. They were wigs such as only Fasieur can supply﻿—perfectly modelled and all of one shade of hair, though differently arranged.

He tossed them on to the table impatiently, groping for something which he knew should be there, and was there unless a thief skilled in the use of skeleton keys, and having, moreover, some knowledge of the office, had taken it. He stopped his search suddenly and examined a pad of paper which lay on the table.

It was a pad which he kept handy for note-taking﻿—to jot down memoranda. On the white face of the paper was a large brown thumbmark, and though Colonel Black knew little of the science of anthropology, he was sufficiently well acquainted with the sign to know that it was the mark of a thumb which ought never to have been in this secret office of his.

Then it was Willie! Willie Jakobs, the befriended, the pensioned, and the scorned, who had removed a certain green bottle, the duplicate of which was in his pocket at that moment.
===Chinese===
他先关好窗板、放下百叶窗，才开灯。他掀开帘子，察看门面。没有一丝撬动的痕迹。雅各布斯知道这间小密室的存在，还曾在某封催索信里口无遮拦地提到过它。

布莱克从口袋里掏出一小串拴在银链上的钥匙。房门应手而开。里面是间更小的屋子——不比一个大橱柜大多少。天花板吊下一盏白炽电灯，照明尽够。屋里有张梳妆台、一把椅子、一面大穿衣镜，还有一排挂钩，悬着十来件行头。空气靠墙上两个通着大楼主通风道的气窗进来。

他打开梳妆台的柜门，取出几副假发。那是只有法西耶才供得出的货色——做工完美无缺，发色清一色相同，只是式样各异。

他不耐烦地把假发丢在桌上，摸索一样他知道本该在那儿的东西——只要没有哪个精通万能钥匙、又对这办公室略知门径的贼把它拿走，它就该在。他忽然停住搜索，端详起桌上一本便笺簿来。

那本簿子是他随手记事用的——草草记些备忘。雪白的纸面上印着一枚褐色的大拇指印。布莱克上校虽然对人类学所知寥寥，这记号他是认得的：那是一枚绝不该出现在他这间密室里的拇指印下的痕。

这么说，是威利！是那个受过关照、领着津贴、又遭人白眼的威利·雅各布斯，取走了那只绿色小瓶——此刻那只复制品就在他口袋里。
===Original===
Black did not lose his nerve. He went to a drawer in the desk of his outer office and took out a Browning pistol. It was loaded. He balanced it in his right hand, looked at it reflectively, then put it back again. He hated firearms; they made a great deal of unnecessary noise, and they left behind them too sure an indication of the identity of their user. Men have been traced by bullets.

There were other ways. He lifted from the drawer a long thin knife. It was an Italian stiletto of the sixteenth century﻿—the sort of toy a man might use in these prosaic days for opening his letters. And indeed this was the ostensible reason why Black kept the weapon at hand.

He drew it from its ornate leather sheath and tested its temper, felt its edge and gingerly fingered its point; then he put the stiletto in its case in his overcoat pocket, switched out the light and went out. This was not a case which demanded the employment of the little bottle. There was too little of the precious stuff left, and he had need of it for other purposes.

There were two or three places where he might find the man. A little public-house off Regent Street was one. He drove there, stopping the cab a few paces from the spot. He strode into the bar, where men of Jakobs’ kind were to be found, but it was empty. The man he sought was not there.

He made a tour of other likely places with no better success. Willie would be at home. He had moved to lodgings on the south side of the Thames.
===Chinese===
布莱克没有乱了方寸。他走到外间办公室书桌的抽屉前，取出一支勃朗宁手枪。枪是上了膛的。他在右手里掂了掂，若有所思地看了看，又放了回去。他讨厌火器：动静闹得太大，又给用枪人的身份留下太确凿的凭据。有人就是叫子弹给追查出来的。

还有别的法子。他从抽屉里提起一把细长的刀。那是一柄十六世纪的意大利短剑——在这个乏味的年代，这种玩意的用处大抵是裁信封。的确，布莱克手边备着这件家伙，对外说的正是这个缘故。

他把它从华丽的皮鞘里抽出来，试了试钢火，摸了摸刃口，又小心地用指头碰了碰锋尖；然后连鞘把短剑放进外套口袋，关灯出去。这桩案子还用不着动那只小瓶。那点珍贵的存货剩得不多了，他还有别的用场等着它。

有两三处地方可能找得到这人。摄政街后身一家小酒馆是其中之一。他驱车前往，在离那儿几步远的地方叫车停下。他大步走进那间雅各布斯之流常出没的酒吧，里面却空无一人。他要找的人不在。

他又跑了几处可能的地方，照样一无所获。威利该在家了。他已搬到泰晤士河南岸的寓所去了。
===Original===
It was coming from a little public-house off the New Kent Road that Black found his man. Willie had been spending the evening brooding over his grievance, and was on his way home to prepare for his big adventure when Black clapped him on the shoulder.

“Hullo, Willie,” he said.

The man turned round with a start.

“Keep your hands off me,” he said hastily, stumbling against the wall.

“Now, don’t be silly,” said Black. “Let’s talk this matter out reasonably. You’re a reasonable man, aren’t you? I’ve got a cab waiting round the corner.”
===Chinese===
说来也巧，布莱克正是从新肯特路后身一家小酒馆出来时找到了他要的人。威利这一晚都在闷头细数自己的委屈，正要回家去筹备他那番大冒险，布莱克一只手拍上了他的肩。

「喂，威利。」他说。

那人吓得一激灵，转过身来。

「把你的爪子拿开。」他慌忙说，踉跄着退到墙边。

「别犯傻。」布莱克说，「咱们把这事讲道理谈开。你是个讲道理的人，对吧？我在拐角备了辆车。」
===Original===
“You don’t get me into no cabs,” said Jakobs. “I’ve had enough of you, Black. You’ve turned round on me. You cast me out like a dog. Is that the way to treat a pal?”

“You’ve made a mistake, my friend,” said Black smoothly. “We’re all liable to make mistakes. I’ve made many, and I dare say you’ve made a few. Now, let’s talk business.”

Willie said nothing. He was still suspicious. Once he thought he saw the other’s hand steal to his breast-pocket. He guessed the motive of the action. This, then, was where the bottle was.

Black was an adept in the art of cajolery. He knew the weak places of all the men who had been associated with him. Very slowly he led the other aimlessly, so it seemed, from one street to another until they reached a little cul-de-sac. Stables occupied one side of the tiny street and artisan houses the other. One streetlamp halfway down showed a dim light.

Willie hesitated. “There’s no thoroughfare,” he said.

“Oh yes, there is,” said Black confidently. “I know this neighbourhood rather well. Now, there’s one thing I want to ask you, Willie. I’m sure you are feeling more friendly towards me now, aren’t you?”
===Chinese===
「休想让我上什么车。」雅各布斯说，「我受够你了，布莱克。你翻脸不认人。你像撵狗一样把我撵出来。对哥们儿就这么个待法？」

「你误会了，我的朋友。」布莱克圆滑地说，「谁都免不了误会。我就误会过许多回，你多半也有过几回。来，谈谈正事。」

威利不吭声。他还是满腹狐疑。有一回他仿佛看见对方的手悄悄摸向胸袋。他猜到了这个动作的用意。原来，瓶子就在那儿。

哄人是布莱克的看家本事。凡同他共过事的人，弱点在哪儿他一清二楚。他慢慢引着对方，看似漫无目的地从一条街转到另一条街，直到走进一条小小的死巷。巷子一边是马厩，一边是工匠住屋。半当腰一盏路灯亮着昏光。

威利迟疑了。「这儿是条死路。」他说。

「哦不，有出路。」布莱克满有把握地说，「这一带我熟得很。对了，有件事要问你，威利。我敢说，你这会儿对我又亲热起来了，是不是？」
===Original===
His hand rested almost affectionately on the other’s shoulder.

“You didn’t play the game,” persisted the other.

“Let bygones be bygones,” said Black. “What I want to know is, Willie, why did you take the bottle?”

He asked the question in a matter-of-fact tone. He did not raise his voice or give the query unusual emphasis.

The other man was taken off his guard.

“Well, I felt sore,” he said.
===Chinese===
他的手近乎慈爱地搭在对方肩上。

「你不守规矩。」对方还不依不饶。

「过去的事就让它过去。」布莱克说，「我只想知道，威利，你为什么拿走那只瓶子？」

他问得平平常常，既没抬高嗓门，也没格外加重语气。

对方猝不及防。

「唉，我心里憋屈呗。」他说。
===Original===
“And I suppose,” said Black, with gentle reproach, “you’re waiting to hand that bottle to our friend Fellowe?”

“I haven’t handed it to anybody yet,” said Willie, “but to tell you the truth﻿—”

He said no more. The big man’s hand suddenly closed round his throat with a grip like steel. Willie struggled, but he was like a child in the grasp of the other.

“You dog,” breathed Black.

He shook the helpless man violently. Then with his disengaged hand he whipped the telltale phial from the other’s pocket and pushed him against the wall.

“And I’ll teach you that that’s nothing to what you’ll get if you ever come across me again.”
===Chinese===
「那么我猜，」布莱克带着温和的责备说，「你正等着把那瓶子交给咱们的朋友费洛？」

「我还没交给任何人。」威利说，「不过老实告诉你——」

他没能再说下去。大个子的手骤然扼住他的咽喉，像铁钳一般。威利挣扎，可在对方掌中不过像个孩子。

「你这狗。」布莱克低声道。

他狠狠摇晃这个无力反抗的人，腾出的那只手从他口袋里迅猛摸出那只露底的药瓶，把他往墙上一推。

「我还要让你明白，这不过是个开头——你再敢撞到我手里，有你好受的。」
===Original===
Jakobs dropped, white and ghastly, against the wall.

“You’ve got the bottle, Black,” he said, “but I know everything that you’ve done with it.”

“You do, do you?”

“Yes, everything,” said the other desperately. “You’re not going to cast me off, do you hear? You’ve got to pension me, same as you’ve done other people. I know enough to send you for a lagging without﻿—”
===Chinese===
雅各布斯瘫靠在墙上，面无人色。

「瓶子你拿回去了，布莱克。」他说，「可用它干过的事，我件件都知道。」

「你知道，是吗？」

「对，件件都知道。」对方拼死说道，「你不能把我一脚踢开，听见没有？你得给我养老钱，像你给别人那样。我知道的就够送你去服苦役，不用——」
===Original===
“I thought you did,” said Black.

Something glittered in the light of the lamp, and without a cry Jakobs went down in a huddled heap to the ground.

Black looked round. He wiped the blade of the stiletto carefully on the coat of the stricken man, carefully replaced the weapon in its leather case, and examined his own hands with considerable care for any signs of blood. But these Italian weapons make small wounds.

He turned and, pulling on his gloves, made his way back to where the cab was still waiting.
===Chinese===
「我就知道你知道。」布莱克说。

灯光里有什么东西一闪，雅各布斯一声没吭，蜷作一团倒了下去。

布莱克四下望了望。他把短剑刃口在那倒地之人的外套上细细擦净，小心地把凶器放回皮鞘，又把自己的双手仔仔细细检查了一遍，看有没有血迹。不过这种意大利兵刃，创口本就细小。

他转过身，戴上手套，走回那辆仍在等候的马车。
